  • “Your life or his.” “Let’s cut the cancer off now.” A countdown to five.
  • Orion listens: “The more Orion listened in, the more it sounded like Kage wanted to help instead of hurt.”
  • At “three”: “That’s enough counting! I made my choice. Let me out of here, and go after Kage. If you aren’t lying and you will give Kage a chance since I answered so quickly, then this choice is the most logical.” He’s calculating: answer early, earn mercy for Kage. It’s logical, and it’s still choosing Kage to suffer instead of himself. Orion carries that for the rest of the book.
  • The 45-second game: two tentacles; “Any limb my tentacles touch will be broken when the time is over… if a limb is touched twice, I will break that same limb on Hermes here as well.” Kage is now protecting Orion’s limbs.
  • Thanatos stops Orion from fleeing and pats his head: “I want Kage to have more of an audience than just myself… Who knows? Kage may end up untouched completely, and you both can go home and talk about your emotional trauma together.”
  • The right arm is skimmed immediately.
  • The moon. Kage blasts straight up, “hundreds of feet,” “closer and closer to the moon… the tentacles were not a fan of being alone with moonlight.” “Abby’s death had been playing in his head over and over since it happened. Every time it replayed, more of the scene became clear to him. He knew now that the light did damage to Nerezza before the explosion.” He’s using what he learned from watching Abby die. That’s grief turned into tactics, and it’s both admirable and awful.
  • “The whole world became colder and the air grew thin.” Lipids run out. “About eight seconds to fall.” Caught by the right leg and left arm.
  • Then the left leg, and the right arm a second time. Thanatos, timing on his phone: “you grabbed Kage’s right arm after time was up. That last touch is null and void.” Thanatos saves Orion’s arm with a rules technicality. It’s also, strangely, Thanatos protecting Kage’s wish.
  • “This brat actually protected his little friend. It’s your lucky day, Hermes.” Kage: “I did it? Heh…nice. Hermes is gonna be okay…”
  • Then four tentacles break all four limbs anyway. (Under the rules, all four were touched once, so she’s technically within her word.) The description is graphic: “His right arm was split straight down the middle. Bones showed through.” “Thanatos looked on in satisfaction. He couldn’t help but respect Nerezza for making good on her word.”
  • Thanatos releases Orion: “You may run off now. There is nothing more for you to see.” “His only thought was to flee… he took it despite his heart yelling at him to turn and help Kage.” Orion runs. It’s the second thing he’ll never forgive himself for.
  • “A head on a torso with pieces of raw flesh and bloody bone attached to it.”
  • Nerezza: “He is a fun plaything. I see now why you keep him around.” She leaves “without a trace.”
  • The rebuild is the best-written passage of body horror in the book:
    • “He could feel everything that was wrong with Kage’s body, the way a blacksmith feels the flaws in warped metal.”
    • Black biomass from his forearm into Kage’s ribs: “It didn’t eat away at Kage the way it ate everything else Thanatos touched.”
    • “That’s when the hunger hit.… every cell in his body was screaming to fill the hole.”
    • His shoulder “visibly shrank” giving the second piece.
    • “Bits of the things he’d absorbed over the years started scratching at the back of his mind, their instincts trying to claw their way out.” There are other creatures’ instincts inside that biomass. Now some of it is inside Kage.
    • “Kage’s body rejected it twice.”
    • His thighs: “two pieces at once.” Then: “His cells stopped blending in with Kage’s and started trying to take them over. ‘No, don’t you dare.’”
    • “He looked smaller now… His body wasn’t asking for biomass anymore. It was ordering it.”
  • The rules for Kage: “this life isn’t meant for the weak. I’m strengthening you. You’ll be able to regenerate like never before, but it’s not all sunshine and rainbows, kid. This mass inside you will amplify your traits, especially the negative ones. The things you try to suppress and hide will be difficult to hold back now… You’ll work for me from now on.”
  • “There were barely even scars visible.” Kage sits up “hyperventilating.”

What it’s doing. It’s the book’s second hinge. Orion learns Kage’s secret and makes his own secret choice. Kage’s body becomes something else.

What works. The dome choice is one of the best moral dilemmas in the book, because Orion’s logic is sound and still feels like betrayal. The moonlight tactic is beautiful. The rebuild is written with real physical specificity, and it complicates Thanatos: whatever his motives, he hollowed himself out for this boy.

What I’d push on. The limb-breaking is long and very graphic. That’s a choice, and it serves the horror, but the paragraph that describes Kage’s “wailing” and “the dissection” could lose a sentence or two and be just as effective. Also, consider giving Orion one more beat inside the dome where he remembers Kage slinging Abby’s scorched backpack over his arm a few minutes earlier. It would make his choice at “three” even heavier.

Score 9.5/10

2.8 About Kage…

2,057 words. Orion’s point of view. Wednesday.

What happens. Two days later, Orion is carrying everything alone. People keep asking where Kage is, and he keeps saying “I don’t know.” He blames himself twice over: for never getting to know Abby, and for the part of him that wanted Kage gone, which became a real choice in the dome. He decides to tell one person, the one who didn’t even notice Kage was missing: Ailani. In her dorm he sees an old photo of her laughing at a glue-covered Kage. He tells her Abby and Kage are both dead. She breaks down; he holds her. He says he won’t tell Huoyan until after the upcoming Elite Tournament, and he doesn’t tell her about Shifting Horror. He tells her Kage loved her, and they swear to make him proud.

Details I caught.

  • “Tuesday was the day of the extremes.” A great, compact phrase.
  • The askers: “Rhatella asked casually. Zulfiya asked in wonder. Lucifer asked in some deranged form of curiosity, and then, almost under his breath, whether Abby was with them. His tail stayed stiff the whole time.” Lucifer cares about Abby. He sent her to Kage. His tail gives him away.
  • “Even Huoyan asked, and he was the one who sent them out that night.”
  • Orion’s guilt, part one: “his lack of care for Abby. He didn’t take the time to get to know her. He was given the opportunity, and he threw it away out of contempt for another.” She wanted them all to hang out. He was too busy disliking Kage.
  • Part two: “A part of him prayed for this. A part of him desperately wanted Kage out of his life.… The day in the nurse’s office kept replaying in his head, when he said, ‘At this rate, I’m gonna be the one to kill him.’… He never meant that sentence literally, and yet he made that choice. He told Nerezza to attack Kage.” The joke from 2.2 becomes his confession.
  • “Every person who had asked him, ‘Where’s Kage?’ was met with a simple, ‘I don’t know.’”
  • “The one singular person he knew he was going to reveal the truth to, was the one person who didn’t even notice Kage’s absence.” Ailani told Kage to lose her number. She hasn’t noticed he’s gone.
  • “He got up from the desk and walked, not ran, to the dorms.” The speedster who loves walking slowly walks to deliver bad news. That one comma carries a lot.
  • Ailani: “You’re not the type to come over.” She starts to close the door; “Orion stopped it by putting his foot in the way.” Abby put her foot in Lucifer’s door two days earlier. Now Orion does it.
  • “She knew Orion wasn’t the type to ask, and that he’d respect and accept any boundary she gave.” Ailani knows him well. They’ve been talking.
  • Her room: “beautifully purple and fluorescent,” string lights, photos with friends. And on her dresser, in the center: Kage with “glue dripping down his face,” “a smear of motion from Kage’s hand going up to try to block the camera’s lens,” Ailani laughing beside him. “I never took it down, and I kinda forgot it was still there.” She didn’t forget. It’s in the center of her dresser.
  • Orion puts it back “exactly where he found it.” “It…looked nice where it was.” Orion, who likes Ailani, preserves the photo of her with Kage. That’s the kindest thing he does in the book.
  • “Ailani was the type of person that hated beating around the bush.”
  • Orion’s speed makes the wait unbearable: “his enhancement made this moment feel all the longer. Each millisecond began feeling like an eternity.” A superpower used to show an emotion. That’s the best kind of power writing.
  • “He wasn’t really the hugging type.”
  • “Abby burst completely into light, and Kage…Kage was attacked beyond recognition.” Both true. Neither is the whole truth.
  • Ailani: “The last thing I ever said to him was to lose my number…”
  • Orion: “I wasn’t going to tell anybody until after the upcoming Elite Tournament.” “What he said was true, but it wasn’t the whole reason.” The book is honest about Orion’s dishonesty.
  • Ailani: “The Elite Tournament? That’s where your priorities are right now?!” She’s right.
  • “WELL, I DON’T KNOW WHAT TO DO!” Then softer: “I don’t know what to do…” The calm boy cracks.
  • “Kage would’ve wanted us to say fuck him, go to that tournament and kick some fucking ass!” in “a very ‘Kage’ voice and cadence.” Ailani laughs. Orion is using Kage’s own coping strategy (make them laugh) on Kage’s grieving ex.
  • “He loved you. I could see his eyes get all sparkly when he was thinking about you.” Orion tells the girl he likes that his rival loved her. That’s selfless.
  • Ailani: “How did you guys end up separated?” Orion “wrestled with the moral question of whether to tell her Kage was working with Shifting Horror.” He chooses: “I don’t…want to relive that right now…”
  • “Oh, and keep smiling! Abby wouldn’t have wanted us to have frowns the whole time.”
  • Orion holds back his tears: “Kage wouldn’t have wanted to see him sobbing. Especially not in front of Ailani.”

What it’s doing. It gives Orion his own wound and his own secret, puts him in Ailani’s room, and quietly starts the love triangle.

What works. The photo. The walk. The foot in the door. Orion’s speed stretching the silence. It’s a quiet chapter full of precise gestures, and it’s one of the best-written in the book.

What I’d push on. Orion’s guilt is explained in one long paragraph at the top. It’s good, but it’s a lot of telling at once. You could break it up, letting some of it surface later in the scene, when he sees the photo or when Ailani asks about the night.

Score 9/10

2.9 You’re Supposed To Know

1,288 words. Anubis’s office, Thursday.

What happens. Huoyan storms into Anubis’s office demanding to know where Kage and Abby are. Anubis corrects his grammar, admits his main city drone broke the night of the patrol, and says he was at a sponsor dinner. He suggests the kids took mental health days. Huoyan explodes: Anubis only cares about students as marketing. Anubis asks him to leave. Huoyan slams the door. On the desk are two handprints, scorched into the wood.

Details I caught.

  • “Hellfeather! To what do I owe the pleasure?”
  • “Oh, cut the shit, Khaled.” Huoyan is the only person who uses Anubis’s first name.
  • “Please, Mr. Niao, use our last names. It’s a formal respect.” “I give that courtesy to my students. I am not calling you Mr. Nasser or Anubis. You’ll always be just Khaled.”
  • Anubis says “Sigh…fine” out loud. Huoyan: “What person actually says the word ‘sigh’?” A great character quirk.
  • “We are simply coworkers. Nothing more.” Huoyan: “Oh, I know. You’ve made that very clear. I am certainly not trying to rehash anything right now.” They had something. In 2.13 Huoyan says, “One day, I’m your sidekick.” Anubis calls him “Hon” throughout. The book is quietly telling you these two were partners, and probably more than partners.
  • “Both have been missing since Tuesday… Neither have shown up to class since then, and it’s been three days now.” Anubis: “It’s ‘has,’ Huoyan.… You have to keep working on your English.” Huoyan is a non-native English speaker. The author deliberately planted a grammar error in Huoyan’s mouth so Anubis could correct it. It’s the pettiest, most revealing line Anubis has.
  • “I do have footage of Neosame leaving the building Monday night and heading to the city. After that, well. I’m not sure.”
  • Huoyan’s palms on the desk: “The few papers on Anubis’s desk curled and browned at the edges.”
  • “You’re supposed to be the watcher!” That’s what Anubis is. His power may be watching (see Theories).
  • “My main city drone broke and is only being repaired now. You know how expensive they are… Do you know how difficult it is for me to get everything approved by the United Nations every single time?” The UN controls the program’s budget. That’s the Prologue’s mystery showing up in a budget complaint. And the “strange camera” Nerezza destroyed in 2.6 was this drone.
  • “I’m beginning to fear that they might have something to do with the murder that happened on Monday.” He’s right.
  • “Vance has no clue where Ozul is either.” Orion lied to Huoyan too.
  • “I was at a sponsor dinner for the tournament.” While two of his scouted students were dying.
  • “Maybe both of them just needed mental health days.”
  • “You’re supposed to know where everyone is all the time, Mr. Know-it-all!” The title.
  • Huoyan’s indictment: “Marketing! That’s what we need these kids for! Not saving lives, but brand recognition!… Let’s get all these kids straight into your agency so you can line your pockets more from their heroism! My students are good children. They may be dysfunctional, but all of them are truly heroic.” And he quotes Anubis: “‘I don’t decorate my office because that would be a waste of money.’ ‘Students should be given licenses earlier.’ ‘Let’s put the Elite Tournament on T.V. and sell tickets to watch it in the arena!’” That explains the bare office in 1.14 and sets up the televised tournament in Section 3.
  • “And if they don’t come back? Then what, Number One? You gonna take responsibility for that? No, that would damage your reputation too much, wouldn’t it? You’ll end up asking me to take the fall even though I’m the one who actually cares about these children.”
  • “You never have time for anything unless it benefits you.”
  • “Anubis looked down at his desk. Huoyan’s two handprints had been scorched into the wood.” The best final image of any chapter in Section 2. Huoyan leaves a permanent mark, and he’s the only one in the book who does.

What it’s doing. It’s the adult version of the book’s argument. Huoyan is the heart; Anubis is the brand.

What works. It’s a great argument because both men have history and both are partly right. Anubis’s grammar correction is a perfect petty detail. The handprints are a perfect ending.

What I’d push on. Anubis is a little too obviously wrong here (“mental health days”). He’s more interesting when he’s half-right, as he is in Section 3 (“If he wanted to quit, he would have”). One line where he admits, privately, that he’s worried would make him more dangerous, not less.

Score 8/10

2.10 Another Task

2,805 words. The villains’ warehouse, Thursday.

What happens. Kage has spent days curled in the corner of a bathroom, blowing Abby’s silent whistle over and over and clutching her backpack. When Frostbite reaches for the whistle, Kage flips him on instinct, then apologizes. Frostbite pats his head and brings him clothes, grooming supplies, and makeup. Thanatos and Frostbite argue: Frostbite says Thanatos “invaded the boy’s body without consent.” Kage emerges with a self-cut, cartoonish new hairstyle and eyeliner. Frostbite gives him a hood and yellow contacts. Thanatos leaves for a mission in Russia. Frostbite apologizes for their first meeting, calls Thanatos weak, and asks why Kage is loyal to him. Kage asks, “Why am I not dead..?” and says he has to be number one “for Abby.” Frostbite gives him a test: infiltrate the school and bring back the strongest student in the class, Lucifer Tulivalo. Kage smiles, a little too widely.

Details I caught.

  • “The boy has been in there for days. He can’t live there; especially not for free. Pretty soon I’m gonna have to charge him rent or something.” Frostbite’s dry humor.
  • The image: Kage “crouched so low that he was nearly on the floor, just facing a wall.” “In his hands, though, was a lone gold whistle. It was totally silent, but Kage kept blowing it. Apparently, he had been blowing this whistle since he’d been thrown into this bathroom.” He refused to use it when she was alive. Now he can’t stop. The whistle is the book’s best symbol, and this is its saddest use.
  • And a thought: Abby said “only I can hear it.” If she’s anywhere, she’s been hearing it for days.
  • “It’s gonna rust at this rate if you keep blowing it.”
  • The flip: “On instinct, he grabbed hold of Frostbite’s arm and flipped him over his shoulder.” Kage has never shown martial arts like this. That’s the biomass, already sharpening him.
  • “Oh my…I-I’m sorry! I didn’t…that wasn’t…”
  • Frostbite “patted Kage’s head. ‘It’s okay, kid. Just don’t do it again. I’ll kill you if you do.’” Comfort and threat in one breath. That’s Frostbite.
  • Thanatos: “Frostbite being warm? I mean…he isn’t doing it too well, but he’s trying!”
  • “Sorry, I’m not really good with kids. And I kind of forgot your gender.” A strange, funny line. “Not really good with kids” also suggests he’s tried before.
  • Kage bows to Frostbite “with a reverential edge… that Thanatos noticed and didn’t take too kindly to.” Kage has only bowed to Huoyan before, and only to apologize.
  • Frostbite: “Your perceptive self can’t read between the lines?” “I may have just seen a smidgen of what you see in him.”
  • Thanatos: “That is my project. I do not need you stealing that from me.” In 3.4, Gene calls Kage “Anubis’s project.” Both sides call him a project.
  • “I saved his life. That is the most anybody can ever give to anyone.” Frostbite: “Oh, that’s right. Did Kage ask you to do that for him?”
  • “Manipulation tactics were about the highest level of emotional understanding Thanatos had. ‘Figure out what makes them tick, and try to give them what they desire.’ That was his usual motto.”
  • The consent argument: “You just invaded the boy’s body without consent and forced yourself onto him. He’s in that bathroom, confused. He doesn’t know how he’s even alive… He’s busy grieving that girl… I know for a fact you didn’t ask him how he was mentally. Only physical questions came from you.” This ties directly to Zulfiya’s rule in 1.11: “If he doesn’t wish to be healed, he can’t be forced.” The villain makes the healer’s argument.
  • The new look: “messy and layered, with long, wispy pieces framing his face. His bangs were swept up and to the side, and the top stood up so high it almost looked like it was floating. It looked almost cartoonish.” And eyeliner. Thanatos: “What are you doing, making yourself look like you came out of a children’s show?… I’m training a degenerate.” Frostbite: “What, Thany? I think it looks good.”
  • Kage: “The hair I had before was…well, for lack of a better term, lazy… That lazy me is gone now, though, so the hair needs to go with it.… Doesn’t it? Just like an action figure!” The action-figure dream from 1.9. And the new hair looks permanently like it’s standing up, which is what his hair does during telekinesis.
  • The black hood: “It wasn’t exactly what he imagined when it came to a new accessory. But it was something, and Rhatella’s gear was something only the old Kage was after.”
  • “Your blue eyes are too much of a tell… Put these on too.” A pair of yellow contacts. New in Draft 3 (they were gray). Abby had yellow eyes.
  • Thanatos: “The boss is sending me on a mission in Russia. I won’t be in the U.S. for a couple of months.” Nerezza turns out to live in Russia (4.0).
  • Kage: “You’re…not going to save me anymore?” Thanatos: “No, not that! What I said before that!” Kage’s first fear is abandonment. Thanatos means “don’t obey Frost.”
  • The whistle goes on a string around his neck: “I put the whistle there, and that’s where it’s staying. Same with this backpack.”
  • Frostbite’s bare hand: “pale skin… The tips of his fingers were dark and blistered, like the cold was eating him from the inside out. Kage’s own hand curled around nothing again.” The second time. “He still couldn’t place where he’d seen Frostbite before, so he eventually assumed it was on the news.”
  • “Kid, I wanted to say sorry.” Kage’s face reddens. “Whether I’m your boss or your partner, I shouldn’t be threatening your life.” “That was the other half of why he hadn’t gotten angry. At least, it was the half he was willing to say out loud.” There’s a third half, so to speak, that he won’t say.
  • Frostbite on Thanatos: “Thanatos is weak!… All he does is strategize, but strategy can only get you so far… Have you ever heard the phrase ‘man plans, and God laughs’?… strength, that’s absolute.” He’s pitching himself as a better mentor.
  • “Why am I not dead..?” “The words interjected into the conversation like they didn’t belong.” Frostbite: “I don’t know, kid. I couldn’t tell you.”
  • Kage: “He told me that he could make me good enough. He said that he could make me number one… That’s all I want to be now…I have to…I have to, for Abby…” The worst irony in the section: he’s following the villain to honor the girl the villains killed.
  • “Oh, God. You’re another one of those.” “Potential” again. “Yeah, I know. You’ll kill me.” “That’s not what I was going to say! I told you before that I’m going to stop doing that.”
  • The task: “Your task is to beat the strongest of your class and prove it by bringing them here… I need you to bring me Lucifer Tulivalo. Show me you’re above your nobody classmates.”
  • “Then, an almost creepy smile appeared on his face.” “Oh, I’ll do it. I’ll happily kick Luci’s ass for you.” The biomass amplifies “the things you try to suppress.” Kage’s dislike of Lucifer is now appetite.

What it’s doing. It shows Kage’s grief, introduces the new Kage (look, contacts, tools), deepens Frostbite, and sends Kage after Lucifer.

What works. The bathroom image is unforgettable. The consent argument is the book’s ethics stated by the least likely person. The makeover is both funny and sad: he’s trying to look like an action figure, a hero for kids, while dressed in a villain’s hood.

What I’d push on. Frostbite’s speech about strength vs. strategy is long, and Kage isn’t listening to it (the text says so). You could cut it by half and keep the “man plans, and God laughs” line. Also, the shift from Frostbite the would-be killer (1.13) to Frostbite the awkward father figure is big. The “other half” line promises an explanation. Deliver it soon, or plant one more hint in the meantime.

Score 8.5/10

2.11 Appreciate The Invite, Luci

1,422 words. The school at night, Thursday.

What happens. Kage sneaks into the school using another key cast. Hiding on a hallway ceiling, he overhears Orion and Ailani walking together, laughing, then fist-bumping “for Kage” and “for Abby.” Alone, Orion punches a wall: “Why’d you have to go and die on me?! You piece of shit!” Kage almost leaves a strip of tape on Orion’s door as a sign he’s alive, then decides not to. In Lucifer’s room (only one bedroom), he finds trophies, medals, and a poster for the Elite Tournament under a black claymore engraved “Minkowski.” He decides to enter the tournament. Lucifer walks in, and Kage knocks him out with the flat of the claymore. He tapes Lucifer up, pats his head, and carries him off.

Details I caught.

  • “This isn’t heroic in the slightest. I want to throw this thing off right now.” He hates the villain outfit.
  • “He was down the hall from Ailani’s dorm.” Lucifer and Ailani are neighbors.
  • Kage sticks himself to the ceiling and holds his breath. It’s the Arachnid Guy move from 1.6, now used for sneaking.
  • Ailani: “Nice going today, Orion! You were taking out the robonoids crazy fast!… I think you’re gonna get to Lucifer’s level soon.”
  • Orion: “You mean have Huoyan look at me the way he looks at Lucifer? Not exactly sure that I want that. It might make me as egotistical as him.”
  • “Seeing the two of them together made Kage’s stomach curl in a strange way. He was happy to see Orion okay now. If he were in front of Orion, he’d probably cry.”
  • “Orion and Ailani never hang out together. They’re smiling…a lot. Do they even care that I’m not around?” Jealousy, abandonment, and the start of the triangle, from Kage’s side.
  • “For Kage! Right?” Orion stares at the fist bump, “thinking hard about something,” then: “Yeah, for Kage. Don’t forget about Abby. For her too.” Orion remembers Abby when Ailani doesn’t.
  • Ailani: “Kage cares way more about this than Abby…Abby just wants to see us happy.”
  • Alone: “Am I doing this right?… I should tell her the truth about Kage. She should know, but if I tell the truth then I have to tell her the whole truth. Dammit, Kage!” He punches a wall. “Why’d you have to go and die on me?! You piece of shit!”
  • Kage, on the ceiling, watches his own mourner. It’s one of the best scenes in the book, because Kage gets to see how much Orion cares, and can’t say anything.
  • “Should I leave a subliminal message? Like…stick a piece of tape on his dorm door.” He decides not to: “I don’t want him thinking I have some crazy vendetta against him. I’ll…I’ll see you soon, Hermes.”
  • “He slapped his own face a couple of times to try and get back in the zone. Sure enough, a smile appeared on his face again.” He resets his own mood like a machine. That’s the “very good at being fake” Thanatos described.
  • “Unlocked! Awesome.” Lucifer’s door is unlocked. Anubis, who “never” leaves his unlocked, gets broken into anyway.
  • “To his surprise, there was only one bedroom door. Kage had always assumed Elias and Lucifer shared a dorm.” Lucifer has a bigger, solo dorm (“The favoritism is loud”). But in 2.4 and 2.5, Elias was inside, “heading to lay back down,” and Lucifer “flopped onto the bed next to Elias.” So Elias sleeps over, in the one bedroom. He’s there to watch Lucifer sleep. (Section 4 confirms it.)
  • The room: “decorated with accomplishments. Medals were pinned all over. A trophy display case.” “God, his ego just knows no bounds.”
  • The poster, “notably newer,” under “a large black claymore.” “On the back, in smaller print, it said the top students would go into the Elite Program for special training.” Kage: “of course the top students will get even more special treatment than they already do.”
  • “Damn, I’m gonna have to make a surprise return this weekend!”
  • The engraving: “Minkowski.” “Minkowski like…Elias? Guess Lucifer is borrowing it from that weirdo. That guy still kinda gives me the creeps after what he told me and Orion last time. It was like he knew something bad was going to happen.”
  • “Maybe getting them to do some in-fighting could give me an advantage this weekend.” He decides to take the sword, then leaves it.
  • Lucifer: “WHAT IN THE HELL ARE YOU DOING HER-” Kage swings the claymore’s flat “like a baseball bat.” Lucifer was awake when Kage entered. He “never quite sleeps,” as Abaddon explains next chapter.
  • “That’s a Tulivalo knocked out and secured.” Kage “patted Lucifer’s head a couple of times.” The boy who’s been patted by everyone now pats someone else, as a victor.
  • “Appreciate the invite, Luci. Maybe I’ll get to fight you this weekend, but for now, you’re coming with me to meet a friend of mine.” “A friend of mine” is what Thanatos said to Kage in 1.12. Kage is now saying the villain’s lines.
  • “This’ll show him I deserve much more than I’m given at this good-for-nothing school.”

What it’s doing. It lets Kage see his own grief from outside, plants the claymore and the tournament, and sets up the reveal of Abaddon.

What works. The ceiling scene is extraordinary. Kage listening to Orion swear at him for dying is funny, heartbreaking, and full of dramatic irony at once. The claymore details are meticulous and pay off over two more sections.

What I’d push on. Kage takes the poster and talks about sowing “in-fighting” between Lucifer and Elias. That’s a petty, villainous thought, and it’s played lightly. It’s worth knowing that this is the first time Kage plans to hurt classmates for advantage. It could be a quiet alarm bell if he caught himself thinking it.

Score 8/10

2.12 What’s Hiding Underneath

3,004 words. Outside the villains’ warehouse, Thursday night.

What happens. At the warehouse, Frostbite isn’t there. Lucifer starts foaming and seizing, then transforms into a ten-foot black demon with burning horns: Abaddon, who speaks of himself in the third person and explains that Lucifer “never quite sleeps.” Knocking Lucifer out let him free. Abaddon pounds Kage into a wall, and Kage discovers he coughs black blood and regenerates. Abaddon snaps his leg; it heals in real time. Abaddon bites his forearm down to the bone; Kage, clutching Abby’s whistle, says “you can’t break…what’s already broken!” and drives his own exposed bone into the back of Abaddon’s throat, and enjoys it. Frostbite arrives late and knocks Abaddon out with ice. Kage asks for a reward: let him go back for the Elite Tournament. Frostbite reluctantly agrees: “I’ll be watching, so don’t embarrass yourself.”

Details I caught.

  • “Maybe I should’ve listened to Thanatos. Clearly he’s unprofessional.”
  • Lucifer’s mouth “was frothing with foam.” Seizing. Skin “darker and darker.” The tape snaps. His eyes turn “blood red.” Limbs and horns lengthen. The tail becomes “thick and scaly as opposed to its normal string-like size.” Skin goes “from his normal pale to a hollow black.” “The tips of both horns caught fire.”
  • “OH! FREEDOM IS SWEETER THAN ABADDON COULD HAVE EVER IMAGINED! STUCK IN A WEAKLING FOR AGES WITHOUT A BODY!” The third-person voice and caps are perfect for a prisoner who’s been talking to himself for years.
  • “You! You’re the one who helped get Abaddon free!” Kage freed a demon. He has no idea.
  • “The boy…he never quite sleeps. It’s a skill that has made Abaddon frustrated beyond belief! That boy is similar to a dog. He rests but never fully.” That explains Abby’s diagnosis (2.1), Lucifer’s constant yawning, why Elias sleeps over, and why Lucifer woke when Kage snuck in.
  • “Abaddon can see everything the boy does! Abaddon gets to choose that luxury for him, though. Abaddon is not letting him see anything anymore!” So Lucifer won’t remember this night (until 3.16).
  • Kage: “If I was going to die, it would’ve happened a couple of days ago.” He’s starting to feel unkillable.
  • “At the end of the day, you’re just Luci.”
  • Abaddon flies at him with flaming talons. Kage dodges: “I’ve been training for months to beat someone that moves way faster than that.” Orion’s rivalry pays off against a demon.
  • Concrete punch to the stomach: “as if Kage had only poked him.”
  • “Abaddon loves hearing bones breaking! Every crack on your feeble being is music to Abaddon’s ears! Crack. Crack! CRACK!”
  • “Abaddon loves letting people die on their own.”
  • “The bloody coughs went from red to black.” “The hell is that…yeah, that definitely can’t be good.” That’s how Kage learns about the biomass: through his own blood.
  • “Maybe it’s just my heroic spirit keeping me up,” laughing at his own joke.
  • Abaddon’s body heat vaporizes the glue. “It was glue vapor, and Kage knew that smell very well.”
  • The telekinesis rule, explained: “Against Orion, Kage had made pillars like that float. He didn’t even think to try now. Back then, he’d been sure he was better than whoever stood in front of him. Somewhere around the mirror room, he’d stopped being sure, and it didn’t help that Abaddon was so imposing.” Telekinesis runs on certainty. It explains the failed butterfly in 2.3. It’s the single best piece of retroactive worldbuilding in the book.
  • “BUT YOU CAN’T STAND IF YOU DON’T HAVE ANY LEGS!” SNAP. Then: “the leg appeared alive for a moment. Abaddon watched the injured leg repair itself in real time.”
  • Kage remembers “something about healing faster” from Thanatos’s words while he was dying.
  • “Are you trembling? You’ve never been scared before, have you?… It takes a lot more than that to take a real hero like me down. No one’s challenged you like this, huh? Fear looks really bad on you.”
  • “You’re just a cockroach!” Kage: “You know, all that bug talk is making you sound a lot like Luci! No wonder you two are a team!” Lucifer’s insect insults (ant, termite, bug, worm, mosquito) might be Abaddon’s vocabulary leaking out, or vice versa.
  • “ABADDON AND THE BOY ARE NOT A TEAM! I HATE HIM! HATE HIM, HATE HIM, HATE HIM!”
  • Abaddon “fired off the wall with so much force that he injured both of his legs.” Even the demon pays costs.
  • The bite: “Both bones in his forearm were completely exposed. The demon licked the bones.” “Your blood is tasty, red one!”
  • “Abby’s whistle had slipped out of Kage’s collar. It sat on the asphalt next to his face, speckled with his blood.”
  • “You can’t break…what’s already broken!” One of the book’s best lines. And he “closed his hand around the whistle.” His hand holds on to something, finally.
  • The bone stab: “The bone stabbed directly into the back of Abaddon’s throat.” The most inventive body horror in the book. Lucifer’s throat is sore for two days afterward (2.13, 2.14).
  • “For a moment, Kage enjoyed hearing him in anguish. He wasn’t sure why, but he knew it was…different.” The biomass at work, exactly as Thanatos warned.
  • “Black cells moved around his muscles. They pulled the bones of his arm back into place before fusing them back to normal.”
  • “Luci, you can’t keep secrets from me like that. I could’ve died!” Pot, kettle.
  • Frostbite’s icicles and a giant ice block knock Abaddon out. Kage: “Oh, come on! I had him all on my own!”
  • “YOU DIDN’T THINK TO TEXT ME YOU WERE ON YOUR WAY?!” “I DID TEXT YOU! YOU NEVER LOOKED AT MY MESSAGES!” Thanatos never read Kage’s texts either. The villains keep ignoring him.
  • “You asked me to do something and I got it done quickly and efficiently. Don’t question my methods.”
  • Frostbite and Lucifer: “Frostbite crouched beside him longer than required. He reached towards Lucifer’s face with his bare hand, then pulled it away. Even asleep, Lucifer was still hot enough to burn.” This is the most suspicious moment Frostbite has. Why linger? Why reach for a sleeping boy’s face? See Theories.
  • “So, I suppose you passed, if that’s what you’re waiting to hear.”
  • The reward: the tournament poster. “It’s also being broadcast all over… it’ll show my friends I’m alive and okay! You forget that Anubis was watching me!”
  • Frostbite “now getting tired of how often he needed to sigh.” (Four sighs in one scene.)
  • “I’ll be watching, so don’t embarrass yourself.” In 4.0, he’s watching from a bar, with a perfect ice sphere in his scotch.
  • “WHY IS THE WAREHOUSE SO BANGED UP?!” “Sorry, sorry! I’ll fix it when I wake up tomorrow! I promise!!!”

What it’s doing. It reveals Abaddon, reveals the biomass’s power, explains the telekinesis rule, and sends Kage to the tournament.

What works. It’s a horror-action set piece with comic timing. The bone stab is unforgettable. The whistle in the blood is perfect. The telekinesis paragraph is quietly brilliant.

What I’d push on. The fight is long, and the middle section (glue, vaporize, glue, vaporize) repeats. Cut one cycle. And the moment Kage “enjoyed” Abaddon’s pain is the most important beat in the chapter, the first visible sign of what the biomass is doing. Give it one more sentence of Kage being disturbed by it, so readers know he noticed.

Score 8.5/10

2.13 Sophomores Are Tournament Bound

2,362 words. Saturday morning: Anubis’s office, Ailani’s dorm, Lucifer’s dorm, the bus.

What happens. Anubis is giddy about the tournament and has saved the sixteenth slot for Kage, whom he’s certain will return. Huoyan slips that he used to be Anubis’s sidekick. Anubis nearly barred Ailani because she might distract Kage, and mentions his brother is competing. In Ailani’s dorm, Orion punches a bag while they talk about Kage; she grabs his hand, and he blushes. In Lucifer’s dorm, Lucifer spars Elias with strange new energy and a sore throat. Elias notices the claymore is crooked by a degree or two and concludes someone was in the room. On the bus to the stadium, in the lobby, the door opens, the room goes silent, and Zulfiya says it: “King! You’re back!”

Details I caught.

  • Anubis: “We have fifteen wonderful students ready and enrolled!… The first year shows off the students when they’re too green. The Elite Tournament is that wonderful sweet spot of skill and freshness!” He talks about students like produce.
  • “I saved a slot for Kage.” “I don’t see any universe where we don’t see Sticky Situation make his grand return!” He saw Kage on camera Thursday (2.15). He knows.
  • Anubis loves the name: “It’s creative and whimsical. There are days I wish our hero names were like that. Ours are kind of boring.” Huoyan: “They’re our identity now… You’re the brand and merchandise guy.”
  • Huoyan’s slip: “One day, I’m your sidekick. The next day, I’m just a teacher and you’re my boss?… Then, one of the sleepy students realizes after a tragedy that he has to actually try. Then, and this is all of a sudden, you show extreme interest?! Weren’t you-” Anubis: “Huoyan, stop! You’re airing out dirty laundry in my office. You save that for when you’re crying at home.” Huoyan: “I sleep here.” Three words, and Huoyan’s whole life opens up. He has no home. He lives at the school. He used to be someone’s sidekick. Now he’s alone in a building full of kids he pretends to loathe.
  • Anubis didn’t save a spot for Abby “because she is not a fighter… Plus, she could also distract Kage. That was almost the reason I stopped Ailani from attending.” He treats girls as distractions in a boy’s story.
  • “Her desire to be a hero is only temporary. She has other dreams and goals.” That’s Ailani’s dancing, revealed in 4.1.
  • “Luckily, I believe my brother will do well in terms of school spirit.” “He has to carry that Nasser name well!” That’s Youseff.
  • Huoyan: “your brother hasn’t had the greatest showing recently. He is heroic in spirit, but as for how well he’s done…”
  • Orion’s warmup: “Every punch seemed so deliberate, too; it was like a boxer aiming for someone’s vitals.” That’s the exact phrase used for Kage in 1.1 (L15). Orion is now the one grief-punching a bag.
  • “I feel like if we lost, Kage would be cursing at us from up above.”
  • Ailani: “We’ve been training together, so I know all your moves.” They’ve been training together.
  • “You do know that you’re talking about Kage like he was your best friend.” Orion: “We were not and never were friends. He was just…I don’t know. When we fought it kind of spoke to me in a way I hadn’t felt before… our competition, that was…for lack of a better and more accurate term, special.”
  • “I was taught never to speak ill of the dead. I’ve never really lost someone before that was close to me. He was the closest person to me who’s ever passed on.”
  • Ailani: “I just feel like I’ve…cried enough for him already. Maybe I’m used to crying for him?” That’s a whole relationship history in one line.
  • “He deserved to know I still cared about him.… I want him to see that I…I cared. Even when I didn’t show it.”
  • “Ailani grabbed Orion’s hand… on Orion’s face, there was the faintest of blushes.”
  • Lucifer: “I’m in the zone, Cruz! I’m not sure why, but I feel like I got energy like crazy!” Abaddon got out, so Lucifer finally slept. It’s a clever cause and effect.
  • “Between punches, he rubbed his throat. It’d been sore since he awoke, but he didn’t know why.” The bone stab.
  • Elias: “You seem well rested. That’s good.”
  • “The claymore. It’s crooked.” “He was right, but only technically. The claymore was only off by about a degree or two.” “If I said it’s crooked, then it’s crooked, Luci. Go fix it.” Elias’s precision is part of his power (he tracks exact positions and forces).
  • “Push it up the tiniest bit on the left.”
  • “You really aren’t lying, are you?” Elias reads truth.
  • “Those holders were specifically forged for the claymore. A simple tremor wouldn’t cause it to move. It was definitely touched. Someone was here.”
  • “Elias simply fidgeted with his collar.” A nervous tell. He suspects more than he says.
  • “I don’t think anyone in our class could beat me.” “Aside from me, of course!”
  • Elias: “I’m fairly certain you’ll be in big trouble if you have to face Reina.” Reina: “If me and you fight, I’ll get you like BOOM!!!!” “flexing dramatically,” with “super thin and scrawny” arms. In 3.14 she becomes enormous. This is a great setup.
  • Elias: “Strength is a boring path to victory… You’ll end up my sidekick though.” Sidekicks again.
  • Reina’s fake tears: “Hey, what about me..?” Elias slaps Lucifer: “It’s a trap, you moron.” Reina: “Oh, c’mon Min! I almost had him!” This trio is genuinely charming.
  • The bus: “Zulfiya, Cheyenne, Severine, and Volkan were on their way themselves.” A “seven-minute drive.”
  • The lobby: “a creak came from the entrance. The door opened up, and the room went completely silent… It lasted for maybe a total of thirty seconds. Until finally, Anubis gave a smirk towards Huoyan.” He knew.
  • “King! You’re back!”

What it’s doing. It’s the morning-of chapter. It sets every piece on the board (Anubis’s slot, Ailani’s dream, Lucifer’s energy, the claymore, Reina’s “BOOM”) and ends on the return.

What works. Huoyan’s “I sleep here.” The Orion-as-Kage mirror. The claymore detective work. Reina’s BOOM. And the ending is a great button: we know who walks in, and the book makes us wait for it anyway.

What I’d push on. Anubis is very cartoonish in his first scene (“Ah, my favorite time of year!”). The book’s best version of Anubis is charming and dangerous at the same time. Here, he’s mostly silly. One line of his calculation (he knows Kage is alive, and he’s chosen not to tell Huoyan) would make the scene sharper.

Score 7.5/10

2.14 He’s Back

1,659 words. The stadium lobby.

What happens. Kage walks straight past Anubis and Lucifer toward Orion. Elias stops Lucifer from attacking. Everyone parts except Ailani. Orion is terrified that Kage heard his choice in the dome. Kage bursts into tears and hugs him: “Hermes, you’re okay..!” He “kinda blacked out.” Kage apologizes to Ailani. She grabs his chin, stares into his eyes, smiles, then slaps him and calls him “HIJO DE PUTA,” slaps Orion for not checking whether Kage was breathing, and tells Kage to keep her number. The three form a “band.” Orion asks quietly about Abby; Kage clutches the whistle, which is answer enough. Anubis tells Kage they need to talk on the walk.

Details I caught.

  • “Hermes…” Kage’s first word back.
  • “Zulfiya could hear that name exit Kage’s mouth and knew she shouldn’t be running up to him. No one else knew Kage called Orion that.” Zulfiya heard it in 2.2, and so did Abby. The narration states as fact what’s really Zulfiya’s belief. Small wobble.
  • “Kage didn’t even turn his head towards Number One.”
  • Lucifer: “Hey, freak! What makes you think you can walk back in with a haircut…” Kage “saw his slightly reddened throat and sneered.” Kage knows where that redness came from.
  • Elias grabs Lucifer’s arm: “Luci, don’t. Trust me.” What does Elias know? He’s already suspicious of the claymore.
  • “Every student moved out of Kage’s way to give him a clean path to Orion; everyone except Ailani.”
  • Orion’s terror: “Not even against Nerezza had he been this terrified… now he was just as frozen as Abby was that day.” He fears Kage heard him in the dome. He fears exposure. He weighs running, standing, and striking first, and chooses “act casual.”
  • “It took maybe five seconds before it was completely broken by a sniffle from Kage.” Orion “remembered what happened last time Kage cried in front of him.” The schoolyard feint. Great callback.
  • “Hermes, you’re okay..! I’m sorry; I kinda blacked out the other night, and I wasn’t sure if you were able to make it out safe… You wouldn’t believe how bad of a headache I had after I woke up the next morning.” “Blacked out” for having all four limbs broken. Kage being Kage.
  • Kage to Ailani: “I am so so so so SO sorry about flirting with Reina before… You’d be surprised how rare it is for a pretty girl like you to laugh at my dumb jokes.”
  • “Hurt, bewilderment, sorrow, and something akin to relief all fought for it at once.” Good line.
  • “She stared deep into his blue eyes.” No contacts at school.
  • “It really is you. You’re okay…” A smile. Then: “HIJO DE PUTA!” Slap. “PENDEJO!” Orion chuckles. “That chuckle alone was a death sentence.” “YOU DIDN’T THINK TO CHECK AND MAKE SURE THIS IDIOT WASN’T BREATHING BEFORE LETTING ME CRY?!” “IMBÉCIL!”
  • “And Kage? Keep my number.” The reversal of “lose my number” (2.4). It’s the best callback in Section 2.
  • “Since we’re a three-man band now…” Ailani: “three-MAN band instead of a three-person band.” “I should’ve known you were sexist considering all the girls you flirt with.”
  • “I’m not touching Elias. That man gives me the heebie-jeebies.” Ailani: “he is super creepy.”
  • Orion: “I never agreed to do anything with you two!” Kage: “You stood next to us! You could’ve never talked to us again, but you chose to come over! You’re committed, whether you like it or not!”
  • Orion, quietly: “Kage, if you’re okay, is…Abby..?” “Kage was paralyzed… He pulled himself together at the last second by tightly clutching the gold whistle he had around his neck. Orion hadn’t noticed it there until now, but the way Kage was acting made it all the more obvious what the truth was.” No words. Just the whistle.
  • Orion: “Do good for Abby’s sake.”
  • Kage: “Why do you think I showed up? I came here to make sure I beat you in front of everyone.”
  • Anubis: “I didn’t make the bracket! A different teacher did.”
  • Huoyan: “I am mildly contented by your return, Ozul.” “Kage knew that was about as much warmth as he was going to get.” It’s funny, and it’s love.
  • Anubis: “you’re going to be walking with me in the back. I need to have a word with you, Sticky Situation.”

What it’s doing. It brings Kage back, resolves the immediate tension with Orion, and restores Ailani.

What works. Orion’s fear is real and well-written. The double slap is perfect. “Keep my number” is a gift. The whistle as an answer to “is Abby…?” is beautiful.

What I’d push on. The “no one else knew” line, as noted. And Ailani’s Spanish curses are funny, but this is the first time she uses Spanish, and her heritage was introduced in a single line in 1.3. A little more texture earlier (a word or two in the mirror room) would make this feel less like a surprise.

Score 8.5/10

2.15 The Talk And Walk

2,582 words. The “camaraderie walk” to the stadium doors.

What happens. Walking at the back, Anubis tells Kage he saw him on camera Thursday, clinging to the ceiling and entering Lucifer’s room. Kage deflects by attacking Anubis for photographing students without consent. Anubis, delighted, says he wants Kage as his successor: “You don’t put on a heroic act; you just act heroic,” and “an orphaned failure turned number one hero just sells itself!” He mentions Lucifer’s “backstory” and Frostbite. Kage lies that he barely escaped. Lance and Rebento, the class reps, mention the crowd will see whoever walks through the doors first. Kage wants to be first. Lance offers to help: “Heroes help other heroes.” Kage asks Anubis why he never encouraged him in first year. “You weren’t Sticky Situation in your first year.” Lance and Rebento throw Kage over the crowd. He races to the doors, and the capacity crowd cheers him. “He wanted this forever.”

Details I caught.

  • The walk: “no one was allowed to use their enhancements.” The groups: Lucifer leads; Ailani and Orion together, joined by Zulfiya; “Severine walked alone”; “Volkan nervously trailed alone”; Cheyenne with “a gray-haired boy and a brown-haired boy” (Lance and Rebento). It’s a little sociogram.
  • Anubis: “our security cameras in school are top of the line and everywhere. I saw you clinging to a ceiling while looking at Ailani and Orion.… Last I saw, you were heading into Lucifer’s room, and you never came back out. We aren’t legally allowed to have cameras in student dorms despite my asking.” He asked for dorm cameras. In 4.0, the villains are murdering politicians who want “cameras all over the hero city.” The heroes want surveillance. The villains oppose it.
  • Anubis knew Kage was alive on Thursday and told no one, including Huoyan, who was frantically texting staff about Abby.
  • Kage: “Wait, you want cameras in all of our rooms..?” A fair question.
  • “DAMMIT, KAGE!” Then: “Woo-! I apologize. My temper kinda came out there for a moment!” Anubis has a temper under the charm.
  • Kage: “Like how you’re honest about taking pictures of students without their consent? We know we’re on camera at all times, but we don’t know that you photograph and log everything into your files.” Kage just admitted he saw the files. Anubis doesn’t pursue it.
  • Anubis grabs his shoulder: “Kage, are you challenging my authority as head of the hero program?” Kage feels he “might fall through the floor down to the earth’s core.” Then: “That’s wonderful!” and a pat on the head. Everyone pats this kid’s head.
  • “Heroes aren’t meant to take everything at face value!” “God, I knew I loved you, but you’re becoming even more of my favorite every day!”
  • “Last time I gave you my little speech about systems, you just agreed with me. I almost called a physician!” Confirms 1.14 was a test.
  • The pitch: “I need someone to take my place someday soon. I strongly believe that person will be you! There’s something in you that I don’t see in many others. You’re real! You don’t put on a heroic act; you just act heroic. That’s incredibly rare! Plus, your story is wonderful for the program’s brand! An orphaned failure turned number one hero just sells itself!” Sincerity and marketing in the same breath. That’s Anubis exactly. And why “someday soon”? Is he sick? Retiring? Planning something?
  • Successor history: “I had my brother in mind at first for who’s next, but his heart’s just not in it. Then, it was Lucifer! It would be an effort trying to cover up all that backstory, and his personality leaves a lot to be desired… And, Huoyan is kind of scouting him himself.”
  • “Backstory?” “He thought of the ten-foot demon outside the warehouse and kept his face perfectly still.” “Irrelevant.” Anubis knows about Abaddon.
  • “I just wanted to catalog the rise of Kage Ozul! I mean, you survived an attack from Frostbite!” “His hand curled around nothing at his side, but Anubis didn’t see.” The third time, and again it’s about Frostbite.
  • Kage: “That dude is insanely powerful. I barely got away.”
  • Anubis: “I was worried you might’ve been compromised and instead, you know when to retreat?! Marvelous!” He says “compromised” out loud. Either he believes the lie, or he wants Kage to think he does.
  • Lance: “gray hair was quite reflective in the light… His stern face was not something Kage enjoyed.” Rebento: “Dude, you do know you’re only vice-rep, right? I’m the main one here.”
  • “Lucifer has been quite vigilant about no one walking past him. Then again, no one has actually tried walking past him yet.”
  • Kage: “I want to be the number one person they’re cheering for.”
  • Lance, intrigued: “You wish to beat Lucifer to the door?”
  • “OH-! I did a funny! ‘Sticking’ it to someone! Eh! Eh! C’mon, you guys could at least pretend to smile at my joke…” “While your humor is subpar…”
  • Kage first refuses: “I kind of want to do this myself. It’s sorta my thing.” Lance: “Heroes help other heroes. I’m not saying you couldn’t do this yourself, but in the future, I’m sure there will be a time where we need to work together. Why don’t we help establish that relationship now?” Kage: “I should probably get used to working with others. That’s never been my strong suit.” That’s growth. The boy who said “I’m gonna be able to handle it all on my own” lets two classmates throw him.
  • Lance on Lucifer: “a hotheaded, poor-tempered, egotistical idiot… that horned moron.” Kage: “You only get one insult before I start insulting back, though!”
  • “You don’t know our enhancements, Kage?” “I haven’t really paid much attention to either of you the past year or so.” “Right, I forget that everyone here is stronger than me…”
  • The question: “If you really think this much of me… why didn’t you ever try talking me into doing this during my first year?… maybe I would’ve actually believed it if it came from you.” Anubis: “That’s easy. You weren’t Sticky Situation in your first year.” It’s warm on the surface. Underneath, it’s chilling: Anubis wasn’t interested in the boy. He was interested in the brand the boy became.
  • Rebento: “Are you ready to show that demon boy that we aren’t just little beetles for him to step on?”
  • The throw. “He got that feeling again. That same wonderful heroic feeling when he was airborne during that race with Orion. Except this time, people actually were able to see him up above.”
  • Orion to Ailani: “There goes Sticky Situation soaring overhead.” He says the full name with pride now.
  • Elias “stood there analyzing in confusion.” Lucifer: “BASTARD!” Elias smacks him: “He’s too far ahead, Luci. You’re just gonna have to eat it on this one.”
  • The doors: “he could just feel the explosive energy of the capacity crowd. They were applauding him… All he knew was he wanted this forever, and he was going to make sure he was the best hero he could be. That way, the whole world would never stop cheering for him.” The section ends with Kage hungry to be watched. That’s the danger the whole book has been circling.

What it’s doing. It closes Section 2 with Anubis’s pitch, Kage’s first real teamwork, and the first taste of an adoring crowd.

What works. Anubis’s speech is the best version of him: charming, sincere, mercenary, and frightening. “You weren’t Sticky Situation in your first year” is one of the best lines in the book. The throw is a joyful image, and the ending is a perfect ominous note.

What I’d push on. Kage confronts Anubis about the files, which means he’s admitting he broke into the office. Anubis doesn’t react. Either have Anubis clock it (“So it was you”), or have Kage realize his slip a beat later. Right now, it’s the kind of thing a careful reader stops on.

Score 8.5/10

Section 2 overall

Section 2 is named for Abby, and it’s about what happens when support is gone. It opens with Abby choosing not to be Lucifer’s friend, because that’s what helps him, and it ends with Kage running toward a crowd because the person who supported him is dead and he needs someone, anyone, to cheer. In between, it holds the book’s two best chapters (2.6 and 2.7), its best quiet chapter (2.8), and its biggest reveals (Abaddon, the biomass, the telekinesis rule). The middle (2.4, 2.13) is a little slower, but even those chapters plant things that pay off in Sections 3 and 4.

This is the best section of the book.

Section score: 8.6/10.

Section 3: “Elite Tournament”

A note on how this section is built. The tournament is a 16-person bracket split into two halves. Gene names the rounds unusually: Round 2 is “the semi-finals,” each half’s final is a “final,” and the last match is the “grand finals.” The bracket so far:

First half Second half
Lucifer def. Severine (ring-out) Lucifer def. Reina Orion def. Inazuma (ring-out) Orion def. Rebento
Reina def. Geneki (out of bounds) ↓ Rebento def. Alexander (ring-out) ↓
Kage def. Zulfiya (forfeit) Kage def. Elias (yield) Ailani def. Youseff (fake trip) Ailani def. Lance (out of bounds)
Elias def. Cheyenne (ring-out) Kage def. Lucifer (KO, by a split second) Lance def. Volkan (walked out) Orion vs. Ailani (next)

Kage waits in the grand final for the winner of Orion vs. Ailani.

3.1 And…Begin!

2,242 words. The stadium, the student lobby, and the hero lobby, Saturday.

What happens. Lucifer, furious, threatens Kage in front of the cameras; Reina makes him drop Kage. Orion privately tells Ailani that Kage “shouldn’t be breathing right now,” and asks her to keep an eye on him, without telling her why. Upstairs, we meet Kelly Iasacht (a top hero who doesn’t teach and gets less funding for it) and Gene Gardner (Rhatella and Geneki’s mother, creator of the tournament and the Elite Program, and its announcer). Anubis says he’s cheering for Kage partly because Kage has no parents in the stands; Huoyan says that’s self-serving and selective. Gene explains the stakes: losing in the first round means no Elite Program, and fewer than half will be chosen. The first match is Lucifer vs. Severine.

Details I caught.

  • “Kage’s massive smile radiated all over the arena screens.” The screens matter. In 3.4 they show his screaming face.
  • “Boy, aren’t I lucky you can’t use your power right now, huh?”
  • “I hope it was worth it, termite.” “I pray to any and all gods that I get to crush you in the first round.” He gets to crush him, eventually, in the half final, and loses.
  • Reina: “Lu! Put him down, and let’s go!… Let’s move, or I’ll make you move!” Reina gives Lucifer orders. He obeys.
  • Orion’s briefing: “You asked me why I didn’t check if Kage was breathing before I told you he was dead. That’s because I didn’t think I had to.” “Kage shouldn’t be breathing right now!… I could see blood pouring out of everywhere blood could pour. Nobody could just survive that.”
  • Ailani: “maybe a person with an extreme healing enhancement got to him.” Orion: “Healing enhancements are already rare… I know it’s really him and not some shapeshifter. No one can act exactly like Kage.”
  • “Every word Orion said was true, but it wasn’t everything.” This sentence (or a version of it) appears every time Orion talks to Ailani about that night. The book is tracking his omissions carefully.
  • Ailani: “It seems to me like you DO care about him, Orion!” “Kage taught me humor is a good way to handle feelings like that… If he didn’t teach me that, I probably would’ve killed both of you earlier when Kage came back.”
  • Kage: “You guys must’ve ate my dust!” Dialect, fine.
  • Kelly Iasacht: “a blond and blue-eyed hero… a consistent top hero who wished to claim Anubis’s spot, but he got significantly less government funding since he chose not to teach at the hero school.” The state pays heroes to teach. That’s why Anubis and Huoyan are there.
  • “My wife was already yelling at me to call you.” Kelly has a wife and a son.
  • Huoyan slumps on the couch, phone out: “Anubis had been right about Kage, but Huoyan still needed to find out what’s going on with Abby.” He’s the only adult still looking.
  • Gene Gardner: “rat-like features with large ears, a snout, and whiskers… She was the creator of the Elite Tournament and Elite Program. Her green suit… her green glasses… black hair.” “Oh, I just adore announcing and hosting stuff like this.”
  • “I know Rhatella is more interested in inventing than hero work anyhow.” So Rhatella chose not to enter.
  • Kelly: “My son didn’t end up showing up after all, so I got no kids of my own to watch, sadly. I can never win, I swear.” Who’s his son? He’s a student in this program who didn’t compete. He may be the nineteenth classmate.
  • Gene: “my wonderful daughter Geneki! I have her in the first half of the bracket.”
  • Huoyan: “I teach all of them. I am impartial. I do not care who wins.”
  • Anubis: “I personally am hoping Kage Ozul wins the whole thing, but if not, I’d be very happy if Orion Vance ends up champion. It’d be wonderfully poetic if either of them win after Huoyan here tried to stop them from fighting.”
  • “I would be happy if my brother won, but I don’t think that’ll happen.”
  • Anubis teases Huoyan about scouting Lucifer: “I get your enhancements are similar, but he really is just too much of a character to be at the top.” Huoyan: “Tulivalo has the highest average of anybody in class… He wants to be a hero more than most, so I want him to hero alongside me… if he’s in my agency.” Huoyan has an agency.
  • The orphan speech: “Every kid in that lobby has their parents cheering for them… Kage doesn’t have that… He’ll perform better if he isn’t sad about his orphanism.” Huoyan: “That last sentence ruined the whole speech for me… you know that line about every kid having parents cheering isn’t completely true. If that were really your reason, you’d be rooting for every kid out there with no one in the stands or watching on T.V.” (This rebuttal is stronger in Draft 3.) Anubis: “Believe what you’d like, but I’m cheering for Kage no matter what.”
  • Gene’s rules: “If you lose in the first round, you are disqualified from the Elite Program entirely. You may as well go home.… Less than half of you will be selected.”
  • Ailani: “Don’t be sad if you get eliminated first.” Kage: “I came back here just for the extra training. I’m getting in that program.” That’s not the whole reason, but it’s the one he says.
  • Ailani’s eyes “lingered on Kage a tad longer than usual… If anything, it made her squint harder, looking for an imperfection.” Orion has planted a seed.
  • “Thank you to the millions watching at home!”
  • Severine “didn’t respond to or acknowledge anyone.” (Grammar fixed in Draft 3.)
  • Lucifer: “Cruz, you better watch this… And Min, can you not judge my technique when I get back?” Elias: “Fine, Luci. I will remain exclusively supportive.” A dry, funny line.
  • The arena: “a massive dome of thick, clear glass,” “a simple, large square made of white concrete surrounded by grass that counted as out of bounds.”
  • The rules: “You can win by either submission, forfeit, knockout, or ring out!… Killing is also obviously banned, but I would pray you guys don’t want to kill one another!” “I hope you listened because I am not saying them again!”
  • “Three…Two…One…And…Begin!” The title.

What it’s doing. It’s the tournament’s setup chapter: two new adults, the rules, the stakes, and Orion’s suspicion.

What works. Kelly and Gene are introduced efficiently and distinctly. Huoyan’s rebuttal of Anubis is sharper in this draft. The rules are clearly stated, which matters because the whole tournament turns on technicalities.

What I’d push on. This is a lot of setup before the first punch. The adult banter is fun, but it runs long. You could move some of it (Anubis’s favorites, for example) into the intermission in 3.3, so the first match starts sooner.

Score 7/10

3.2 Quick And Painful

3,088 words. Arena: Lucifer vs. Severine; then Reina vs. Geneki.

What happens. Severine throws up a barrier and gloats. Lucifer blasts it with fire from both hands and his mouth, but he isn’t trying to break it. He’s noticed the barrier is a half-dome she’s standing on, so he pushes her backward across the floor until she’s over the out-of-bounds grass. When she drops the barrier to attack, the bell rings. Severine melts down on live TV. Kelly is sent to escort her out (so the program isn’t liable if she fights him). Realizing she’s talked to no classmate except Kage, she screams for him to defend her. Kage, seeing the convenience store and Abby’s death, slides down the wall out of sight. Lance comforts him, Kage snaps, and Ailani calms him. Then Geneki stomps the floor so hard she breaks through it, and loses.

Details I caught.

  • Severine: “I wouldn’t want to embarrass the hero program’s current poster boy with my absolute perfection!”
  • Lucifer: “you’re nothing but a mosquito.” Insects again.
  • Lucifer’s card: Hellspawn. “Hell isn’t somewhere Lucifer came from; it’s something he carries with him!” Fire from “his fists, his horns, his tail, his eyes,” at any temperature, from “a surgical burn” to “an inferno that melts steel like paper!” And the second half: “When Lucifer is put fully to sleep, something inside him cracks open!… Abaddon, a ten-foot wall of black skin and scorched bone!… Very few know of Abaddon’s existence, and even fewer have lived after seeing it!” That last line tells you Abaddon has killed.
  • “You’ve exhibited one of my many dislikes: foolishness!”
  • Elias pats Reina’s head: “Luci actually seems to be thinking strategically. If he noticed the same thing I did, then I believe he’s going to win.” Reina: “He’s being tactical right now?! No way!”
  • The trick: “Severine’s barrier wasn’t a dome, but rather a semicircle. That meant she was standing on the flat bottom of her own barrier… A barrier that could be pushed and moved. He didn’t need to break through the barrier. He just needed to push her out of bounds.” It’s a perfect puzzle-fight. And it’s poetic: Severine told Kage “drive does not win fights.” She loses to strategy disguised as brute force.
  • The third beam comes from Lucifer’s mouth.
  • “These idiotic onlookers! All they care about is flash, even if it’s substanceless!” She’s not wrong about the crowd.
  • “Prepare yourself, Tulivalo! Now, it’s my turn!” She drops the barrier. BONG.
  • Lucifer “didn’t even acknowledge Severine… He simply smiled through the glass at Reina, who was starstruck.”
  • “THIS IS BLASPHEMOUS! THIS IS PROFANE! THIS…THIS IS…BORDERLINE SACRILEGIOUS!” Her perfection is a religion. Its rules have been violated.
  • Huoyan: “I’ll head down to help her out.” Anubis: “No, you won’t… It’s better if it’s someone who doesn’t work for the hero program. That way, if she does something dumb and a fight ensues, we aren’t fully liable.” Liability before care.
  • Kelly: “Listen, kid. There will be other times to win. Everybody loses.” “I AM NOT EVERYONE! I AM IN A CLASS AND LEAGUE OF MY OWN!”
  • “Why am I always stuck with these jobs?”
  • “THAT’S FOR THE PEASANTS! THE COMMONERS! THE NOBODIES!” Severine thinks like an aristocrat.
  • New in Draft 3: “She realized in her rage that she hadn’t really talked to a single one of her classmates, and for a moment that rage quieted enough for something somber to bleed through.” That one clause makes her a person.
  • “OZUL! TELL THEM! TELL THEM ALL HOW PERFECT I AM RIGHT THIS INSTANT!” She calls out to the only classmate she ever spoke to, the boy she beat and laughed at.
  • Kage’s trigger: “His hand reached out to press against the glass, and his other clung to the whistle. He didn’t see Severine down there. He saw the convenience store, and the world breaking around him. Then, it was Abby, the tentacle stabbing straight through her. His hand let go of the whistle. Then he pulled his other hand away, turned, and slowly slid down the wall, out of Severine’s sight.” A person calling his name for help is the one thing he can’t take.
  • “Her body slumped in Kelly’s grasp, if only for a second.” She noticed him disappear.
  • Rebento: “I heard you were a ladies’ man, but Severine?!… Were her lips cold during kissing too?” Lance slaps him: “REBENTO, THAT IS REPULSIVE!! STOP BEING SO CRUDE!”
  • Kage: “Me and her were nothing! I wouldn’t even say we were friends!… she laughed at me and that was it! She also said that she wanted to fight me again later when I was uhhh…significantly superior.”
  • Elias: “He’s telling the truth, idiot.… I don’t want him to have the excuse that he was ‘shaken’ if I have to beat him in the first round.” Elias reads truth, and he seems to know who he’s facing. Kage fights Zulfiya first, then Elias. It’s close enough to make you wonder whether Elias knows the bracket or something more.
  • Lance’s counsel: Kage: “A person was calling out for me to save them, and I just…left them. What kind of hero does that make me?” Lance: “All heroes freeze, Kage.” (Orion said almost the same thing in 1.1.) Kage: “No, they don’t! They’re not supposed to!… I didn’t just freeze. Oh, no, I walked away from her!… God, I’m such a fucking coward.” Lance: “What Severine was going through there wasn’t danger. And you know that too, don’t you?… You can’t save people from themselves.” “If she was hardheaded enough to not speak to anyone for a year and a half…”
  • Lance: “Is everything okay, Kage?”
  • “DO NOT TELL ME TO CALM DOWN!” Kage clamps the whistle. “You’re just the perfect hero, aren’t you? The great Lance knows everything about everything. Well, you don’t know me! I’m gonna be the hero that saves everyone! I am never ever gonna mess that up again!”
  • Ailani’s touch: “Kage, please calm down. It’s okay.” He calms instantly.
  • “Orion didn’t say anything, but he had this moment filed in his head for now, right next to every other unanswered question about Kage.”
  • Kage bows to Lance and apologizes. Lance: “Rebento is more like you, so maybe you’d prefer hearing this kind of thing from him.” “I tend to get kind of preachy anyway.” Kage: “Was that a joke?… You do know that’s my job, right?” Lance: “Oh, the straight man doesn’t get to be funny too? You’re putting me in slots, Kage. Not cool.” A great line, and it’s the start of their friendship.
  • Geneki: “a large and muscular woman… easily eight feet tall… one of her eyes was lazy… the same rat ears as her sister Rhatella, but her tail was just a cut-off stump.” Why is her tail a stump? Nobody says. The whole class steps back from her except Ailani and Kage.
  • Ailani: “Hey, good luck out there, Gen!” They’re friends.
  • Kage: “That’s the big girl?! I didn’t think she was a monster!” Ailani: “She’s not a monster! She’s a very nice girl. It’s just everyone here is scared of her.”
  • Gene: “I would’ve had her go first, but that would’ve showed too much favoritism.” Anubis: “She’s incredibly strong, but lacks any uhm…personality.”
  • Huoyan hands Gene the mic “in a sort of genuflection. He was obviously being sarcastic.”
  • “Green energy appeared around Geneki’s leg. She slammed her foot hard into the ground… BONG.” “Uhm…what just…happened..?”

What it’s doing. It opens the tournament with two puzzle-endings, devastates Severine, and shows Kage’s trauma in public.

What works. Lucifer’s half-dome trick is clever and well-staged. Severine’s meltdown is funny and then, with the new line, sad. Kage’s flashback is one of the best-placed in the book. Lance’s “putting me in slots” line is a delight.

What I’d push on. Kage’s outburst at Lance is the second time he’s yelled “don’t tell me what to do” at someone trying to help (after 1.11). That’s in character, but with the biomass now amplifying him, it would help to show him noticing his own temper, a beat of “where did that come from?” That would separate this outburst from 1.11 and make the biomass’s influence visible.

Score 8/10

3.3 Which Kage Is Best?

2,169 words. The lobby and hero lounge, during an intermission.

What happens. Gene explains Geneki’s loss: she stomped so hard the floor disintegrated, so her foot touched under the arena, which counts as out of bounds. Geneki asks to go home. During the repair break, Gene plugs the merch tables. Huoyan argues with math that Kage can’t win, since everyone else has a year more training; Anubis says Kage trains harder than anyone, and that the hero world wants someone “relatable.” Down in the lobby, Inazuma Sokudo, a blue-haired speedster, greets Kage as an old friend from middle school in Japan and reveals he’s the one who sent the maid-outfit photo. He calls Orion slow, then calls him a “big black dolt.” Ailani calls it racist. Kage walks away. Kage’s first-round match is called: he faces Zulfiya.

Details I caught.

  • Gene: “here’s the four-one-one!” “She was touching under the arena floor. Which, by my rule standards, is technically out of bounds.” This exact rule wins Ailani her match against Lance in 4.7.
  • “You need to be more careful with your powers, hun. I thought I taught you better.” Gene scolds her daughter in front of millions.
  • Kage: “Concrete is like the strongest thing I can make!” “Another person was that much further ahead of him in the strength department. Duly noted, and horribly scary.”
  • Ailani: “I trained with her a little bit because her workouts are best for strength. I didn’t keep going though because I was worried my muscles might get as big as hers. It’s hard to dance when you’re that bulky.” Second dance clue.
  • Geneki: “Mother, you said that anyone who loses in the first round is disqualified from the Elite Program entirely. If that is the case, then I wish to go home now. I would much rather be in my room. Alone, preferably.” Gene: “You may go home. I’ll see you when I get there.” “For once, Gene didn’t sound like she was talking to the cameras.” A small, sad family moment.
  • Reina, staring at the crater: “That…could’ve been me…”
  • Lucifer: “Cruz, you did it!” “Reina… tackle-hugged Lucifer. Elias gently patted her on the back.”
  • Elias: “I wouldn’t want to let you two surpass me.”
  • Gene: “now would be a great time to hit up the concessions! And, don’t hesitate to check out our merch tables! We have hero program T-shirts, cups, hats, and souvenirs galore!” In 4.2, Kage and Lucifer end up wearing that merch.
  • Anubis: “Now, that is good advertising! See, Huoyan? That is how you do it!” “If Kage lost in the first round, I’d probably give him a similar look. What a failure that would be.” Kage later says Anubis’s love “is conditional.” This is the line that proves him right.
  • Huoyan’s math: “every other student has a year more experience than him!… it’s still only been what…six months?… If someone has half the potential as Ozul, and they’ve done a year more work, then they’re winning. They have triple the training time. One times one is less than zero point five times three. That’s just basic math, Khaled.” I checked: 1 × 1 = 1, and 0.5 × 3 = 1.5. Huoyan is right. Anubis: “I was never big on math unless it came to money, but by your logic, if Kage has three times their potential instead of twice as much, then he would surpass them all.” Huoyan: “You’re still clearly not big on math. If Ozul had three times, that would make him equal.” Also right: (1/3) × 3 = 1. The author built a character joke on correct arithmetic.
  • Anubis: “Sometimes you need to break to grow back stronger.” Kage was literally broken and regrown on Monday. Anubis doesn’t know.
  • Huoyan: “Hm, Khaled, why do you believe in him so much? I get seeing yourself in someone, but what draws you to Ozul?” Does Anubis see himself in Kage? Was Anubis once a lazy, orphaned, written-off kid? That would explain a lot.
  • Anubis: “The hero world is boring now. Everyone holds the same ideals, and everyone is getting more and more samey. People aren’t into the whole ‘righteous perfect man’ anymore. They want someone they can relate to. Kage is ultra-relatable!… A kid willing to do most anything to win a fight. That’s a good hero, no?” “Most anything” is exactly what Thanatos bought.
  • “If you’d broken it up like you wanted to, Kage might not’ve found the spark.”
  • Inazuma: “WAZZZUPPPPPPPP!!!” “Duddddddeeeeee, it’s the red to my blue!” Blue hair, dark blue eyes. “You barely talk to me anymore.”
  • “HELL YEAH WE’RE FRIENDS!… I’ve known Kage since middle school! You should’ve seen the surprise on my face when this bum said he wanted to go to America for the hero program!” So Inazuma is also from Japan, and also in the program.
  • “I sent him that ‘Remember this?’ pic of him in a maid outfit a few months ago. Never got a reply, though…” Pays off 1.5 exactly (sharper in Draft 3).
  • And Inazuma is a speedster. That’s the “another speedster” from 1.2.
  • Ailani knew about the café: “I always loved laughing at seeing his different-colored costumes. He made good money too! Those guys there must’ve really liked him!” (In 4.1, we see first-year Kage with cat ears.)
  • “Which Kage is best? I think maid Kage crushes desk-nap Kage!” The title. Ailani: “Who wouldn’t pick the Kage that was so cute?”
  • “They joked about ‘first-year him’ as if he didn’t hate that version of himself.” “Abby called him that once, too. His hand drifted onto the whistle before he even noticed.” “Cute” is Abby’s word.
  • Inazuma to Orion: “Big Vance over here! You do know this guy is pretty slow, right, Kage?” “My last name is Sokudo, after all!” Sokudo means “speed” in Japanese. Inazuma believes his name is his destiny.
  • Orion: “If you truly were faster than me, then there would be no debate.” “Kage might be rubbing off on him too much.”
  • “You seriously think this big black dolt is faster than me?” A racist slur, said casually.
  • “Kage didn’t even blink at the comment. He put his hands up and backed out of the conversation. ‘I am not doing this right now.’” Kage walks away from his friend being insulted. It’s a real failure, and the book makes him answer for it in 3.5.
  • Ailani: “That’s a little racist, Inazuma.” Inazuma: “Oh, don’t be a snowflake! I’m not being racist. I’m just describing him!”
  • “Orion’s jaw tightened, but he didn’t say anything. He just took a slow breath.”
  • Orion: “Tell this blue-haired fool that he could never beat me in a race.” Ailani: “Is that a hint of nervousness in your voice?” Inazuma: “He could tell from my aura alone.”
  • Kage vs. Zulfiya is called. Kage “bolted on stage, itching to hear the cheering crowd again… The realization didn’t come until he was face to face with the light blue hair.” “Hi, King.” “The smile seemed off.”

What it’s doing. It delivers two old payoffs (the photo, “another speedster”), introduces a new antagonist in Inazuma, and sets up the racism thread that drives Orion’s next two chapters.

What works. Huoyan’s math, which is correct, is a delightful touch. The “Which Kage is best” banter is light and then quietly painful. Inazuma’s intro is loud and well-drawn.

What I’d push on. Kage walking away from Inazuma’s slur is a choice the book takes seriously in the next chapter, which is good. But in this scene, the line “Kage didn’t even blink” could use one more beat of interiority. Is he avoiding conflict? Does he not hear it as a slur? Is he ashamed? Giving readers his reason (even a bad one) would make 3.5’s confrontation land more fairly.

Score 7.5/10

3.4 Forfeit Versus Failure

3,124 words. Arena: Kage vs. Zulfiya; Elias vs. Cheyenne.

What happens. Orion tells Ailani that Zulfiya’s hair can read bodies, so if anything’s wrong with Kage, she’ll find it. Before the count finishes, Zulfiya raises her hand and forfeits, publicly, because Kage is her King and she wants to “support” him. Kage, triggered by that word, grabs her and screams at her to take it back, on every screen in the stadium. Anubis stops Huoyan from intervening (“PR nightmare”). Zulfiya, seeing his eyes are sad, not angry, says she forfeited because he’s stronger. Orion notes it’s Kage’s second outburst in an hour. We learn Lance and Cheyenne are dating. Nobody knows Elias’s power; Orion guesses strength based on a baseball and tells Cheyenne to strike fast. She charges at “one,” and her power card is cut off mid-sentence as Elias freezes her, beats her while frozen, and releases all the banked force at once. Kage will face Elias next.

Details I caught.

  • Orion: “Zulfiya’s enhancement can read bodies well. It’s not as good as Abby’s was… She was able to read Kage’s temperature to the exact degree… she corrected herself down to the exact minute.”
  • Ailani: “Woah, woah, woah. Kage collapsed?” She didn’t know. They weren’t talking.
  • “We’re not spying… We’re just…watching for the nuances.”
