  • Ailani asks for a plan; Orion stays silent for twenty seconds. “Half of the reason was that he didn’t exactly have an answer. The other half was focus.”
  • Huoyan: “You don’t need to yell! The microphone is right in front of you!”
  • Gene: “Plus, it’s Anubis’s project on that stage!” “Project.” Thanatos called Kage “my project” in 2.10. Both his mentors call him a project.
  • Huoyan: “If you tell me to take notes one more time, I will burn this lobby down.” Anubis: “Technically, I’ve never told you to ‘take notes.’” Great comic timing.
  • Kage: “Sup, Zebra! Eh, nah. Sorry, I’m not the greatest with nicknames.” He came up with five Hermes epithets for Orion. He’s great with nicknames when he cares.
  • Zulfiya: “King, I didn’t think this would end up this way. I do apologize.”
  • Gene: “It all starts in three…two-!” Zulfiya “casually raised her hand as if asking a question in class.” “I forfeit.”
  • “Ailani facepalmed in frustration while Orion’s eyes squinted that much harder.”
  • Gene: “She just lost her eligibility for the program!”
  • Zulfiya: “You see, Kage Ozul is my King. He claimed it himself, and I wish to honor his want.… I am forfeiting my admittance to the Elite Program. I want Kage to prosper. Consider this my way of publicly showing my support.”
  • “At the word ‘support,’ his hand instinctively clutched Abby’s whistle.” (New in Draft 3.)
  • “Zulfiya, take that back.” “Hmm? What was that, King?” “That was a pretty big mistake.”
  • “TAKE BACK WHAT YOU SAID! YOU’RE NOT GOING TO LIVE YOUR LIFE TO SUPPORT MINE! IF YOU WANT TO FORFEIT, FORFEIT CUZ YOU’RE WORRIED YOU’LL GET GLUE IN YOUR HAIR OR SOMETHING! SAY IT’S CUZ YOU KNOW I’M STRONGER! NOT CUZ YOU’LL BE SUPPORTING ME!” The last person who said she’d support him died for it. He’d rather be called arrogant than let anyone else sacrifice for him.
  • “The same screens that had beamed Kage’s massive smile at the start of the tournament now blew up his screaming face for the whole stadium to see.” Great structural echo.
  • Anubis: “Leave it. If you go down there, then the PR nightmare this could be gets worse.… We’re not doing that again.”
  • “Zulfiya should’ve been afraid to see Kage yelling at her, but instead she focused on his eyes. They weren’t angry. They were distressed, and they bordered on sorrowful.” A healer reading a body. She puts a hand to his cheek. “Okay, I’m forfeiting because I think you’re stronger.”
  • “Never say you are doing anything to exclusively support me ever again.”
  • Orion: “This is the second time Kage had an outburst in the past hour. His stress levels are crazy high.”
  • Ailani: “Maybe he’s still just hurting from Abby. You told me that whistle was hers, right? I saw him clutch it when yelling at Lance.” Orion told her about the whistle. That means he’s told her more about Abby than we saw.
  • Orion: “Kage is civilized enough to not grieve by lashing out like this.… If he couldn’t control himself, then wouldn’t he have gone after you every day after that whole gym incident? He didn’t… He’s more emotionally intelligent than he looks.” Orion understands Kage better than almost anyone.
  • Ailani: “If he wasn’t, he’d be somber over his family all the time.”
  • Orion’s plan: “We need to just keep watching until he shows something more. Something no one can come up with an excuse for.” “It’s the only plan I have that’s foolproof.” “For a guy with no plan, he sounded weirdly relieved about it.” Ailani notices that Orion doesn’t want to find anything. Because if Kage’s secret comes out, Orion’s might too.
  • Zulfiya walks back “all smiles,” but “Her hair… stayed tucked behind her ear the whole walk, and not a single strand drifted his way.” She’s respecting his boundary, or avoiding finding something. Possibly both.
  • Kage, forced: “Zulfiya here didn’t think she could hang!” Zulfiya: “There wasn’t a world where I would beat him, so I thought to just give up right then and there!” She covers for him.
  • “Of course you can win, babe!” Lance and Cheyenne are dating. Rebento: “Like, I get I’m single, but do you two have to shove how I’m single in my face every single day?” “Kage doesn’t know anything about anyone!” “That one stung.”
  • Cheyenne calls Lance “sugar.” Lance: “effort is one of the most important parts of a relationship. If you don’t put forth any after you’ve officially started dating, maybe that’s why your relationships don’t last longer than a week.” Rebento: “I swear I’ll blow you up in front of everyone here!” (He means it literally. See 3.7.)
  • Nobody knows Elias’s power. Orion: “All I know is I saw him with a baseball once, and the next thing I knew it was flying out of the building. He didn’t have a baseball bat.” That’s banked force, released. Orion guesses strength and gives Cheyenne the wrong advice: strike first, at “one.”
  • Kage wonders “if Elias actually wanted to be a hero or just be Lucifer’s babysitter.” More right than he knows.
  • New in Draft 3: “Technically, the rules only said they could start as soon as Gene said so, and Gene had definitely said ‘one.’”
  • “Her name is Cheyenne Bandalore! Her enhancement is—” The card is cut off mid-sentence. The narrator gets frozen too. It’s the best meta-joke in the book, and it keeps her power a mystery.
  • Elias: “Why in the world would you ever dash towards me like that? You shouldn’t have done that. That’s very sad. I truly feel bad for you.”
  • “P-Partner, how are you doing this?”
  • Elias: “You don’t know my enhancement? That’s unfortunate, but it explains your naive charge.” Remember this line. Kage returns it in 3.11.
  • “You’re making my clock tick down rather fast. You are strong. That’s very admirable.” He adjusts his glasses, and the banked strikes hit all at once.
  • Elias’s card: Chronal Inertia. “Motion is a privilege!” Touch stops anything “dead in spacetime.” Forces are “banked, not blocked!” and released all at once. It takes extreme focus; every hold is “another stream of live calculation.” Training gave him “superhuman perception and razor-sharp processing.” His glasses are Rhatella’s support item; they “track exactly how long every hold has left.” “Every hold starts with three minutes on the clock, but that clock only burns when the world pushes back! A still object barely spends a second of it… Forces getting banked on objects eat through the clock! And living organisms can struggle against Elias’s grasp!” It’s one of the most carefully designed powers in the book, and the next fight uses every rule.
  • “Orion put his head in his hands. He’d turned one baseball into a whole game plan, and Cheyenne was the one who paid for it.”
  • Elias “pushed his glasses up with one finger.” He and Kage lock eyes. “Kage’s first real battle of the tournament was going to be against Chronal Inertia.”

What it’s doing. It turns Kage’s grief into a public spectacle, reveals Orion’s reluctance to find the truth, and introduces Elias’s power in full before Kage has to face it.

What works. Zulfiya’s forfeit is a brilliant trigger: a kind act that hits Kage’s deepest wound. The cut-off card is genius. Elias’s power is designed like a puzzle box. Orion’s “weirdly relieved” is a sharp, quiet beat.

What I’d push on. Ailani says “You told me that whistle was hers.” That means Orion told her about the whistle. That’s a meaningful conversation we didn’t see. Either show a line of it, or acknowledge it (“Orion had told her that much on the bus”), so readers don’t wonder when it happened.

Score 8.5/10

3.5 Just Like You Both

1,187 words. The student lobby.

What happens. Inazuma complains the tournament has been “nothing but talking.” Ailani sends him away. Kage defends Inazuma: he texted Kage all last year; he’s just “devoid of a filter.” Ailani says Inazuma reminds her of Kage. Orion asks, flatly, “Are you racist as well, Kage?” Kage protests that he dates Black women and talks to Orion all the time. Orion says that isn’t evidence and brings up Kage breaking Ailani’s heart, and Ailani slaps Orion for using her relationship as ammunition. Orion apologizes, but internally he’s furious that he’s the one apologizing. Kage tells him to “let them slide off you.” Orion vs. Inazuma is called. Inazuma refuses Orion’s handshake and plans to win by provoking him.

Details I caught.

  • Inazuma: “This is the fourth fight in a row that’s had nothing! I’m dying to see some real combat!… This whole tournament has been nothing but talking!” The author letting a character say what some readers are thinking. It’s a good wink, and it works because it’s said by the most annoying person in the room.
  • Kage “fixed his big hair.” The new look has a name now: big hair.
  • Kage: “You two really are heroes to me.”
  • Ailani: “we only halfway did it for you. We more so don’t want him around cuz of what he said earlier.”
  • Kage: “You mean the black comment? Yeah, Inazuma doesn’t really mean anything by it. I’ve tried to stay nice to him because at the very least he texted me all of last year. Even when I never texted back.” Kage owes Inazuma something real: he was the only person who reached out during the ghost year.
  • Orion: “Racist. He’s always been racist?” Kage: “He’s just devoid of a filter.”
  • Ailani: “Yeah, that’s not an excuse, Kage… If he was nice, then he would’ve apologized when we called him out on it.”
  • “He kind of reminds me of you.” “Yeah, he’s like you if you had Orion’s enhancement! One hundred percent! Wait, ew, that’s kind of weird to think about.” That’s the title. Inazuma is a dark mirror of Kage: loud, provocative, will do anything to win, and blind to what his words do.
  • Orion: “Are you racist as well, Kage?” “It didn’t even sound like an interrogation, but Kage could feel Orion’s eyes piercing him.”
  • Kage: “I talk to you all the time, and I dated Ailani. Is that not enough evidence?” The classic defense. The book knows exactly what it’s doing.
  • Orion: “That isn’t evidence, Kage. You broke Ailani’s heart on two or more occasions, and you enjoy fighting me because you like to ‘prove you’re better.’” “Orion’s voice never rose, and somehow that made it worse.”
  • Ailani slaps Orion: “DON’T YOU BRING MY PAST RELATIONSHIP INTO THIS! WHAT IS WRONG WITH YOU, CRETINO?!”
  • Orion’s apology: “Maybe Inazuma is just getting to me. Cheyenne getting hurt on my advice isn’t helping either. I don’t take those kinds of comments lightly, and you defending him kind of made me want to oppose you as well, Kage.”
  • New in Draft 3: “He was the one Inazuma had called a ‘big black dolt,’ but now he was the one apologizing? Whatever calm he’d scraped together started slipping again.” The book sees the injustice clearly.
  • Kage: “Let them slide off you, okay?” It’s terrible advice from the boy whose power is making things stick.
  • “Orion didn’t answer. His hand balled into a fist at his side before he made it let go.” Hands again. Orion’s restraint is physical.
  • Kage: “You can take a minute if you need one.”
  • Kage: “You think Inazuma is like me. If that’s the case, then you know Inazuma would do anything to win.… He hates being slower than anyone.” Kage half-accepts the comparison.
  • Orion: “May the faster man win.” Inazuma slaps the hand away: “A speedster should never make things go slower.” Orion’s favorite thing in the world is walking slowly.
  • “Inazuma had a fear that Orion might be faster than him, but speed wasn’t how he planned on winning… His own words had caused clear friction in Orion’s mannerisms, and that—that made Inazuma grin.”

What it’s doing. It confronts the racism thread head-on and shows the cost to Orion of being the calm one.

What works. The book doesn’t let anyone off. Kage’s defense is weak and called weak. Orion’s comeback is cutting and also unfair to Ailani, and she calls that out. The new interior line for Orion is excellent. This is a thoughtful chapter about a hard subject, and it’s handled with more care than most YA fiction manages.

What I’d push on. The resolution leaves Orion apologizing and Kage giving bad advice, and the book knows that’s unfair. Make sure it comes back. Right now, Inazuma never apologizes, and Kage never fully acknowledges he was wrong to walk away. If the book means to return to it later (and I think it should), plant a small hook. If not, give Kage one moment of real accountability.

Score 7.5/10

3.6 Faster Than Fast

2,497 words. Arena: Orion vs. Inazuma.

What happens. Kage stops watching after the first exchange and plays with glue, telling Ailani that nobody is faster than Orion and that the only way Orion loses is doing something dumb, so she should watch over him. Ailani checks Kage’s forehead for “a fever” (really, for anything off). Orion and Inazuma circle at speed; Inazuma taunts that Kage “must not like you,” hits him with lightning, and says “Heroes don’t run from danger,” which freezes Orion with guilt about running from Kage that night. Orion attacks angrily; Inazuma whispers the slur again and electrocutes him, kicks him down, and says “When you end up a criminal, you’ll get used to that.” Orion catches his leg, low-blows him, and kicks him out, announcing his pro hero name: Hermes. Kage yells, “DUDE, THAT HERO NAME IS TERRIBLE! PICK ANYTHING ELSE!” The lobby turns on Orion for the low blow, Ailani defends him furiously, and Anubis arrives to say any method is fine: “Win by any means necessary.”

Details I caught.

  • “Both tested each other’s speed in a whirlwind of blue, gold, and violet.”
  • Kage “sat down and started playing with some glue in his hand.” “One, I’m working on something important.” He’s developing fire-stop caulk for Lucifer (revealed in 3.15). Later in the chapter he “shot a quick glance across the lobby at Lucifer before squishing it back into a ball.” The clue is planted.
  • “Inazuma is a character that causes stupidity out of others. He’s a rage-baiter.”
  • Ailani: “I want to know when you became such a high and mighty leader… you’re here delegating?”
  • Kage’s request: “Nobody is faster than Hermes. The only way he loses is if he does something dumb. You reel me in all the time; reel him in if you see something. I’m trusting you to make sure Orion is okay… I’m not the only one who went through something traumatizing that night. As perfect as he seems to be, I’m sure he’s still hurting. Help him for me, Ailani. Please.” Kage sees Orion’s pain more clearly than anyone, including Orion.
  • Ailani palms his forehead: “I’m checking for a fever. Everything you just said made sense, so I’m making sure you’re not sick or dying.” Really, she’s checking for anything wrong.
  • “Damn, you haven’t touched me this much since we were together.” “That’s your Inazuma showing!” Kage: “Your face looked so serious, so I felt you needed to smile. While you didn’t smile, a blush works just as good.”
  • “I’ll keep Orion in check while you play with your arts and crafts.” The title of 2.3, returned.
  • Inazuma: “You’ve been talking to Kage more than me for the past six months and you two still aren’t friends. He must not like you.”
  • Inazuma’s card: Lightning Fast. A “blaze of pure blue,” static from friction stored as lightning, “if he doesn’t expel the electricity, he can burn out and be useless!” “One misstep could send him hurtling in the wrong direction!”
  • “God, you’re annoying, Orion! You didn’t train for that speed! You just came out that fast, and that enhancement only made you faster. Heroes don’t run from danger.” That second sentence is the knife.
  • Orion freezes: “The speed at which he ran that night. Faster than ever before, and yet it was still not good enough. He used that newfound speed to then run away and fail another peer.” “His legs began to vibrate in a way he’d never really felt before. The feeling of wanting to run, but standing your ground anyway.” He looks for Kage in the stands. “Kage wasn’t there. ‘He must not like you.’ Only Ailani stood there looking back at him.”
  • Inazuma: “I was born with the name Sokudo, and you were born with the chance to try and reach what I was predestined to do!” He believes in his name.
  • The bolt “singed his chest and left a hole in his shirt, but it woke him up.”
  • “How’s this for a snowflake, huh?!” An uppercut, then body shots: “The strikes didn’t seem calculated the way Orion’s normally were. They were forceful, intense, emotional.”
  • Inazuma whispers: “You really are just a big black dolt.” Then grabs his arm and shocks him.
  • Kage, glancing up: “I can tell he’s doing something dumb, Ailani. Help him.” Ailani: “You can beat him, Orion! Show him how hard we trained!” “His only beacon of hope during these past few days of despair.”
  • Inazuma: “You can’t run if you’re stuck on the ground… It’s like having a gun to your head. When you end up a criminal, you’ll get used to that.” “A clear jab at Orion again, but this one with enough deniability that the crowd wouldn’t be able to call it the racist comment it was.” The book names it.
  • “Orion looked up at him with an almost Kage-esque smirk. ‘Your perception of me is off.’” He catches the leg: “And, your timing is predictable.” His catchphrase, split in half and turned into a weapon.
  • The low blow: “I hate to do this, Inazuma, but you deserve it.” “I felt you needed to learn a lesson on what you say to who.”
  • Inazuma: “Pro hero my ass! I bet you don’t even have a hero name!” Orion: “Oh, I have a hero name. I’m pro hero…Hermes.” He takes Kage’s nickname as his real name, in front of the world.
  • Kage runs to the window: “DUDE, THAT HERO NAME IS TERRIBLE! PICK ANYTHING ELSE!” Orion: “WHAT?! I LIKE IT!” Kage: “I DON’T CARE THAT YOU WON OR THAT YOU LIKE THAT NAME! I’M NOT YELLING THAT IN A CRISIS!” Word for word, Orion’s lines from the race (1.6), with the speakers swapped. It’s the best callback in the book.
  • Lucifer: “Yo, if you try low-blowing me I’ll beat your fucking ass. That wasn’t cool, dude.”
  • Kage: “it was tactical!… You think villains care about honor?” Elias: “So, you’re comparing Inazuma to a villain?” Kage: “I’ve been the one defending Inazuma the most.”
  • Orion’s apology: “He said it as calmly as he said everything, but he couldn’t tell if he meant a word of it.”
  • Lance: “That apology doesn’t do anything for Inazuma’s chances… a victory that is the antonym of clean.”
  • Ailani: “EVERYONE CÁLLENSE!” “Do any of you guys actually think Inazuma deserved to be in the Elite Program?!… He’s lucky we didn’t punch him in the face for his racist comments before!”
  • Anubis: “We purposefully have few rules in this tournament because a real fight has none. When lives are on the line, you do whatever you can… If I were the one who needed rescuing, I wouldn’t care if you needed to shoot the villain.… No one is pretentious enough to care how it’s done. Just get it done.” “Then treat every fight as someone stopping you from saving a person in need. Win by any means necessary.”
  • “Lance’s ‘Yes, sir’ came a beat late. His eyes weren’t on Anubis, though. They were on Kage, the one who’d started all this talk about villains not caring about honor.” Lance is watching Kage now.

What it’s doing. It’s Orion’s chapter: his guilt, his anger, his name. And it sets up Lance’s honor crusade.

What works. Orion’s freeze is the best interior moment he has in Section 3. The callback is perfect. Ailani’s “CÁLLENSE” is a great character beat, and Lance’s late “Yes, sir” is a great setup.

What I’d push on. The low blow is presented as justified, and the book lets Ailani and Anubis defend it. That’s fine, but it would add depth if Orion, privately, felt sick about it. He can’t tell if he meant his apology. Let him think about why. Does Inazuma’s slur make him want to be someone he doesn’t like? That’s the same question the book asks of Kage.

Score 8.5/10

3.7 Gone In An Instant

2,436 words. Arena: Rebento vs. Alexander; Ailani vs. Youseff.

What happens. Rebento, Cheyenne, and Lance gossip about whether Kage and Orion are really friends or just both close to Ailani. Alexander, an anxious boy with flower earrings, is distracted when his mother cheers from the front row, and Rebento ends the match instantly with an exploding gumball. Kage stares at the mother. Lance confronts Kage: his “win by any means” philosophy is spreading, and now no one can even turn to look at their parents. He asks Kage to fight honorably for the rest of the tournament. Kage apologizes. Lance says they’re cool when he sees Kage win with honor. Then Youseff Nasser, Anubis’s little brother, fakes tripping out of bounds the instant the match starts, and laughs at his furious brother.

Details I caught.

  • Rebento: “Finally, dude, I feel like I’ve been waiting for my match forever.” Cheyenne: “I know your weird way of consoling, Rebento.”
  • Rebento “popped a gumball in his mouth.”
  • Cheyenne: “You couldn’t drag me away from Fai with a tow truck.” “Want a cowgirl massage?”
  • Lance: “I…don’t feel right with this whole thing. Anubis just trampled on what I was saying. Should I talk to Kage?”
  • Cheyenne: “Is he friends with Orion, though? Didn’t Inazuma say they weren’t?… I think they’re just both close to Ailani.”
  • Rebento: “Ailani is Kage’s ex, bro. If I had an ex that hot, I’d introduce everybody as an excuse to talk to them. Maybe it’s just Kage using Orion as a shield to talk to Ailani. They aren’t friends. They just want the same thing.” He’s joking, and he’s accidentally right: both boys want Ailani.
  • Cheyenne: “We got together because I was friends with Rebento and he introduced us.” Rebento set up Lance and Cheyenne.
  • “She’s right, Lancey.”
  • Alexander: “His flower earrings sparkled… He moved his green hair out of the way of his eyes.” He never gets a power card. (See Theories for a guess.)
  • “You can do it, sweetie!!! I’m so proud of you for trying!” His mother, front row. The only unconditionally proud parent in the book. “Proud of you for trying,” not for winning.
  • “M-Mom?! What are you doing here?!”
  • Rebento “turned a crank on his side and opened a flap. He grabbed a gumball before throwing it up, catching it, and giving it a kiss. The gumball glowed.” Kiss, throw, boom.
  • “Heh, I had to give the crowd some fireworks!”
  • Rebento’s card: Sweet-Toothed Surprise. Glucose becomes “pure kinetic charge.” Candy becomes bombs. Rhatella built the gumball machines on his sides. “Rebento can’t charge living organisms—he tried to charge Lance once during an argument.” A perfect aside. Charging costs his own energy.
  • Anubis: “These people came here to see a fight, and everything keeps ending in seconds!” Huoyan: “I told you they were all heroic. I didn’t say they were all fighters.… I teach all heroes, not just ones who punch well.” “You can’t just have every one of your students be a pacifist!” “I can, actually. Pacifists make great rescue heroes.” This is the clearest statement of Huoyan’s philosophy, and it’s the opposite of Anubis’s.
  • Kage: “Oh, his mom cost him.” “His hands drifted towards the glass as his eyes stayed fixed on that front row.” An orphan watching a mother. Ailani sees it.
  • Lance: “Your dumb combat philosophy has now bled into my friends.… you made it so we can’t even risk turning to our parents for a moment.” Lance doesn’t know he’s saying this to an orphan who just stared at someone’s mother. “Kage’s eye twitched at Lance’s parent comment, but he tried to let it slide off him. The same thing he’d told Orion to do before.” And it doesn’t slide off.
  • Kage: “Orion, who’s not my friend, by the way, was the one who punched Inazuma.”
  • “We’re not classmates here, Lance. Just opponents trying to get to the next round.” Lance: “It figures you of all people would say something like that. You only just started talking to most of the people here this year!”
  • Lance’s challenge: “For the rest of the tournament, when you fight someone, fight them how you’d want to be fought. Treat them with respect and dignity instead of just trying to rush to the next round. Win not because you bend the rules, but because you can show the world you’re better than them.” “What if I end up against you?” “Then I’d want you to fight me the way I’m asking you to fight everyone else. That’s the whole point.”
  • Lance just walks away. “No yelling in Kage’s face. No jab at his ideals. It kinda threw Kage for a loop.” Kage is used to fights. He doesn’t know what to do with someone who simply leaves.
  • “I’m sorry! You’re…you’re not just an opponent to me! We cool?” A fist bump, refused: “We’re cool when I see you winning fights with honor. I don’t think you’re a bad person, but I can’t associate with you right now.”
  • Rebento “blew a bubble right in the middle of” Lance’s scolding. “Kage could’ve sworn Lance’s voice went up an octave.”
  • Orion: “Did you just apologize?” Ailani: “Maybe I should check for a fever again.”
  • Gene changed the order: “if I can’t have favoritism towards my daughter, then we can’t have favoritism towards your brother.” Huoyan: “She makes the rules, Khaled.”
  • Kage mistakes Volkan for Youseff. “He was the slow kid who Huoyan made run! Yeah, and then Huoyan yelled at us, but we didn’t get any punishment! Oh, good times.” Orion: “You are so off topic that it amazes me you’re tactical.”
  • Youseff: “a small brown-skinned boy just twiddling his thumbs.” “He’s like…five-three!” The crowd chants “Nasser, Nasser, Nasser!” and “Youseff looked like he couldn’t care less.” “He’s picking his nose out there!”
  • Kage: “He’s living what I want and he’s just chilling?!”
  • Anubis gives two thumbs up. “Youseff didn’t wave or anything.”
  • “OH NO! I’ve tripped and fallen out of bounds!” He flops onto the grass at “go.”
  • “Huoyan just replied with a smirk and a shrug.” Did Huoyan know? Did he encourage it?
  • Ailani: “That was a joke, right? I have no problem restarting the match with you.” Youseff, yawning: “Nah. There’s no joke here. I hope the Elite Program is fun for you. Because it sounds like torture to me.”
  • Ailani shoots one crystal at him. “He didn’t even try to dodge.” He laughs at Anubis “yelling behind the soundproof glass.”

What it’s doing. It’s the honor chapter. Lance throws down the challenge that shapes the rest of Kage’s tournament, and Youseff refuses the whole system.

What works. Lance’s confrontation is excellent, especially the ending: he walks away, and that’s what gets through. Huoyan’s pacifist line is the truest thing an adult says in the book. Youseff’s fake trip is the funniest single beat in the tournament.

What I’d push on. Alexander gets no card, no lines beyond “M-Mom?!”, and is gone. That’s thematically right (he never got a chance), but it means a classmate exists only as a punchline. He gets his moment in 3.16, which is the payoff. You might give him one more line here, something he wanted, so the 3.16 cheer means even more.

Score 8/10

3.8 Dinner Needs A Show

1,461 words. The hero lounge.

What happens. Anubis panics about the tournament’s ratings. Huoyan predicts Lucifer will forfeit against his girlfriend and Elias will beat Kage instantly. Anubis calls Lucifer up. Lucifer says he’ll fight Reina without his power, because fire makes her stronger. Anubis begs for a spectacle. Lucifer names his price: stop grooming Kage for the top. They make a deal: if Kage loses to Elias, Anubis cuts him off; if Kage beats Elias and then loses to Lucifer, Anubis cuts him off; if Kage beats both, Lucifer is “Huoyan’s problem” forever. Gene hears every word, and her whiskers twitch. Kelly returns from escorting Severine, who fought him all the way home.

Details I caught.

  • Anubis: “These people came here to see a fight… Fans are in the stands eating overpriced food, and they have nothing to enjoy before them!”
  • Huoyan: “This tournament was a terrible idea. You could always cancel it.” Anubis: “Are you kidding?! No, then we have to give refunds to everyone!”
  • Huoyan’s forecast: “Tulivalo won’t want to hurt his girlfriend. I wouldn’t either. He’ll probably forfeit. Minkowski is going to beat Ozul with ease… All he needs to do is touch him once.”
  • “Stop being right, Hon!”
  • “I’m not playing on my phone. I’m asking staff and personnel if there’s been any word of Neosame.” Anubis: “That’s not important right now!” The most damning thing Anubis says in the book.
  • “We need to make sure people don’t start trying to leave the arena and people don’t turn off their T.V.s at home!”
  • Lucifer assumes being called up means “a nice offer.”
  • Lucifer: “I plan on fighting Cruz without using my enhancement.” “If I use my enhancement on Cruz, my chances of beating her plummet.” (Draft 3 consistently has him say “Cruz.”) This sets up Reina’s card in 3.14: she absorbs heat.
  • “You said, ‘Win by any means necessary.’ It’s best if I win by not using my enhancement.” Lucifer uses Anubis’s own words against him.
  • Huoyan “smiled and opened his mouth to praise Lucifer’s tactics.” He’s genuinely proud.
  • Anubis: “I do still think that in ninety percent of cases, but these people came to see real fighting.”
  • “Behind him, his tail started to sway. It moved slowly and heavily, like it belonged to something much bigger than him.” That’s Abaddon, stirring. A brilliant, quiet detail.
  • “What’s in it for me?” Lucifer believes everything is a transaction (2.1: “I know nothing is free”).
  • “You stop grooming Kage for the top. Let him fall where he deserves to fall. Have him leave the school as the nobody he came in as.”
  • “Anubis fixed his collar. He smiled back at Lucifer. He was a fan of this negotiation.”
  • “If Kage beats Elias in a beautiful showcase to millions of people, then it’s kind of challenging to keep him out of the general public’s headspace… You can’t drag that kind of momentum down.”
  • Lucifer: “if I beat Cruz, he has to fight me. I can crush him no problem. If I destroy him like I know I will, then the win over Min looks like a complete fluke.”
  • The terms: Lucifer fights Reina with fire. If Kage loses to Elias, Anubis cuts him off “of all help going forward.” If Kage beats Elias, Anubis cuts him off when Lucifer beats him. Anubis adds: “If Kage manages to beat both of you, then I’m never considering you as a successor again. You’ll officially be Huoyan’s problem. I will make sure Kage ends up a top hero.”
  • Lucifer: “No tips on how to beat me or Min, and no pep talks either… I want to see him struggle hard in front of all these people.” Anubis: “That’s fine. Sticky Situation doesn’t need my help.”
  • Huoyan tries: “Khaled…” Cut off.
  • “[Gene’s] smile stayed bright, but her whiskers gave one sharp twitch, and she didn’t look at Anubis once.” She heard everything, and she’s angry. She acts on it in 3.11 and 3.14.
  • Kelly passed Lucifer in the hall: “the kid had been grinning like he’d already won the whole tournament.”
  • “Geez, that Neant girl fought me all the way back to her dorm.” Huoyan: “Is she alright?” He asks first.
  • “I had to use my enhancement on her cuz she wanted to start a full-on fight with me.” Kelly’s power is never shown. His surname is Irish for “a loan” or “borrowing.” (See Theories.)
  • “Hope we saved the best for last.” Volkan vs. Lance.

What it’s doing. It’s the backroom deal: the adults turn Kage’s future into a wager for ratings.

What works. It’s a tight, nasty little scene. Lucifer’s tail is a perfect detail. Gene’s whiskers are a perfect detail. Anubis’s “That’s not important right now!” is the line that defines him.

What I’d push on. The deal turns on Anubis “cutting off all help,” but we’ve never actually seen Anubis help Kage with anything concrete. What exactly would Kage lose? A place in Anubis’s agency? A recommendation into the Elite Program? The successor track? Naming one specific thing would raise the stakes of every Kage fight that follows. And Huoyan watches one of his students get gambled away and manages only “Khaled…” before his phone buzzes. The Abby text explains his distraction, but given the handprints in 2.9, a reader will want him to push back harder. Even one sharp line would do it.

Score 8/10

3.9 A Lighter Crown

1,531 words. The student lobby; Lance vs. Volkan begins.

What happens. Rebento and Cheyenne tell Kage they still like him. Cheyenne asks if he really thinks he’ll be King, and Ailani and Orion, learning about the King speech for the first time, can’t stop laughing. Cheyenne recites the speech back to him word for word and says it made her believe. Kage feels like that speech was the last time he told the full truth. Cheyenne asks about the whistle, and Orion steps between them: no one can know about Abby until after the tournament. Rebento squares up to Orion. Kage and Cheyenne slip away. She tells him to “take the time to enjoy being a prince.” Lance draws a beam rapier against the hooded Volkan.

Details I caught.

  • Lance looks at the trailing Volkan “with a bit of concern… his size was intimidating.”
  • Kage: “You guys are cool associating with me?” He’s become aware of his reputation in the room.
  • Rebento: “Since we met you properly, you’ve been pretty cool. Even if you got Lance to yell at me.”
  • Cheyenne: “when I met him he was a bit of an asshole. He was rude to me on the field, but then I saw the way he felt about himself.”
  • “You really think you’re going to be King?”
  • Ailani: “Wait, you said that? I thought you said the king thing with Zulfiya was nothing?” In 2.3 he told her “Oh, that’s nothing.”
  • “WAIT! YOU SAID THAT TO HUOYAN?!… DID HUOYAN BOW?!” Orion and Rebento crack up too.
  • Cheyenne doesn’t laugh. “‘People will call me King Kage one day. That’s not arrogance, by the way. It’s a spoiler.’ That’s what you said, right?” It’s a word-for-word quote of 1.11 (corrected in Draft 3). “When I met you, I thought you were a total dickhead who paid no mind to what anybody says, but that speech—I don’t know, dude. There was something there that made me really believe. Not as hard as Zulfiya. I’m not calling you anything other than Partner.”
  • The key interior line: “It felt like that speech was the last time he’d ever told the full truth, and he was unsure if he even believed that truth anymore.” Everything since then has been secrets and lies.
  • New in Draft 3: “He clutched Abby’s whistle, then caught himself and tried to tuck it back under his collar, but Cheyenne clocked it quickly.”
  • Cheyenne: “Is that whistle from them? That…person you said you wanted to show you were a real hero?” She thinks it belongs to “that soul up above.” She’s half-right. It belongs to the second person he failed.
  • “Orion’s laughter cut off the instant he heard Cheyenne’s question.”
  • Kage “wanted to be truthful with Cheyenne, considering how open she was being.” He almost tells her. Then Orion steps in front of him.
  • Orion: “Hey, could you relax with all the probing?” Whispering to Kage: “People can’t know about Abby yet. After the tournament, Kage.” Orion protects Kage’s secret because it’s tangled with his own.
  • Cheyenne: “you’re the type of person that Lance can’t help but gravitate towards, I reckon.”
  • Rebento: “Yo, bro! Who do you think you’re getting big with?… I’ll explode you here if you talk like that to her again.” Orion: “You’d have to catch me first. You’re such a bluff, Rebento.” Rebento: “If our half goes the same as the first, that means me and you will square off!” (They do, in 4.4–4.5.)
  • Ailani stays to watch Orion, because “The last time Orion got into it with somebody today, it ended with a low blow in front of the whole stadium.”
  • Kage steals Cheyenne’s hat and wears it: “Don’t worry, Partner! I’m sure our boys will kiss and make up.” Kage is now calling her Partner too. Cheyenne: “Nice try, Partner. Only one of us can pull this off.” (In 4.4, the wager: if Kage loses the grand final, he has to wear this hat on the podium.)
  • The title: “Take that whole ‘King thing’ slow. You don’t want to overwork yourself like you did that day. You didn’t see it, but Huoyan was super worried. You can take the time to enjoy being a prince, alright?” Kage: “You got it. Prince Kage for now.” A lighter crown. It’s a lovely act of care: she lets him keep the dream and lowers the stakes.
  • Volkan “hid deeper in his hoodie.” “He just wanted to last long enough for them to remember his name.”
  • Lance throws a hilt into the air: “A show was asked of me, and a show I shall give!” A yellow beam forms a rapier.
  • Lance’s card: Avalon’s Legacy. Any blade “as a beam of yellow energy!” It “cuts right through almost anything rigid like it isn’t even there! But flesh doesn’t get cut! It moves with the blade instead of parting for it, so a direct hit lands like a searing-hot strike rather than a slice!” Rhatella built the hilt that keeps his hands from burning. “There seems to be a limit to Lance, a point where he decides that he needs to stop even when his hilt and hands are fine, but people aren’t quite sure why!” That limit is revealed in 4.7: “King Arthur.”

What it’s doing. It deepens Kage’s guilt about his lies, gives him a new title (Prince), and sets up Lance’s fight.

What works. Cheyenne is wonderful here. Her word-perfect recitation is a gift, and “enjoy being a prince” is one of the kindest things anyone says to Kage. The line about the speech being “the last time he’d ever told the full truth” is one of the best interior lines in the book.

What I’d push on. Orion’s intervention reads as aggressive (“Hey, could you relax with all the probing?”) toward a girl who was being kind. That’s the point (he’s protecting a secret), and Rebento calls it out. But it would help to see Orion register that he overstepped, even just a flicker of guilt as Cheyenne walks away.

Score 8/10

3.10 Honoring A Snail’s Pace

2,007 words. Arena: Lance vs. Volkan.

What happens. Lance’s rapier clanks off Volkan, who rolls into him. Volkan throws off his hoodie: he’s built like a scaly-foot snail, with an iron shell and iron scales. Lance switches to a greatsword and bats him with the flat, sending him rolling toward the edge. Then Lance runs ahead and stops him with his bare hands, saving him from a ring-out. He offers Volkan a deal: land one hit, and Lance will walk out. Volkan wants to earn it. Lance explains he can’t fight him at full strength because his blade would cut Volkan’s armored body and kill him. Volkan tries to give up. Lance knights him instead, with an arming sword: “Sir Salyangozu.” The whole stadium chants “Knight Volkan.” Volkan mouths “thank you” and rolls out.

Details I caught.

  • “instead of a smack, there was a loud clank.”
  • “Volkan’s massive body promptly rolled before slamming into Lance.” Lance “drove his sword into the ground,” and it “slowly sawed through the arena floor.” That “sawed” matters. In 4.7, we learn his blades work like a cast saw.
  • “My sword isn’t supposed to do that against skin. What the hell is under that hood?”
  • Volkan’s card: Volcano Snail. “Born with similar characteristics to a scaly-foot gastropod!” That’s a real animal: a deep-sea snail found at hydrothermal vents, whose shell and foot scales are plated with iron sulfide. The card gets it right: “a massive iron shell on his back,” “armored in iron scales,” it can “handle high temperatures extremely well because scaly-foot gastropods live in hydrothermal vent fields,” and “he also has gills.” “He may be slow to move, but he tries to think fast!” And his name is Turkish for “volcano snail.” The joke in 1.7 (he’s late, Huoyan makes him “count your minutes”) was hiding in his name all along.
  • “A rapier isn’t going to do it here. I need a bigger weapon for a man of your stature!” A greatsword. He hits “with the broad of the sword.”
  • Anubis, shaking Gene: “No, no! Oh, we can’t have another match end in seconds!”
  • “Kelly looked up at the two with an incredibly warm smile. He loved the way this whole tournament was crumbling.”
  • Huoyan: “Good job not forcing your kid to compete. This whole thing is a shit show.” Kelly: “Woah, did you just curse?” “No, I feel like it’s a waste of time. That’s the shit show part.”
  • Kelly sees Huoyan’s phone: “It was thread after thread of messages to staff, all asking about Abby. Huoyan had found…nothing… God forbid, but Huoyan was so desperate by now that Kelly could tell he’d at least be glad to find her body.” A devastating sentence about a teacher.
  • Kelly takes the phone: “constantly refreshing your phone over and over isn’t going to make the info appear any faster.” “Why don’t you sit down and watch this whole tournament burn with me?” Kelly is kind to Huoyan.
  • Huoyan: “I could never get through to that kid, and now he’s just going to roll to a loss.” He’s talking about Volkan. He cares about the kid he punished.
  • “For once, Huoyan didn’t reach for it. His eyes stayed on Volkan.”
  • “Volkan could feel himself almost start to cry in his shell. He had given up.”
  • Then he stops. “Once Volkan poked his head out, he saw gray hair flowing. Lance had a smile on his face with his hand on the shell.”
  • “Volkan, we’re classmates! Just call me Lance! Now, on your feet, or, well, foot!” A snail joke, made kindly.
  • Gene: “What a beautiful display of sportsmanship from Lance Faisceau!”
  • Cheyenne: “Woohoo! That’s my boyfriend! You see that, Partner?!” Kage: “If that’s what he calls honor, then I’m screwed. I’m not saving him from a loss if we end up fighting.”
  • “Lance’s legs looked shaky, and his boots were heavily dug into the ground.” Volkan: “You stopped me yourself?!… I’m heavy, dude!” Lance stopped “a hurtling five-hundred-pound mass” with his body. He’s strong without his power.
  • “If you manage to get one hit on me, then I’ll walk myself out of bounds.”
  • Cheyenne: “He always does this dumb stuff!”
  • Kage: “Let him be… I want to see more of this ‘honor’ he claims. Cuz this just looks dumb to me so far.”
  • Anubis: “Ah, so you do know how to teach your kids some showmanship, Hon!” Huoyan: “I didn’t teach him that, and you know I’d never teach them to go for showing off over handling the issue. But, he isn’t doing it in a bad manner, I suppose.”
  • Volkan: “No, no, no! That’s too unfair to you! I want to earn it! I’m not just the slow kid!”
  • The rule, used perfectly: “if I did, I’d seriously injure you. Most of your body is completely hard. If I actually went all out against you, my sword could slice through you and kill you. Why do you think I switched to a greatsword? It was for its mass, not its sharpness.” Lance’s blades cut rigid things and spare flesh. That makes them safe against nearly everyone, and lethal against the one classmate whose body is armor. It’s a beautiful use of a power’s own rule.
  • “Then, I want to give up. You deserve to go to the Elite Program over me. I’m just a snail…”
  • “Sir Salyangozu.” The arming sword on his left shoulder: “To me, you are much more than a snail.” On the right: “You are incredibly armored. I feel the term ‘snail’ is wrong for you. A more accurate term for a man covered in armor would be…” Above his head: “A knight!”
  • “He is Knight Volkan Salyangozu! Let’s go, people! Knight Volkan, Knight Volkan, Knight Volkan!” The crowd joins.
  • “The new knight felt like he was glowing on the field. He’d never had this much validation in his life. All he’d wanted was to last long enough for them to remember his name. Now the whole stadium couldn’t stop shouting it.”
  • He mouths “thank you” and rolls out.
  • Kage: “Lance better never try knighting me. I’m not a knight; I was crowned prince by you.” Cheyenne punches him: “if Lance can knight people, wouldn’t that make him a king? That’s better than a prince, Partner.” “Maybe I’ll start calling Lance king too! Then, the real king will be whoever everyone else here thinks is king. Cuz right now, it’s an even one follower each.” (Zulfiya for Kage, Cheyenne for Lance.) This is funnier and darker after 4.7, where Lance’s monster is named “King Arthur.”
  • Huoyan “smiled softly at the knighting ceremony.”
  • Anubis: “The matches before this were all just palate cleansers! Now, the Elite Tournament has officially hit the airwaves!”
  • Kelly: “Yeah, now the only people left are the pawns that put on a good show for you, huh, Anubis?” “Pawns” is Frostbite’s word for Kage (1.13). Heroes and villains use the same word for children.

What it’s doing. It’s the honor chapter’s demonstration: Lance shows Kage what he meant.

What works. It’s the best feel-good scene in the book. The knighting is earned, funny, and moving. And the power logic (a blade that spares flesh but cuts armor) is used to make kindness necessary, not just optional. Huoyan quietly caring about Volkan is a lovely touch.

What I’d push on. Kage’s reaction (“this just looks dumb to me”) is right for him, but he never gets a moment where Lance’s display actually lands, until 3.13 in the hotel (“What you did to Volkan was nice too… I didn’t really understand how good what you did was until later”). Consider giving Kage a flicker here, a glance at Volkan glowing, so the later admission has a seed.

Score 8.5/10

3.11 Studying Is Important

3,795 words. Arena: Kage vs. Elias. One of the two best fights in the book.

What happens. Gene, who hated Anubis’s deal, swaps the order: Kage vs. Elias is now first. Huoyan and Kelly physically stop Anubis from going down to coach Kage. Kage’s real plan isn’t to win; it’s to hide the biomass, which flares with injury and high heart rate. So he stays calm. He charges at “one” to feel Elias’s power, and Elias freezes him and turns to the crowd for a speech: Kage doesn’t deserve to be here, came to hero school for food, and is “a nobody… that’s what he chose to be.” The crowd boos. Kage doesn’t struggle. Elias’s three-minute clock barely moves, because nothing pushes back. Released, Kage sticks to the wall instead of falling out (“I’m sticky, Elias!”), yanks off Elias’s glasses, hesitates at Rhatella’s face stamp, and breaks them. He overloads Elias’s holds with tape, glues himself to Elias so he can’t be launched alone, and slams him. Frozen again, Kage explains Elias’s rule (vital processes continue) and that his power is excretion, “super similar to sweating,” which counts. He buries Elias in a tidal wave of glue. Launched by the banked force, he catches the dome on a thin strip of tape, falls back in bounds, and yells at Orion, “I remembered to eat breakfast!” Elias yields.

Details I caught.

  • Gene: “Did you forget this is MY tournament?”
  • Huoyan: “It was hard to pay attention with how much you were quivering.”
  • Gene announces Elias vs. Kage first. Anubis: “NO NO NO GENE THAT’S NOT RIGHT!” Gene: “Welp, it’s too late. I already made the announcement.”
  • Gene’s reason: “I absolutely hated the deal he made with Lucifer. If the tournament goes bad, it goes bad. Will I be sad? Of course, but I’m not going to gamble away a kid’s future to try and save a possibly burning ship. On the bright side, Lance had a fire extinguisher.” Huoyan: “Wait, but you’re gambling with Kage’s future now. What makes this different?” She doesn’t answer. “Sticking it to Anubis is fun…I need to do it more often with you guys.”
  • Anubis tries to go coach Kage. Huoyan and Kelly block him: “I may not agree with Lucifer, but I defend my students. You’re not gonna do something unfair to him. A deal’s a deal.” Huoyan protects Lucifer too.
  • “WHAT’S THAT OVER THERE?!” Anubis fakes a distraction and gets caught. “So, he went limp in their arms and was dragged back.” Physical comedy for the number one hero.
  • Ailani: “You’re shaking, Kage…” “a bit frustrated he keeps pushing her away.” (A tense slip: “kept.”)
  • Cheyenne: “I was just frozen and everything went by so fast. Sorry, Kage. You’re gonna have to be alone on this one.”
  • Kage’s real plan: “His plan wasn’t to beat Elias right now… the more important thing was to make sure he stayed concealed. He knew his biomass flared up during injury, and during cases of high heart rates. So, in order to fight Elias without being exposed, he needs to go all in on tranquility.” (Tense slip: “needed.”) The secret becomes the fight’s constraint. That’s excellent design: the fight is harder because of what he’s hiding.
  • “Oh, Cheyenne! I almost forgot something! Make sure Lance watches this one!” He’s going to fight honorably, and he wants Lance to see.
  • Lucifer: “That’s not the order. I’m supposed to be fighting Reina right now.” He’s nervous about the deal. He lies to Elias (“I just think it’s not right when I was so ready to go”). Elias clocks it and sets it aside.
  • “There was no repartee between Elias and Kage, just the slow countdown.”
  • Kage charges at “one” on purpose: “He wanted to learn more about Elias’s enhancement, so what better way than to feel it directly.”
  • Elias doesn’t attack. He turns to the crowd: “I’m not an egotistical person, ladies and gentlemen… I’m not doing this to embarrass Mr. Ozul here. Well, the second half is partially a lie, but I feel he needs some reflection.”
  • Huoyan, patting Anubis’s back: “Nothing you can do, Khaled. The math wasn’t adding up.”
  • Kage “knew if he did that then the biomass could feel threatened.”
  • Elias’s speech: “This man does not deserve to be here! He spent the entirety of the previous year treating our hero education as if it were cutting into his ‘sedentary life’ of being lazy… This man went to a hero school for food instead of the goodness in his heart.” That’s true, according to the Prologue. Elias is cruel and accurate.
  • Elias’s list of the thirteen classmates he’s studied: Orion, Ailani, Reina, Lucifer, Lance, Inazuma, Rebento, Cheyenne, Volkan, Geneki, Severine, Zulfiya, and Alexander. Missing: Kage, Abby, Rhatella, and Youseff. Huoyan: “Heh, he didn’t say your brother’s name.” Elias studied everyone who might matter, and decided Youseff doesn’t. (Or decided Youseff isn’t worth studying because he never tries.)
  • Alex’s mother is “excited that somebody would be studying him.”
  • “Only a minute and fifteen seconds had passed on Elias’s three minute clock, but that was outside Kage’s knowledge.” Because Kage isn’t struggling, the clock barely burns. That’s exactly the rule from Elias’s card.
  • “Why should I, one of the top students at this prestigious program, ever take the time to care about someone who barely even cares about himself?… Kage Ozul? He’s a nobody, and I mean that as in that’s what he chose to be!… One of his friends forfeited their future for him! Now, he’s even convincing others of the lie he tells himself! Nobody ‘deserves’ to be the number one hero! They are meant to earn it… Most people look at a car crash as they drive by, but what do they do after? They drive off.”
  • “Kage’s whole body was screaming at him to do something. Yell, grab the whistle, anything.”
  • Elias notices his speech “running longer than the three minutes he’d allotted himself, but Kage was still frozen.” He doesn’t understand why. Kage isn’t struggling, so the clock isn’t burning.
  • “When I return to that lobby, tell me and the audience here what your insignificant power is. I hope your fifteen minutes of fame were fun. They end right now.”
  • Kage hits the wall and sticks. “I’ll tell you my power right now.… Hah, hah! Woo! I’m sticky, Elias!”
  • The tape snags Elias’s glasses. “He went to snap them, but he noticed a small symbol of Rhatella’s face on the temple of them. There was a moment of hesitation, but he chose to break them in half anyway.” This is the decision Lance grills him about that night.
  • Anubis: “DID YOU SEE THAT?! He didn’t lose yet!”
  • Kelly to Huoyan: “Seems like you like Anubis’s main prospect, huh?” Huoyan ignores him.
  • “I almost didn’t…stick…that landing.”
  • Elias: “Your jokes are idiotic. On top of that, I don’t need those to see. They just help me calculate things faster.”
  • The tape barrage: Kage “was trying to win through overloading Elias. Elias gave him what he thought was the answer.”
  • Elias “knew which pieces of tape he let go of.” He ducks the trap.
  • The glue grab: “he covered his hand in glue, and at the same time of Elias touching him, he grabbed ahold of Elias’s arm.” “Heh, so you know the ‘Sticky Situation’ you’re stuck in now. If you strike me and I get launched, you’re coming with me.” If Elias banks force on Kage, Kage takes Elias with him. Elias has to release.
  • Kage slams him, lands one punch, and backs off: “he didn’t want to overexert himself for the biomass to try and help.”
  • Lucifer, angry for the deal. Reina: “Min got this! Right?” Lucifer: “He better.”
  • Elias drops the holds, dodges only, then yanks the loose strips still attached to Kage, and pounces. “You’re…done, Ozul!”
  • Kage’s deduction: “This is the third time I’ve been held down by you, Elias. I get your power now. A person’s normal internal processes need to keep going. Otherwise, you’d end up killing anyone you freeze. That’s how Cheyenne and I are able to talk while under your control.”
  • “The thing about me is…my power is weird. I don’t need to contract my muscles a certain way or really do any sort of motion at all. It’s basically just excretion. Y’know, super similar to sweating. That’s a natural internal process. You don’t know my power? That’s unfortunate, but explains your naive run in.” That’s Elias’s line to Cheyenne in 3.4, returned with interest.
  • “Elias’s hand rose to adjust his glasses only to find nothing.” His tic, and the glasses are gone. Perfect.
  • “An absolute tidal wave of glue erupted from Kage.” “You quit?” “No, I don’t.”
  • Elias: “you had to have spent all your reserves getting me stuck… You can’t save yourself anymore, Kage.”
  • The launch. “A small thin strip of tape came from his index finger and stuck to the top of the dome.” He swings, it snaps, and the momentum carries him “barely” back in bounds.
  • The crowd cheers his effort.
  • “You seriously had a little left in the tank after an attack like that? How is that possible?” Kage, to Orion’s window: “I remembered to eat breakfast!” A payoff of Orion’s “eat breakfast” from the race (1.6), more than 2,500 lines later. “Orion couldn’t help himself from smiling. Ailani turned to Orion as well before hugging him.”
  • “So, you quit?” with a finger gun. “I yield, Kage Ozul.”
  • Lucifer punches the wall: “DAMMIT! THAT FUCKING ANT IS UNBELIEVABLE!”
  • “Kage didn’t bask in the crowd cheering like he wanted to… in his mind, the biomass survived unexposed. That was his top priority.” The boy who wanted the crowd “forever” can’t enjoy it now. “In his mind” is a warning: Elias noticed more than Kage thinks.

What it’s doing. It’s the fight that proves Kage can win with brains, honorably, while carrying a secret.

What works. Everything. The fight uses every rule from Elias’s card (banked force, the clock, vital processes, the glasses). It makes Kage’s secret part of the strategy. It gives Elias a cruel, articulate, mostly true speech that the fight then answers. And it ends on the best callback in Section 3: “I remembered to eat breakfast!”

What I’d push on. Two tense slips (“keeps,” “needs”) early in the chapter, and the narration in Kage’s plan paragraph switches to present tense. Easy fixes. And Gene’s logic for switching the order still doesn’t quite track, as Huoyan points out. If that’s intentional (she’s more mischievous than principled), lean into it with one line of her admitting she just wanted to see Anubis sweat.

Score 9.5/10

3.12 The Whole Truth

2,045 words. After the fight; the bus; the hotel.

What happens. The day’s matches are over. Orion wants Ailani to room with Kage to spy; she refuses and rooms with Orion, to keep an eye on him instead. Kage cleans the glue off Elias, offers him a hand, and says he’d like to be friends. Elias refuses: Kage didn’t struggle in his holds when he clearly wanted to, which means he’s either illogical or hiding something, “and I believe the answer is both.” Kage runs to the bus and calls dibs on rooming with Lance. On the bus, Elias pulls Lucifer’s horn and asks what he did; Lucifer won’t say. Reina realizes both boys are keeping something from her. At the hotel, Kage eats an entire minifridge.

Details I caught.

  • Gene: “Each hotel room only accommodates two people, so choose who you hang out with tonight wisely! It might be a viable strategy to spend the night with a future opponent so you make sure they’re not plotting against you!”
  • Orion: “you should spend the night with Kage then. That way we can keep an eye on him.” Ailani: “Absolutely not. No, we need to just relax tonight. We can go back to your whole spy thriller tomorrow… I’m spending the night with you.” “Ailani said, ending the talk with something funny to distract him from the full reason she wanted to spend the night with him.” Her real reason: she’s worried about Orion now.
  • Orion’s “face reddened at Ailani’s proclamation that they were spending the night with one another.”
  • “Let’s get moving. I wanna see if the hotel beds are soft!” She grabs his hand.
  • Kage cleans the glue off Elias and offers him a hand up. Elias: “You are tactically more in tune with yourself than I gave you credit for.” “Is that your way of saying you like me?” Elias’s deadpan “killed the vibe.”
  • Kage’s offer: “the Kage you were criticizing doesn’t exist anymore. I know Zulfiya forfeited for me today, but I didn’t want that. I am not just the kid in the back you can step on. I’m Sticky Situation… the man I’m going to room with tonight is trying to show me how to be more respectful… we’re on the same side. I’d really like us to end up friends one day.”
  • Elias’s refusal: “I cannot be partners with someone I cannot trust.” (“Partners” again: partner vs. pawn.) “Why not struggle out of my holds?… someone as flamboyant as you doesn’t seem like the type to do that… it seemed like a part of you wanted to struggle and you had to actively fight against that urge.… That conflict is internal, not external. By all logic, everything you did has no validity or reason behind it. So, either I am unable to trust you because you’re illogical, or I’m unable to trust you because you’re hiding something. The worst part is, I believe the answer is both.” Elias is the book’s detective, and he’s exactly right.
  • “He couldn’t risk the goosebumps he was feeling on his body go outward to words in his mouth.” Kage is afraid of his own temper now.
  • “Well, I’m sorry I broke your glasses. I think I would’ve lost if I didn’t.” Elias: “Sure, that was the most logical thing you did all battle.” A softer scoff.
  • “I CALL DIBS ON SPENDING THE NIGHT WITH LANCE!” Rebento: “HAH! I THOUGHT I WAS GONNA HAVE TO LOOK FOR A RANDOM TONIGHT!… you spend yours with your new boyfriend, Lancey!”
  • Cheyenne: “He’s cool, hun… Plus, you know I’d punch Rebento in the jaw if he got all handsy.” To Kage: “if you get all touchy-feely on Fai here, I’ll have you six feet under in less than two shakes of a lamb’s tail.” Kage: “I have no idea what that means!”
  • Lance: “Can you please wait to talk to me till we get to the hotel?”
  • Elias grabs one of Lucifer’s horns: “What did you do?” Lucifer: “I didn’t do shit, Min. So, you can let go now, or I’ll burn this whole bus.”
  • Elias: “By the way, I’m sorry I lost.… My loss could’ve made it so I don’t end up in the Elite Program. I know we all wanted to go as a group, so I apologize.” Section 4 explains why he needs to be in the program with Lucifer: to keep watching him.
  • Reina: “Whether we get in the Elite Program or not, we’ll still have each other! That’s what’s important!”
  • “Reina had to look at two boys who were both treating her like she didn’t deserve the whole truth.” The title. Reina is the one person in the trio who doesn’t know about Abaddon. She finds out on live TV.
  • The rooms: Alexander with Elias (“Elias’s social negligence and Alex’s social awkwardness fused together in a single night of embarrassed quiet”); Reina with Lucifer; Zulfiya with Volkan (distant); Cheyenne with Rebento (joking); Ailani with Orion; Kage with Lance. Alex’s mother “forced him to join them so he could make some new friends.” He picked Elias “since he spoke about him at the tournament.”
  • Ailani’s worry has shifted: “After the Elias fight, her worry for Kage diminished a bit, and her worry for Orion only climbed after the day they had. She knew Inazuma had gotten in his head in a way that she hadn’t seen before.”
  • The hotel: “a massive flatscreen, and both the beds were king sized.” Of course they are.
  • The minifridge: “Every soda, candy bar, chocolate milk, all of it.” “slowly gulped down the large hunk of cheese.” “I have to stock up on food for tomorrow.” Lipids. He “unplugged the fridge from the wall and put it next to his bed.” (There’s a second minifridge gag in 4.2, when Kelly kicks one over to him.)
  • “an orange slice wedged in his mouth like a grin.” “I’m so glad you asked, Sir Lance.”

What it’s doing. It sets up the night’s conversations and shows that two people (Elias and, in a different way, Ailani) are now actively watching.

What works. Elias’s refusal is a superb piece of deduction. Reina’s line about “the whole truth” is quiet and sharp. The fridge is funny and true to the power.

What I’d push on. The room-assignment paragraph is a bit of a list. It’s useful information, but it could be broken up with one or two beats (a glance between Zulfiya and Volkan, a line from Reina to Lucifer) so it reads as story instead of logistics.

Score 8/10

3.13 A Kings’ Quarrel

2,046 words. The hotel room, Saturday night.

What happens. Kage demands to know whether Lance thought his fight was honorable. Lance: entertaining, but not quite, because Kage destroyed Elias’s glasses. Kage argues it was smart and says he fought with a disadvantage (he can’t say what it is, so he half-lies). He grabs Lance’s Rhatella-made hilt from his bag and explains that Rhatella is lonely and that he, one of her few friends, smashed her work in front of millions. Lance: “Okay, Anubis.” You only cared how you looked. Kage asks what’s wrong with being like Anubis. Lance asks why he wants to be number one at all. Both want to make up for wrongs. Lance reveals his father was a villain in Quebec who taught him to steal, and that one day, saving some ants from a frog, he realized he’d always hated it. He holds others to the standard he didn’t hold himself to. He says he could look up to Kage. Kage admits he’s bad at saving people and wants to save everyone. They make promises: Kage won’t break anyone’s gear and will fight honorably; Lance will try to stop deciding who deserves what.

Details I caught.

  • “So enlighten me, your kingship!… Eh, what’s the right word an old-timey guy like you would say. Indubitable!” Lance: “That’s not even correct.”
  • “It was certainly entertaining, but honorable? Not quite.” Kage “shoved a chocolate bar in his mouth in anger.” Then a banana. He’s stress-eating his lipids.
  • “You destroyed your opponent’s gear without a second thought, Ozul.” “Oh, now I’m Ozul?”
  • “I had a second thought, but it had nothing to do with Elias.” The second thought was Rhatella.
  • “You beat your opponent below their best. You took advantage of him by taking away the thing that makes him better than you.”
  • “Because he’s your classmate? He’s not some random villain.”
  • Kage: “Anubis said so himself to fight as if you’re meant to save someone!”
  • “What disadvantage did you have?” “The question hit Kage like Lance was using one of his swords.” He can’t say “a biomass I was hiding.” So: “I was the only one who fought a semi-final that hadn’t used their power all day.” (Sharper in Draft 3.) Lance: “Tch, what you need to warm up? Be serious, Kage.”
  • Kage grabs Lance’s hilt from his bag and shows the Rhatella emblem.
  • Lance: “She just handed me the hilt. I went to thank her, but she said there’s no need and ran off.”
  • Kage: “That’s cuz she’s scared to talk to you! Rhatella is a very sweet girl, and she’s super lonely. She’s socially awkward, but she tries to be understanding. I told her why I wanted my hero name and she just understood. The only thing she loves doing is creating gadgets for people because that’s the only way she feels close to them. I’m probably one of two or three people she can call her friends. How do I look, one of her only friends, smashing her work in front of millions of people?” That’s a beautiful, accurate portrait of Rhatella. And Kage, who barely saw her after 1.9, understood her completely.
  • “Okay, Anubis.” Two words that hit harder than any punch in the tournament.
  • “You only cared about how you looked. You knew it would hurt Rhatella and you chose to do it anyway.”
  • Kage, quietly: “What’s wrong with being like Anubis? I want to be number one. He’s number one for a reason.”
  • Lance: “Why the hell do you want to be number one anyway? He’s not the guy who saves the most people.” That sentence is the book’s thesis, in the mouth of its most principled character.
  • “I want to be number one because it’s the only way to make up for the things I’ve done. You wouldn’t get it, dude.” “Try me.”
  • “Same as you. I gotta make up for the wrongs I’ve done.”
  • “Kage felt awkward being the only one sitting, so he laid down as well.” They talk lying side by side, staring at the ceiling.
  • “My dad was a villain.”
  • “a high earning villain in Canada. When I was younger in Quebec, my dad would take me out to teach me how to do the things he did. He taught me how to steal, how to do heists, best ways to escape, breaking and entering without a trace.” Faisceau is a French surname, and Quebec is French-speaking. That fits.
  • “For a while, I was blinded by the fact my dad enjoyed teaching me stuff. It was the only time he spent with me when he wasn’t working.”
  • “A part of Kage wanted to selfishly interrupt. He was curious what that feeling felt like, but he didn’t want to be rude.” An orphan wondering what it feels like to have a father proud of you, even a criminal one.
  • “He got arrested. At first, I was torn apart. I cried daily. I would tell people he was a good man even though the facts were right in front of me.”
  • The ants: “I was looking at some ants on the ground, and a frog was approaching when they were just trying to get to their anthill. I walked over and turned the frog another way, towards the pond. That alone showed me how much I hated every second he tried to get me to do villainous things. Because I had saved those ants without even thinking about it.” Lucifer and Abaddon call Kage “ant,” “bug,” “termite,” “cockroach.” Lance’s origin is saving ants. The book’s insect motif inverts here: the small creatures the powerful want to step on are exactly who a hero is for.
  • “I try to hold people to the standard I didn’t hold to myself. Look, I’m sorry if my expectations for you are too high.”
  • And there’s a mirror Lance doesn’t know about. His villain father made crime the only time they bonded. Kage’s villain “mentors” pat his head and speak “like a father talking to his child.” Lance escaped what Kage is walking into.
  • “I just know what happens when you start taking shortcuts. It never ends well, and someone like you, who I see a lot of good in, would be better off trying to win against the odds instead of turning them in your favor.”
  • “You…see yourself looking up to me?” “In a way, I guess, yeah. The whole king persona was obnoxious when I heard it at first. But, Cheyenne was right. You don’t sound egotistical while doing it. You just can’t see yourself ending up any other way.”
  • “you’re comparing your villain dad to the number one hero.” “No, they aren’t exactly the same, but you don’t want to be the next Anubis, do you? I figured someone like you’d want to be better than Anubis.” Kage laughs: “You’re right, Lance.”
  • Kage’s confession: “I am really bad at saving people. Everyone I’ve tried to save, it never ended well for them.” He squeezes the whistle. “I need to get better so that doesn’t happen again. I’m gonna save everyone who needs saving. That’s what I think the best hero should be. When someone’s in trouble, and they arrive, you immediately know things are going to be alright.” “Things are going to be alright” is Abby’s phrase.
  • Lance: “your smile is pretty comforting. It wouldn’t work if your smile was creepy.” The next day, Kage’s smile is red with blood and missing a tooth.
  • Kage: “sometimes you have to throw honor out to get the job done. It’s just life.” Lance: “I know that, Kage. But, this tournament isn’t life.”
  • Lance: “Duh, of course I was happy you beat the jerk that beat Cheyenne in seconds.”
  • Kage: “What you did to Volkan was nice too… It’s getting harder and harder to turn off the fighting brain as of late.” That’s the biomass, and he half-knows it.
  • “Your face is pretty punchable. And, don’t even try to say it back because you already said you liked my smile.”
  • Sparring for food: “don’t give me your credit card, rich boy.” Lance is rich. Villain money?
  • The promises: Kage: “I promise I won’t break anyone else’s gear. And, I promise that my fighting will be the same quality as the Elias fight.” Lance: “I’ll try and stop deciding who deserves it. That way we’re both trying something.”
  • “Oh, and sorry about grabbing your hilt. That wasn’t very honorable either.” Lance: “No, it wasn’t, Kage.”

What it’s doing. It’s the moral heart of Section 3. Two boys with something to atone for compare notes, and one of them is lying about what he is.

What works. Lance’s backstory is the best character reveal in Section 3, and the ants are perfect: specific, small, and thematically loaded. “Okay, Anubis” is devastating. The structure (two boys lying down, staring at the ceiling) makes it intimate without anyone saying so. And the ending is lovely.

What I’d push on. Lance’s backstory explains his honor, but there’s a hook the book could pull: Lance knows “breaking and entering without a trace.” Kage has been breaking into rooms all book (Anubis’s office, the school, Lucifer’s room). If Lance ever learns how Kage got into Lucifer’s room, he’ll recognize the method. That’s a great future scene.

Score 9/10

3.14 Honor Is For Suckers

1,566 words. Arena, Sunday morning: Reina vs. Lucifer.

What happens. Kage falls asleep on Lance’s shoulder on the bus. Reina vs. Lucifer is first. Elias asks Lucifer to win quickly so he can watch Kage more; Lucifer says he’s going out to “have some fun.” Kage sits next to Elias to watch. Lucifer opens by blasting Reina with fire, which is exactly what makes her huge: her power absorbs heat into muscle. She juggles him like a footbag. Her Rhatella-made backpack lets her store and release energy to shrink and grow at will. Lucifer overfeeds her heat to slow her down, then destroys the backpack with a fire kick and, after a hesitation, kicks her out of bounds. He doesn’t look at her afterward; he looks at Anubis. Gene announces Kage vs. Lucifer immediately. Both boys: “WAIT! WHAT?!”

Details I caught.

  • Viewers were promised entry at six a.m., so the students were brought in early.
  • “Kage yawned and leaned his head on Lance’s shoulder on the bus, falling asleep much to Lance’s dismay… Cheyenne and Rebento… took photos of the sleeping Kage, but Lance didn’t push him away.” The night’s conversation worked.
  • Gene: “we brake for nothing and no one!” “Brake” for a bus. A pun.
  • Reina kisses Lucifer’s cheek: “I still plan to get you like BOOM!”
  • Elias: “I want another look at Kage, and I know you’re winning. You could crack him open for me.” Lucifer: “Sorry, Min. I’m going out there to have some fun.” The deal requires a show.
  • Kage sits beside Elias “circulating some glue in his hand.” Elias reaches for his missing glasses again.
  • Kage: “I’m fighting one of them in the finals so…scooch over?”
  • Reina: “You’re gonna try and dash at me as soon as that count is over, aren’t you?” Lucifer: “Sorry to disappoint you, but you’re dead wrong.” He floods her with fire.
  • Huoyan: “Here it comes. You’re getting exactly what you wanted, Khaled.”
  • “Out walked a hulking person that only slightly resembled a girl. The creature was enormous with a soft face that didn’t match.”
  • Reina’s card: Heat Charge. “Her body is capable of taking in thermal energy and using it to greatly increase her muscle mass. The more she takes in, the bigger and stronger she becomes, but with greater size comes less mobility! As she punches, her body shrinks and burns the energy away! When she’s completely empty, she becomes dizzy and immobile! She’s the only one in the world that can truly say they can handle Lucifer’s heat!” That pays off four setups at once: “Our enhancements work perfectly together” (2.5), “you’ll be in big trouble if you have to face Reina” (2.13), “I’ll get you like BOOM” with her skinny arms (2.13), and Lucifer’s plan to fight without fire (3.8).
  • “Awww, babe you shouldn’t have! I thought you were gonna fist fight me!” A cute voice from a giant.
  • Lucifer, glancing at the teacher lobby: “…give the audience a show!”
  • Reina “slammed his back onto her knee. After that, she kicked him up in the air a few times as if he were a footbag, and went to end the match early with an overhead kick… as if she was trying to land a last minute goal in a soccer overtime.”
  • Lucifer saves himself with a blast of flame.
  • “It’s something by Rhatella! She made a support item I have on my back that can store the energy, take it out, and put it back in on my command!” Cruz, C, is before O in the alphabet. Rhatella’s order holds.
  • “The smaller she got, the faster she became.”
  • Lucifer’s plan: overfeed her heat as she grows (slower), and then “he blasted over her and shattered her support item with a fire-imbued kick.”
  • “Kage turned to Lance as soon as he saw what happened, and he saw Lance’s hand covering his face in indignation.” Lucifer just did the exact thing Lance spent last night lecturing Kage about, to his own girlfriend.
  • “she saw Lucifer hesitating to take her out. But, the decision was written on Lucifer’s face moments later. ‘Sorry, Cruz! I gotta move on!’”
  • “A Lucifer fight was now guaranteed for him, and it made his skin crawl in a way that was oddly alluring.” The biomass wants this.
  • Kage laughs at Elias. “Elias didn’t quite get why Kage would laugh at him. Fight hungry? Possibly, but Elias was frustrated he couldn’t tell despite being this close to the subject.”
  • Reina: “Heh, Lu! That was really…good…” “Lucifer wasn’t even paying attention to her. His eyes were on the teacher lobby with Anubis giving a thumbs up to him.” The deal costs him something real.
  • Lucifer’s eyes meet Kage’s. “Elias recognized the look, recognized the way it matched his own with Kage’s yesterday.”
  • Gene: “WELL! Then why don’t we see more of him right now?!” She’s still throwing wrenches. “Lucifer Tulivalo versus Kage Ozul!”
  • “WAIT! WHAT?!” in unison. Kage and Lucifer, in unison, like Kage and Orion’s “We’re not friends!”

What it’s doing. It pays off Reina, shows Lucifer choosing the deal over his girlfriend, and slams Kage straight into the fight that matters.

What works. Reina’s card is a great four-way payoff. The footbag image is wonderful. Lucifer breaking the backpack right after Lance’s honor lecture is a sharp irony, and Lance’s face says it all. And Lucifer ignoring Reina for Anubis’s thumbs up is a quiet, terrible beat.

What I’d push on. The title “Honor Is For Suckers” is great, but the chapter could make one more nod to it. (There’s a lovely buried pun: Cheyenne’s strawberry lollipop is a “sucker.”) Also, Reina’s reaction to losing her support item and being ignored is one line long. She gets angry later (4.3), but a beat of hurt here would set it up.

Score 7.5/10

3.15 Paid In Exposure

2,386 words. Arena: Kage vs. Lucifer, part one.

What happens. Kage refuses to rest: this advantage is being handed to him. His friends, even Volkan, gather to wish him luck. He gives Abby’s whistle to Orion for safekeeping, corrects Zulfiya (“It’s Prince Kage”), and goes. Elias tells Reina Lucifer will lose, because Kage is “illogically intelligent.” Upstairs, Anubis hands Huoyan a beer; Kelly confiscates it. Lucifer headbutts Kage and burns his face point-blank. In the smoke, black tendrils pour from Kage’s mouth and nose and rebuild his face. He reaches for the whistle that isn’t there, laughs, and rips off his shirt. “DO IT AGAIN!” He blocks fire with a heat-proof “fire-stop caulk,” explaining it like a mad scientist, then demands a fistfight without powers. They trade undodged blows. Ailani: “He’s worse.” Lucifer knocks out a tooth; Kage spits it into his eye, knees him, and headbutts him bloody while laughing. He whispers: “Give…me…Abaddon. Or, I’ll knock you out and bring him here myself.”

Details I caught.

  • “This is an advantage being handed to me on a silver platter. I’m taking it! Right, Lance?” Lance laughs and gives a thumbs up.
  • The send-off: Ailani, Zulfiya, Cheyenne, Lance, Volkan, and Rebento come over with Orion. “Volkan was the one that made him feel the most emotional. Someone he barely knew came over and acted like they were friends.” Kage forgot who Volkan was two chapters ago.
  • “His hand went towards the whistle, but he didn’t clutch it. He pulled it off his neck and threw it to Orion.” “Keep that safe for me, okay? I don’t want it getting damaged.” He takes off his anchor before the fight where he’ll need it most. Orion, of all people, ends up holding Abby’s whistle.
  • Rebento: “I DO NOT want to see him get to the grand finals!” Cheyenne: “You sure you’re not just scared of having to fight Lucifer?”
  • Kage: “You…really all are?”
  • “Of course, King!” “Sorry, you said my name wrong. It’s Prince Kage, Zulfiya!” Zulfiya: “It is odd, but Prince doesn’t sound too bad!”
  • Lucifer: “That rat bitch upstairs is playing games!” (Gene.)
  • Reina: “Love you!”
  • “Lucifer’s eyes met the sticky freak he wanted gone.”
  • Anubis is fine with the change “since the merch numbers were up whenever Lucifer was fighting.”
  • Elias: “No. He doesn’t.” (Lucifer won’t win.) “Ozul’s brand of chaotic is actually chaotic in a way many other ‘drunken’ fighters aren’t. He doesn’t make any sense. He’s illogically intelligent. So much so that it strains reason. That type of opponent will make Luci mad more than anything.” “You have to fight Ozul reactively.”
  • The opening clash: “a classic wrestler collar and elbow tie-up,” glue against fire, mid-air.
  • The beer: Anubis hands Huoyan one. “You know I barely drink anymore. I’m around children all day.” “Anymore.” Huoyan used to drink. (Huoyan’s past keeps leaking out: the sidekick days, the sleeping at school, the drinking.)
  • Kelly confiscates it and chugs it: “A shit brand you chose, anyway.”
  • Gene: “His whole fighting style will probably change!”
  • Huoyan: “Every statistic points towards a victory for my best student.” Kelly: “That Lucifer kid is a monster in the making!” Literally.
  • Lucifer’s headbutt steals “the first syllable” of Kage’s taunt.
  • “THEY’RE GONNA HAVE TO STITCH THAT FACE OF YOURS BACK TOGETHER! SEE YA, LOSER!” Point-blank fire to the face. “Doing something like that to a classmate was deemed as overkill even for Lucifer.”
  • The exposure: “Black tendrils spewed from Kage’s mouth and nostrils. They restructured his face, and Kage could feel every disgusting thing crawling on him. Burned tissue slagged off in slabs of skin.” The smoke hides it from the cameras. “The crowd couldn’t see it, but the students and teachers could. Something in Kage’s eyes seemed off. The emotion looked almost…feral.”
