  • “Kage’s hand twitched on the way up to his hair. He ran his fingers through it before slowly reaching for where Abby’s whistle would normally be. But, when it wasn’t there, a soft laugh escaped his mouth.” Without the whistle, there’s nothing holding him back. He rips his shirt off.
  • “What’s the problem? You afraid?” Lucifer is “backing away.”
  • Huoyan: “He got burned when I shot a fireball at him, so I’m not sure what changed.” A callback to 2.2, and the first adult to notice the biomass’s effect.
  • Anubis: “That’s…PERFECT! A HERO WHO HAS SECRETS IN HIS POCKET IS THE BEST KIND!”
  • “DO IT AGAIN!”
  • Fire-stop caulk: “It’s an epoxy I’ve been working on that can handle extreme heat! I looked up different kinds of adhesives, and when I found that one, well, I worked on making it ASAP! I can make ANY adhesive, Lucifer! Adhesives that were specifically chosen to counter you!” Firestop sealant is a real construction material designed to block fire. This pays off the glue he was “working on” in 3.6 and 3.14, and his glances at Lucifer. It also pays off the Prologue’s “sharp enough to ace anything.”
  • “The explanation went on and on. It wasn’t like Kage’s normal rants. It just dragged, and it sounded akin to some mad scientist talking about their greatest creation.” The biomass is changing how he talks.
  • “No powers for a bit! Just hand to hand combat! Or, are you too scared that you’d struggle to hit me?”
  • “You mouthy little insect!”
  • “Kage didn’t even try to dodge.”
  • Orion: “Every time Lucifer hits Kage, Kage is taking more damage than the punches he’s dishing out… He’s not this stupid to not even attempt to dodge punches or anything!”
  • Ailani, hand pressed against the glass: “I think he’s…trying to prove he’s better. Not the better fighter, better in every category.… Yeah, he’s not…acting himself. He’s worse.” That’s Thanatos’s warning made visible: “amplify your traits, especially the negative ones.”
  • “It was like two rams running into each other to assert dominance.”
  • “he could feel a tooth under his tongue that Lucifer knocked out.”
  • “You done?” Lucifer stomps on his chest. “Kage spit the tooth out of his mouth and into Lucifer’s eye.”
  • He grabs Lucifer’s horns into a knee, then a second knee, then headbutts “again, and again, and again, and again.”
  • “Mothers began to avert their children’s eyes. Alex’s mother was no different, but Alex pushed his mother’s hand away to get a better look at the vicious Kage.” In 3.16, we learn why: he’s scared for Kage.
  • “the only noise that could be heard was Kage’s unhinged laughter.”
  • “His smile was red and blood trailed down his chin from where his tooth used to be.” Lance said his smile was comforting, and “it wouldn’t work if your smile was creepy.”
  • The whisper: “I promised Lance I’d beat you at your best. This isn’t your best. Give me everything you have, Lucifer. Give…me…Abaddon.” He turns his honor promise into a license to summon a demon in a stadium full of people.
  • Lance: “Nothing Kage was doing was dishonorable, but it also didn’t feel like the Kage he had spoken to last night.” “a knot in his stomach he couldn’t place.” Cheyenne hides behind her hat. Zulfiya covers her mouth.
  • “Or, I’ll knock you out and bring him here myself.” He knows knocking Lucifer out releases Abaddon. He’s threatening to do it in front of the world.

What it’s doing. It’s the moment the biomass takes Kage over in public. The title has three meanings: being “paid in exposure” (fame as payment), Kage’s secret being exposed, and Abaddon about to be exposed to millions.

What works. The whistle handoff is the key move. The book shows you exactly why Kage loses control: he took off the one thing that kept Abby with him. The fire-stop caulk is a great power moment. Ailani’s “He’s worse” is the right line from the right person. The final whisper is chilling.

What I’d push on. Kage asks for a fistfight “no powers” with a boy who could kill him. That’s the biomass talking, and it’s meant to look irrational. But the book should make sure readers know Kage knows he’ll heal. A single line where he thinks it (“Let him hit. I’ll grow it back.”) would make his choice frightening rather than confusing.

Score 8.5/10

3.16 Giving Into Yourself

3,658 words. Lucifer’s mindscape; arena: Kage vs. Abaddon.

What happens. Lucifer wakes in his childhood home, where Abaddon sits at the kitchen table in a child’s chair. Abaddon restores Lucifer’s memory of the night Kage knocked him out and tells him Kage regenerates, but regeneration costs energy. Abaddon pushes: “We are one,” accept your true self, let me out and win you this. Lucifer refuses, then, back in the ring, turns his flames on himself, mouths “I’m sorry” to Elias, and becomes Abaddon on live TV. Reina didn’t know. Elias runs to the bathroom shaking. Anubis is thrilled. Kage punches Abaddon so hard he breaks his own wrist, heals, breaks it again, inching the demon toward the edge, until fatigue hits. Abaddon snaps Kage’s arm, and the world watches it heal. Rebento asks what they’ve enabled. Kage chokes Abaddon with tape while being slammed into the floor over and over. Seeing Alex’s frightened face, he smiles and sticks out his tongue. Alex laughs, then cheers; his mother cheers; the stadium cheers. Abaddon flies them both into the dome; both pass out; the biomass heals Kage completely. Gene rules Lucifer was out a split second first. Kage wins. Orion raises the whistle, alone.

Details I caught.

  • “Lucifer’s eyes shot open with hyperventilation following.” Everyone in this book wakes up gasping.
  • “He gently opened the door to see a ten foot tall being in a child’s chair. Abaddon was sitting at the kitchen table.” A demon at a family dinner table. It’s a great image: domestic, childish, and enormous.
  • “YOU ARRIVED!” “Can we relax on the yelling..? I’m standing right here.” Abaddon then speaks normally, and in the first person (“I was saying”). Outside, he speaks in caps and in the third person. Inside, he’s a person.
  • “That’s because I’ve been holding you prisoner here. How’s the jail cell anyway?”
  • “You know I don’t sleep specifically to make sure you stay. You’re like a dog being told to sit and roll over.” Abaddon called Lucifer a dog in 2.12. Each calls the other the dog.
  • Abaddon “placed a hand on top of Lucifer’s head.” Memories flood back.
  • “Being out again after years was a dream indeed!” “After years.” Abaddon has been out before.
  • “That red one is impervious to damage.… He grew his face back in that smoke.”
  • Lucifer: “He’s a sticky piece of shit. He shouldn’t have a healing enhancement.”
  • Abaddon: “That level of regrowth requires energy. The more he breaks, the more fatigued he will be, the easier it will be to defeat him.” It’s sound reasoning, and it’s exactly what happens.
  • “I am tired of living my life watching yours through this domestic kitchen window!”
  • “Holy shit. That means Min was right about the claymore. Kage was the one who was there that night.”
  • “If I let you out, the second you’re done with Kage you’re going to try and kill people!”
  • Abaddon: “At worst, the only person I’d be able to kill is Kage. You seem to want him dead anyhow.” Lucifer: “Yes, devil. I could get expelled.”
  • “You have a soft spot for him. I understand. You knew that blast to the face could be healed by one of the nurses’ enhancements at your school.” Lucifer calibrated the point-blank blast so Kage would survive. He isn’t a killer, and Abaddon knows it.
  • “I am not a sickness or a disease! We are one! You treat us as separate, but we couldn’t exist without one another. You wear those devil horns, but you won’t accept your true self? I’m everything you want to be! I’m strong. I’m powerful. I don’t hesitate.” The title: giving into yourself.
  • “Why do you think you had so much energy as of late? It’s because I was freed!” Confirms 2.13.
  • “See! You agree that he is a bug that needs to be squashed before he cocoons and grows wings!” Insects again, and metamorphosis: Ailani’s butterfly.
  • “You fail here, and you’re lower in the pecking order than him. You’re a secondary player in Kage Ozul’s story. Hell, you might even end up his back up.” Abaddon tells Lucifer he’s a side character. It’s a meta line, and it hits his biggest fear: being someone’s sidekick (Elias: “You’ll end up my sidekick”).
  • Abaddon kicks him out the front door.
  • Lucifer headbutts back. “The mix of Kage’s and his own [blood] marking his whole face in red.”
  • “KEEP GOING! THIS IS AWESOME!” one spectator yells. “They were treated like wrestlers who finally got color on camera.” In wrestling slang, “color” means blood. The crowd wants blood.
  • “For a moment, Lucifer and Kage smiled at each other. Both only stopped when they realized who they were smiling towards.”
  • “You having fun, Luci?” “Only Min gets to call me that. You see me smile and think we’re friends? You’re dreaming.”
  • The release: “He locked eyes with Elias… all he saw was Lucifer’s self-acceptance and him mouthing the words ‘I’m sorry.’ Lucifer’s mouth opened with a blast of flame coming out of it, but it didn’t blast towards Kage. It turned around and enveloped Lucifer’s head.” He sets his own head on fire to bring the demon out.
  • Reina: “WOAH! WHEN COULD LU DO THAT, MIN?!” She didn’t know. “Reina tried to put a hand on Elias, but he smacked it away. ‘I have to leave. Watch Lucifer.’” “JUST WATCH HIM!” “It was the most emotional Reina had ever seen him.” He runs to the bathroom.
  • Huoyan: “Did you know he could do that?” Anubis: “Yeah, I did. I didn’t think he could control it though.”
  • Anubis: “THAT IS HOW YOU PUT ON A SHOW! Lucifer understands this better than anyone! The whole world just switched their T.V.s straight to the Elite Tournament!”
  • “ABADDON IS IN THE FLESH ONCE MORE!” “AND I’M STILL HERE! You ready for round two, demon?”
  • The concrete gauntlets do nothing. Then: “He punched Abaddon so hard that he ended up breaking his wrist.… that quarter of an inch meant the world.… Repeatedly, he broke his hands on every single blow pushing Abaddon back a tiny bit each time.” He uses self-destruction as a strategy. Anubis’s file said “self-destructive tendencies.”
  • “Then, it all hit him at once. The adrenaline had worn off, and his body told him the cost of regeneration. Extreme fatigue washed over him.” Abaddon: “I was right! You can’t repair forever! You’re just like anyone else!”
  • “Every time he smashed his fist into the demon’s body, he filled in the gaps in the concrete gauntlets that were made. All that hardening made Kage burn up unconsciously.” Hardening draws cold from his body. His card said so in 1.2. The book keeps its rules for 3,000 lines.
  • “YOU FOOLISH PEOPLE CHEERED THIS MAN?” Abaddon snaps Kage’s arm over his knee “like a child would snap a pencil,” and “the whole world watched Kage’s arm go from broken to normal in a matter of seconds.” Anubis: “HE CAN HEAL HIMSELF?! THIS BOY IS REMARKABLE! THAT’S YOUR STUDENT!” Huoyan “didn’t even know what to say anymore.”
  • “Orion and Ailani both understood that whatever Kage was doing now had to have been what he did that night.”
  • Rebento: “This is the guy we helped yesterday? What…exactly did we enable by letting him be here?… That’s…not natural… One week and he’s capable of being in the finals? That doesn’t add up…” Rebento is right, and nobody wants to hear it.
  • “You’ll try to attack them if I lose…won’t you?” Abaddon: “Your selective intelligence confounds me. So smart, and so stupid.” That’s Elias’s “illogically intelligent,” from a demon.
  • Kage’s speech: “I needed you out here to drive me to win.… whether they know it or not, I’m the only one standing between this audience and possible death. A future number one hero needs to do that every single day. I can’t lose to you because if I lose and fail to save people a third time then I could never call myself a hero.… I brought you out myself so I can conquer you.” The third time: the store, Abby, and now the audience. But: “Kage spoke while unable to meet Abaddon’s eyes. Abaddon knew he was lying to himself and the world, but that only made him happier.” “The truth is so terrible that you need to make all of this look like your grand plan?! OH, I LIKE YOU!” Abaddon sees what Kage won’t admit: he wanted the demon because the biomass wanted the fight.
  • The choke: Kage climbs Abaddon’s back, wraps tape around his neck, glues his own feet in place: “You’ll pass out in a minute or so without air. Once you’re unconscious, I win.” He beats the unbeatable by not hurting him.
  • Abaddon bursts into flames; “a screeching Kage.” “He dropped loads of lipids into a higher quality and denser tape.” Abaddon jumps and slams him into the floor, again and again.
  • Kage “couldn’t heal himself because if he did, that would drain up the last bits of energy he had and make him pass out from exhaustion.” The cost again.
  • Huoyan: “I…can’t watch this anymore. He’s going to die down there.” Anubis grips his shoulder: “If he wanted to quit, he would have. He’s choosing to continue despite his body saying no. Isn’t that what being a hero is all about?” “Huoyan couldn’t disagree more.”
  • The tongue: “Kage’s eyes started to glaze over… when he finally could see again, he saw Alex’s face. The distress was clear.… Alex and Kage’s eyes locked, and even though Kage was in more pain than he ever had been in his life, he smiled and stuck his tongue out at Alex.”
  • “Alex was so shocked that a laugh ended up coming out. Then, he could see Kage relish in the laugh and understood that was the point.” This is the name speech from 1.9, acted out: a scared person laughs, and the scary predicament becomes less terrifying.
  • “DON’T GIVE UP, KAGE! YOU CAN BEAT HIM!” Alex’s mother: “She’d never seen her son care so deeply about any friend before.” “YEAH, KAGE! BEAT THAT DEVIL!” Then the crowd. The person he comforted holds him up.
  • Abaddon’s legs shake. “With one last desperate blast of fire, Abaddon flung them both so high up with the intention of slamming Kage into the top of the dome.” Abaddon passes out from the choke. Kage smashes into the dome and blacks out. “Just before Kage completely lost consciousness, the biomass took over with nothing left holding them back.” They land in a crater, both looking unharmed.
  • Gene: “With review of the footage, we have determined that Lucifer was knocked out a split second before Kage was. Therefore, as stated in the rules, the ones I just made up, your winner of the first tournament final is…KAGE OZUL!” Gene’s honesty about making up the rules is very funny.
  • The ending: “Orion just stood in front of the glass. He stared at the crater on the ground, and a smile started crawling onto his lips that he desperately didn’t want on his face. He reached into his pocket for the golden whistle Kage tossed him… He balled a fist around it before lifting it up high into the air for Kage.” Ailani holds her tongue. “Orion’s fist was the only hand raised up high for Kage in that lobby.”

What it’s doing. It’s the climax of the tournament’s first half and the moment the biomass, Abaddon, and the crowd’s appetite all meet.

What works. The mindscape is a great scene: a demon in a child’s chair, arguing like a roommate. The fight uses every rule (regeneration costs energy, hardening costs heat, unconsciousness ends Abaddon). The tongue is the purest heroism in the book. And the ending, Orion’s fist around Abby’s whistle, is the best final image in the book.

What I’d push on. One rule question. Section 2 and the Hellspawn card say Abaddon emerges when Lucifer “is put fully to sleep.” Here, Lucifer releases him by setting his own head on fire. Is that putting himself to sleep (burning himself unconscious)? Or did “accepting” Abaddon let him choose? Anubis says “I didn’t think he could control it,” which suggests this is new. That’s fine, but one clear line (Lucifer passing out in the flames, or Abaddon saying “you let me out”) would make the rule airtight.

Score 9.5/10

Section 3 overall

Section 3 is named for an event rather than a person, and the event becomes a character: the crowd. It watches, chants, boos, and eventually demands blood. The first half (3.1 to 3.10) is a deliberate anticlimax, nearly every match ending in seconds, and the book uses the gaps for character work (Severine’s breakdown, the racism argument, Lance’s honor challenge, the knighting). You’ve strengthened that stretch in this draft. The second half (3.11 to 3.16) is the best run of action in the book: Kage vs. Elias, the hotel talk, and Kage vs. Abaddon.

Section score: 8.3/10.

Section 4: “Ailani Shoma” (in progress)

4.0 Chapter NULL: The World’s Been Watching

1,124 words. Four rooms around the world, during the Kage–Lucifer fight.

What happens. Four scenes of people watching. Severine, at home with tea, decides Kage is now “significantly superior” and their rematch is inevitable. Rhatella, alone in her room, rebuilds the gear Kage and Lucifer broke, invents a new ship (Kage x Lucifer), admits she wishes someone would fight over her, and looks away at the exact second Kage’s arm heals on TV. In a bar, a drunk loses a $300 bet on “demon boy” to a man who says Kage is his student and whose scotch ice never melts: Frostbite. In a crappy apartment in Russia, Nerezza rewinds Kage’s screams for fun, until Thanatos kicks in her door with a new job: another politician is pushing the “Security Accords.” She shows him the broadcast. “DAMMIT, FROST! I’M GONNA KILL HIM!”

Details I caught.

  • The title: “Chapter NULL.” A chapter that doesn’t count, set outside the action, where the audience lives. It’s the book’s most formally playful choice.
  • “A teapot whistled on the coiled stove.” The first sound in Section 4 is a whistle. That might be coincidence. It’s still lovely.
  • Severine: “Ozul made it to the finals. I suppose that would make him significantly superior.… Our rematch is an inevitability now.” That’s her line from 1.8, 3,000 lines later. “But, at the end of the day, Ozul still won’t be able to hold a candle to my perfection.” She’s recovered. She’s fine.
  • Rhatella, “clanking away in her room to keep herself from crying”: “MY CREATIONS! THOSE BRUTES! Kage and Lucifer both break my stuff and now they’re breaking each other.” She saw her glasses and her backpack destroyed on worldwide TV.
  • “could I have been wrong about my Orion x Kage assessment? Am I living in a world where Lucifer and Kage are the true main couple of this god forsaken program?!” “PERHAPS THEY ALL END UP TOGETHER AS ONE BIG THREE PERSON PERFECT THRUPLE!” “Maybe that’s the next page in this zany manga!” A wink at the book’s genre.
  • Then: “All I know is I wish I was the one they were all fighting over…maybe I’ll just go back to making replacements of the things they broke…” “Next time I see Kage, he’s gonna get a piece of my mind! Lord knows he wouldn’t take my heart!” Rhatella has feelings for Kage. Ailani mentioned it in 2.3: Rhatella “never shuts up about you.”
  • “When she turned around to rub the sad away, that’s when Kage’s arm fixed itself on camera.… she didn’t see anything out of the ordinary.” She misses the one thing she, of all people, could analyze. And she still has a sample of his blood from before the biomass (1.9).
  • The bar: “I don’t even remember how much I bet on demon boy!” “You bet three hundred on him.”
  • The winner: “I’m betting a lot on that kid.” “He your son or some shit?” “No, no. He’s just a…student of mine. You could say I’m something of his mentor.”
  • “How’s your ice still whole in your scotch?! My shit looks like a sad pool of water now and yours still has a perfect sphere!” “Not sure. It is a bit too warm in here, though.” Frostbite, unmasked, in a bar, keeping his ice frozen. It’s the best “it’s him” reveal in the book, done without naming him.
  • “He has a lot more to learn, but he promised me he’d win. I’m holding him to it.” Frostbite is proud of Kage. He sounds like a father.
  • Worldbuilding: “The Elite Tournament isn’t just broadcast in the United States. It’s a worldwide event with international appeal. It’s subtitled in many different languages and has some commentators in English, Spanish, and Russian.”
  • Nerezza: “She kept rewinding over and over to keep hearing when Kage screamed in excruciating pain.” “Ah, it’s only been a week since I’ve heard that noise and I miss it already. Thank god that the hero program has this to satisfy my needs. Children screaming in pain is next level television.” Put that next to Anubis’s “THAT IS HOW YOU PUT ON A SHOW!” and the spectator’s “KEEP GOING! THIS IS AWESOME!” The cruelest villain and the hero establishment want the same product.
  • “She sighed and put her mask on only to throw it off the next second in annoyance.” She’s at home. She doesn’t need it.
  • Thanatos: “You’re getting soft. I would’ve been stabbed by now from doing something like that a year ago.” They’ve known each other a long time.
  • “Another government official is in office and is already trying to push the Security Accords again.” Nerezza: “The last man gets murdered and they throw another one right away to do their dirty work.” So the official killed in 2.6 was pushing the Security Accords.
  • “They’re fixated on getting these out so cameras are all over the hero city.” The villains are killing people who want more surveillance. Anubis, the hero, wants cameras in the dorms. The book’s politics are getting interesting: the watchers vs. the watched.
  • “It’s annoying to look for you in Russia.” Nerezza lives in Russia. But her card says she “can only go twenty miles from where she has walked.” She’s been in the American city, so she has walked there before. That’s consistent.
  • “We’re spending the night together.” “Oh, Thanatos! You think you can just lay claim to sleeping next to a fine woman like me? You have to earn that. You can sleep on the couch.” The flirtation from 2.7 continues.
  • “Your boy is putting on a show for the world.” Thanatos throws the phone across the room. “DAMMIT, FROST! I’M GONNA KILL HIM!”
  • Nerezza: “No, you can kill him later. We need to have a serious talk first.” A hook. About what? Frostbite’s betrayal? Kage? Orion’s secret, which she knows?

What it’s doing. It turns the camera around and shows us the audience, and every audience member is someone we know. It also advances the villains’ plot.

What works. The structure is clever: four rooms, one broadcast. The Frostbite reveal is perfect. Nerezza’s line is the book’s thesis, said by the worst person in it. Rhatella’s near-miss is a great setup.

What I’d push on. The chapter is all reaction until the final scene. It’s fine as an interlude, but Nerezza’s “serious talk” is the most important plot hook in it, and it’s the last line. Consider giving one more clue about what the talk is (a glance at Orion on screen, maybe), so the hook has a direction.

Score 8.5/10

4.1 Honey Toast

1,913 words. Flashback: first year.

What happens. First-year Kage, cat ears from his maid-café job still in his backpack, spots a girl working out on the field and tries his “Ozul Charm.” It’s Ailani, who knows exactly who he is. He invents a ship name (“Kailani”) and an acronym (“H.O.T.”). She refuses her number. He bets her a fight: if he wins, her number; if she wins, anything she wants. She wants honey toast from a bakery. He doesn’t even know how to stand; her crystal flattens him in one shot. She takes his hand and walks him to the bakery. He lets go to look at a cake. She won’t hold his hand again. He can only afford the toast and a cup of water. She tells him she wants to be a hero to be dependable for her family, and to fund her real dream: being a choreographer and dancer. He says he wants money and a woman. She shares her toast, wipes honey off his face, and leaves her number on a napkin with a heart.

Details I caught.

  • Section 4 opens with a first-year flashback centered on its namesake girl, just as Section 2 did with Abby. The structure rhymes.
  • “When he was in class, sleep. When he was at his dorm, sleep. All the rest of the time was put into work.” First-year Kage worked constantly. He wasn’t lazy so much as exhausted. That’s a lovely reframing.
  • “He awkwardly took the cat ears off of his head and shoved them in his backpack. He had forgotten to take them off after leaving his job.”
  • “Stupid fucking things! They make me look so dumb!… If it wasn’t for those guys giving me those fat tips, I would’ve left a while ago!”
  • Ailani: “a gorgeous girl in a pink tank top. Her curly hair and tanned skin drew him in.” Pink, again (1.3, the pink outfit; 2.3, the pink speaker).
  • “Sup, babe? Why are you working out here all alone? Shouldn’t a pretty girl like you be gossiping or something?” A bad line, and he knows it, sort of.
  • “I know who you are, Kage. You can run along and go take your nap.” Even in first year, his reputation precedes him.
  • “Ailani, huh? That is a very very very pretty name! Y’know, Kailani really rolls off the tongue, doesn’t it?” A ship name, immediately. And “Kailani” is a real Hawaiian name meaning “sea and sky.” It does roll off the tongue.
  • “Did you just make a ship name right after hearing my name? Pfft, my friends were right about you being a hopeless flirt.”
  • “Are you talking about those ladies in general studies?” Kage dated outside the hero program.
  • “Have you seen yourself in the mirror? That mirror definitely blushes every morning.” Mirrors again.
  • “H.O.T. Honest, outgoing, and totally my type.” Ailani: “Wouldn’t that last one be: ‘totally everyone’s type’ then?” She boops his nose. She’s quicker than he is.
  • “cherry head.” “Yeah, no. That’s not happening, chief.” Her name means “chief” in Hawaiian. If that’s a wink, it’s a great one.
  • “you’re in the top class like me, yes?” The tier system again.
  • He shoots “tiny strips of confetti-shaped tape” from his nails with jazz hands. The playful version of his power, before he ever used it to fight.
  • “Well, there is this honey toast I’ve been eyeing at that bakery.” The bread and honey from 1.3.
  • “He wasn’t really sure what a fighting pose looks like, so he looked pretty stupid with both his hands sprawled out and aimed at her. He closed his eyes as he laughed.” First-year Kage literally couldn’t fight. The Prologue said so: “Kage wasn’t used to wielding his enhancement in combat.”
  • “He looked down and saw this semi-translucent, pink rock lodged into his gut.”
  • Ailani’s card: “The freshman class’s Ailani Shoma!” The card is written in its own time period. Crystal Release. “A chromatic sigil blazes to life at her hands.” She can “generate, shape, and command crystals,” launch them, “raise them as walls, and forge them into weapons as massive as a war hammer.” “At her current level, her range caps at twenty-five meters in any direction! Her larger constructs can only be held for ten to fifteen seconds before the strain sets in, though smaller techniques cost her almost nothing and can be maintained indefinitely! One problem: push her too far and the crystals turn against her! Starting at the fingertips, the crystals slowly creep up toward the wrists until her hands are locked down completely!” Every rule here is used in 4.7: the war hammer, the creeping crystal, the locked hands.
  • Her cost is her hands. Kage’s wound is his hands. The two of them are matched at the level of the body.
  • “D-Damn…you’re strong, too? You really are the whole package, Ailani.” Still flirting, while winded.
  • “Nope! I’m just fine, but y’know, let’s just say you won! I don’t want to hurt you eheh…” In 2.3, Kage says the same thing: “I don’t want to fight you.” It’s been true since the day they met.
  • “You wouldn’t wanna hurt a delicate flower like myself.”
  • “She took hold of Kage’s hand and started walking off the field.” “It felt to him as if his hand was meant to be enveloping hers.” The hand again.
  • He lets go to stare at a chocolate cake. “You let go of my hand to ogle at a cake? You know, you may not get the chance to hold it again.” “I won’t let go a second time! I swear!” In the present day, his whole life is about a hand that let go. This is the first one. He did let go a second time, with all nine girls. And then a third, at the store. The book has planted its central image in a flirt at a bakery.
  • “One order of honey toast and…” “a water cup, please.” He can only afford her toast. An orphan with a part-time job, buying a girl the one thing she wanted.
  • Kage: “what’s a gorgeous girl like you doing in a hero school anyway? Wouldn’t your life be easier if you went into modeling instead?… heroing is hard shit.”
  • Ailani’s dream: “I want to be a hero to be dependable. I have a family at home and they deserve to feel safe. The hero course was the best way to get stronger, so the people in my life can rely on me. Plus, the pay is super good. It can help fund my other aspirations. I want to be a top choreographer and dancer!” That’s the “other dreams and goals” Anubis mentioned (2.13), the mirror room (2.3), and “It’s hard to dance when you’re that bulky” (3.3). And in 4.7, “Ailani had been counting beats her whole life.”
  • Kage: “I want money, and I want a woman. The second goal is pretty much accomplished! I’m on a date with a smokeshow right now!” Compare: present-day Kage wants to be the number one hero to atone for a death.
  • She shares the toast. “it was vacuumed up!” “I gave you that to savor! You’re not supposed to gobble up something delicate like this! I’m never gonna cook for you if this is how you eat.” That’s the title in action, and it’s a metaphor for their relationship: she gave him something delicate, and he didn’t savor it.
  • “I gotta make sure you actually want to be with me, and that you aren’t gonna go flirting with another girl tomorrow.” He does. Nine times.
  • The napkin: “her name and number, and she’d drawn a little heart at the bottom.” “She didn’t even turn back to look at him.” Compare 1.3: “looked back one last time, and slammed the door in his face.”
  • “I’ll call you later tonight! Don’t leave me hanging!”

What it’s doing. It shows us who Kage and Ailani were before everything, and plants Ailani’s dream right before her fights.

What works. It’s charming, funny, and full of quiet doom. The hand-holding beat is the best symbolic move in the section. The toast she tells him to “savor” is a perfect metaphor. And placing this flashback immediately after Kage’s bloodiest public moment is a smart contrast.

What I’d push on. Ailani’s dream is revealed in one line of dialogue. It’s the most important fact about her, and it would land harder with one image: what kind of dance, a song she choreographs to, the pink speaker. Section 4 is hers; give her dream some texture.

Score 8.5/10

4.2 The People Want More

1,319 words. A bathroom, the student lobby, the hero lounge.

What happens. Elias calms himself in a bathroom and returns to find Kage and Lucifer have been taken to the hero lounge. Upstairs, Anubis is thrilled by record merch sales. Gene dumped water on the unconscious boys; Kelly wakes them with an “Irish remedy” (smelling salts). They lunge at each other until Kelly separates them. Anubis tells Kage he won. Lucifer, furious, demands to know how Kage regenerates and how he knew about Abaddon. Kage invents a cover: “Tar Healing,” sticking himself back together internally, and says he deduced Abaddon from Anubis’s mention of “backstory” and Lucifer’s biblical name. Kelly kicks a minifridge to Kage. Both boys are wearing hero merch because their clothes burned off. Gene asks the crowd if they want the tournament to continue. They do.

Details I caught.

  • Elias “splashed his face with water a few times. He tried to calm his breathing by rubbing his temples.” A panic attack, after Abaddon. This is the trauma Anubis’s file said he wasn’t showing.
  • “Uhh, Cruz? Where are those two idiots?” Reina: “Anubis went down there to grab the both of them.” “Sorry, I know you told me to keep an eye on Lu, but they swooped him up so fast.”
  • Elias: “When Lucifer gets down here, he’s got a lot of explaining to do.”
  • “Woo! Merch sales are through the roof right now!”
  • Gene: “I dumped a bucket of water on them and still nothing.”
  • Huoyan: “Why not just let them rest? Lucifer is done for the day, and Kage doesn’t have another match in at least an hour.”
  • Anubis: “We need to find out why they both look fine!… A breakthrough in his enhancement like regeneration only comes from extreme determination!” He’s wrong. It came from a villain.
  • Kelly: “Fine, you guys are doing this wrong anyway.” The vial: “It’s an Irish remedy.” Kelly’s surname is Irish.
  • The boys pop up and pounce at each other. “THE FIGHT ENDED TEN MINUTES AGO!” Both: “OKAY! THEN WHO THE HELL WON?!” In unison, again.
  • Anubis pokes Kage: “You did!”
  • “Losing is a path to growth.” Aimed at Lucifer and the deal.
  • Kage offers his hand: “I don’t like you in the slightest, but I wouldn’t have people cheering me if I didn’t have a goliath to overcome.”
  • Lucifer: “Fuck you! I exposed myself in front of millions of people and you couldn’t just lose?! What is wrong with you?! Why do you feel the need to prove yourself?! You’re supposed to be the lazy, quiet kid.” Lucifer gave up the secret he’d protected his whole life, for nothing.
  • “The lying was coming to him more naturally than ever before.” The biomass amplifies the things you hide. He hides things now.
  • Tar Healing: “I am capable of sticking all kinds of things together. But, I never tried doing it internally before. So, one day I tried sticking myself back together after I had hurt myself. It worked, but the adhesive came out black. So, I call it Tar Healing.” It’s a clever lie, because it fits his power and explains the black.
  • Lucifer: “No, I’m mad because I don’t understand how you knew of my demon secret.” Huoyan: “Wait, Ozul, you knew Lucifer could do that as well?!”
  • Kage: “He has demon horns on his head. It’s not exactly subtle.”
  • Kage’s cover: “Anubis said that Lucifer has a backstory that needed to be hidden. I thought the only way that’d be the case is if Lucifer had some great hidden uncontrollable power… A name like Lucifer, just think about it.… I asked him to give me his best. I wanted destruction, or as the bible would put it, I wanted Abaddon.” Abaddon is Hebrew for “destruction.” Kage’s lie is etymologically accurate.
  • Huoyan: “That was…very articulate.” Anubis: “The kid is smarter than he looks.”
  • The slip: “You can ask Hermes. I called him all kinds of mythological names when we…fought…in the…school…yard…” He admits to Huoyan’s face that he and Orion fought in the schoolyard, the fight that got them banned. Huoyan doesn’t react. Maybe he’s past caring.
  • “Don’t snap at me like I’m your fucking dog.” Kelly kicks a minifridge over. “Sorry, I don’t mean to curse in front of kids.” The second minifridge gag.
  • “He gave him a look that asked whether this was really the boy he saw a future in.”
  • “he really looked at himself and Kage and saw they were both dressed in hero course merchandise since their clothes were torn during their fight.” Literally branded. The Prologue’s “brand logos on their chests,” come true.
  • Anubis: “We were wondering if it was best to end the tournament on that… Kelly and Huoyan were having cold feet.”
  • Gene: “It’s not what I think. It’s what they think.” “DO YOU ALL WANT THE NEXT MATCH?!” The crowd decides. The title: the people want more.
  • “The crater was just prepared, so our ring and the people are ready for more!”

What it’s doing. It’s the aftermath: Kage’s lie, Lucifer’s humiliation, and the crowd’s appetite.

What works. Kelly’s smelling salts and the minifridge are fun. Kage’s lie is clever. The merch detail is a perfect callback.

What I’d push on. Kage’s slip about the schoolyard fight is a great comic beat, but Huoyan’s silence about it is strange. He’s the one who issued the ban for that fight. Give him a dry line (“I’m aware, Ozul”) so the slip lands as a joke instead of a dropped thread.

Score 7.5/10

4.3 Can You Outrun Explosions?

1,653 words. The student lobby.

What happens. Back in the lobby, Lucifer pins Kage: he knows Kage broke into his room and knocked him out, and wants to know why. Elias hits Lucifer, hard, without freezing him, so he’ll feel it. Then Elias hugs Kage: if he’d lost, “I don’t think we’d be seeing you ever again.” Lucifer admits his bet with Anubis. Elias reveals why he wanted to be in the Elite Program: to keep watching Lucifer, in case the demon got out. “You know I never wanted to feel that feeling again.” Reina realizes both of them kept Abaddon from her and goes to sit with Kage’s friends. Rebento accuses Kage of lying about his power and calls for a vote to kick him out. Lance says he trusts Kage. Everyone, even Elias and Lucifer, votes for Kage to stay. Rebento accuses Orion of having secrets too. Kage, shaken by “Anubis took that deal,” says nothing. Rebento, to Orion: “Can you outrun explosions?”

Details I caught.

  • Lucifer: “I know how you know about Abaddon. I just asked you up there to see what bullshit you’d come up with for those heroes. I know you were in my dorm and knocked me out.” He played dumb upstairs. “Is lying a part of your training too?” “Training.” What does Lucifer suspect?
  • SMACK. Elias hits Lucifer, then punches him in the face. “He didn’t freeze Lucifer. He didn’t want these forces banked. He wanted Lucifer to feel every hit.” A power used as a character choice. That’s lovely.
  • “You ignorant, selfish, inconsiderate devil!… You put Ozul through that demon and then attack him again after like it’s his fault?!”
  • “This…this…moron tried to apologize to me before going through with it like that would make me feel better!” The “I’m sorry” Lucifer mouthed.
  • Elias hugs Kage. “Kage’s body stiffened at the gesture, but he didn’t push him away.” “You’re lucky you’re alive. I…I’m glad you were able to win. If you lost, I don’t think we’d be seeing you ever again.” Elias knows what Abaddon does to people.
  • “Yeah, I’m glad I beat him too. You can uhm…let go now.”
  • Lucifer: “I made a bet with Anubis that if I beat him he would stop scouting him.” Kage: “And, Anubis took that deal?”
  • Elias: “I don’t try and get a leg up through outside means. I handle things on my own.” That’s Orion’s flaw too (1.4).
  • Lucifer: “Well, you didn’t! You lost to him!… The one you couldn’t do!”
  • Elias’s confession: “I was only sad by the fact I might not make it to the Elite Program. Want to know why? Because I was worried I wouldn’t be able to keep watching you! I was worried that the demon might get out and I wouldn’t be there to stop it! Instead, I have to see the demon come out on my watch right in front of me when I can’t do anything about it?!… You know I never wanted to feel that feeling again.” Elias is Lucifer’s warden. His power stops things in time, which may be the only thing that can stop Abaddon. That’s why he sleeps in Lucifer’s room, why the claymore bears his family name, why he insists it hangs perfectly straight, and why he needed them all to go to the Elite Program “as a group.”
  • Kage: “Uhm, can I say something?” Both: “No.”
  • Reina: “So, both of you knew about this and kept it from me?! I knew you two were acting all weird. How long has this been going on?!” “Okay, cool. I’m gonna go sit over there with Kage.” She grabs Kage by the ear and drags him along.
  • Rebento shoves Kage into a wall: “You’re not off the leash just cuz Lucifer’s got secrets too.… we helped you! You’ve been lying about your power so we underestimate you or something?!” “His power is glue, dude!”
  • “He’s now a wildcard we can’t let stay around! Let alone be our future.” The chapter title of 1.8 (“Perfectionist Meets Wildcard”) comes back as an accusation.
  • Lance: “I trust Kage.” “I trust Kage deeply. I don’t know what’s going on with him, but I know that when we talked last night he promised me three things. He said he’d win, he said he’d fight with honor, and he said he was going to beat his next opponent at their absolute best. He did all of these things.” But Kage’s “at their absolute best” was a perversion of the promise, and Lance half-knows it.
  • “I…get what it feels like. Like, when fighting sometimes you end up as someone else.” That’s a confession. In 4.7, we learn what he means: “King Arthur.”
  • “Last night, he also told me that his hero idea is that when someone is afraid he could look at them and make them feel okay! I saw him do it! He showed Alex down there that everything was going to be alright.” Lance saw the tongue, and understood it.
  • Rebento: “If there’s even a one percent chance he’s hiding more stuff, then we need to act on it!”
  • The vote. “Who’s in favor of getting this liar out of here?” No hands. Lance: “Who’s in favor of Kage staying?” Lance, Zulfiya, Orion, Ailani, Volkan, Cheyenne, Reina, and, to Rebento’s surprise, Elias and Lucifer. Elias: “We hate him and don’t trust him, but we’re not gonna kick him out with some stupid popularity contest.” The two boys with the biggest grudge vote for fairness.
  • Rebento: “I’m never trusting Kage. If that even is his real name.” Ha.
  • Rebento to Orion: “you’ve been protecting Kage a lot since he’s gotten back. Are you lying too? Big Vance got some big secrets along with Kage and Lucifer, huh?” “Big Vance” is Inazuma’s phrase. And Rebento is right.
  • Ailani wants to yell. Kage stops her with a head shake. “Ailani was frustrated with continuing to be reactionary to it all. She wanted to be heard.… Even if he was one of the people she was tired of being reactionary toward.” That’s Section 4’s real subject: Ailani, who always reacts, wanting to act.
  • Kage: “Anubis took that deal..?” “half-questioning, and half-dread. If Lucifer was telling the truth, then his entire future was decided by a split second of breath, and that terrified him. And, on top of it all, he was lying to a room of people who just agreed he was trustworthy.”
  • Rebento: “Tell me Orion, I know you can outrun a lot of things, and you’re probably outrunning a bunch of lies right now with red-head over there, but…” “Can you outrun explosions?”

What it’s doing. It breaks open the Lucifer–Elias–Reina trio, confirms Elias’s role, and puts Kage on trial in front of his class.

What works. Elias’s confession is the emotional high point of Section 4 so far. Elias choosing not to freeze Lucifer is great power writing. The vote, especially Elias and Lucifer’s hands, is a terrific moment. Kage’s guilt at being trusted is exactly right.

What I’d push on. Lance’s defense rests on the claim that Kage fought “at their absolute best.” But Kage got that by demanding a demon, which Lance found disturbing an hour ago (“a knot in his stomach”). Lance would know he’s stretching. Give him one beat of that, maybe a glance at Kage that says “don’t make me regret this.”

Score 8.5/10

4.4 Sweeter Than Sugar

1,341 words. The lobby; Orion vs. Rebento begins.

What happens. Orion asks why Kage didn’t defend him. Kage: “We’re not friends.” Orion agrees, coldly, and gives back the whistle: “You look like you need it.” Ailani asks if Kage and Abby were ever together. Lance asks about the healing; Kage won’t say. Kage swears he’ll stand on the first-place podium, and Lance makes a wager: if Kage wins, Lance bows to him; if not, Kage wears Cheyenne’s hat on the podium. Cheyenne notices Kage’s missing tooth, and Zulfiya explains that detached body parts can’t regrow; if Abaddon had snapped his arm clean off, he’d have a stump. Cheyenne and Ailani tease that Kage and Lance kissed. In the ring, Rebento cracks his knuckles, and when Orion blitzes, the cherry gumball hidden in that fist explodes. “You’re looking at future pro hero: Sugar Bomb!”

Details I caught.

  • Orion: “I defended you and you couldn’t do the same for me?” Kage, laughing: “Defend? Why would I do that? We’re not friends.” Every earlier “we’re not friends” was a joke between them. This one is cruel.
  • Orion: “You’re right. We’re not.” He throws the whistle at Kage: “I think I probably should’ve given that back to you sooner. You look like you need it.” Orion is hurt, and he’s right: Kage does need it. Since Kage took it off, he’s been at his worst.
  • Ailani, whispering: “Were you and Abby ever…together?” “No! We were just friends!” “Oh, really? Just like how you and Orion aren’t friends. I’m just saying. If that’s how you act around your ‘not friends’, you admitting to being friends with her shows a lot of closeness.” Ailani has decoded Kage. “Not friends” means closest.
  • “me and Orion aren’t friends! That’s literally the only thing we agree on!”
  • Lance: “Can you do it on others?” Kage: “Sorry, I can’t reveal too much about it. I could be fighting either of you in the grand finals.”
  • “I’m gonna stand on that first place podium. I swear on everything.”
  • Lance: “We’re both still playing around like we’re royalty, right? If you end up on that top podium, I promise to bow before you.”
  • “what if, and this is just an insane hypothetical that would never happen, I don’t win the grand final.” “You have to wear Cheyenne’s hat and stand like her on the podium.” “That’s a death sentence and you know it!” Cheyenne: “I know you couldn’t pull off a look this good.” (He tried it on in 3.9. She said “Only one of us can pull this off.”)
  • Kage: “He may not be my friend, but he definitely is the one I’m rooting for.”
  • “Kage smiled for the first time since his big win.” Cheyenne: “Jeez, Partner! Your mouth got a hole bigger than a sandpit! Oh, bless your heart.”
  • “Oh, damn! My tooth! Shit, I thought I could heal it back.”
  • Zulfiya’s rule: “Detached body parts can’t simply be ‘grown back’ through healing. If you were to get decapitated, do you think you could just grow your head back and be normal once more, Prince?” (She’s calling him Prince now.) “When I saw that monster break your arm, I worried that he might’ve made a clean break all the way through. If that were the case, you’d end up an amputee right now. Your healing would’ve just covered the wound and left you with a stump.” That’s a crucial limit on the biomass, and it raises the stakes of every future fight.
  • “He stared down at his reformed wrists. The vision of them breaking over and over to move Abaddon entered his mind. He grew apprehensive. A mistake in that fight could’ve meant he never gets to be a hero again.”
  • Lance: “Hey, you got a look now! That’s good! I got nervous that it might’ve messed up your grin, but you don’t look any less comforting.”
  • Cheyenne and Ailani, in unison: “Did you guys kiss in that hotel room?” Kage, angry this time: “ARE YOU GUYS GONNA MAKE THAT JOKE EVERY TIME ME AND HIM ARE HAVING A MOMENT?!”
  • Cheyenne: “I mean I think you two are polar opposites. Oil and water, y’know?” Oil and water don’t mix. Kage’s power runs on oil.
  • Cheyenne to Lance: “It’s okay, hun. You’re sweeter than sugar! That’s why I wanna drink you up.” The title.
  • Zulfiya: “Hey, how long do you plan on ignoring your boyfriend and best friend?” Reina: “As long as it takes till they apologize.”
  • Reina: “doesn’t our class rep have some sort of sugar power?”
  • Orion: “What’s the point in cracking your knuckles?… Does intimidation like that actually work on people?”
  • Rebento: “I ain’t gonna let you near me to hit another nut punch… Inazuma may have been stupid, but at least he was respectful enough to try and beat you with his enhancement instead of fighting dirty.” Orion: “I wouldn’t call anything about Inazuma’s fighting style clean. Sounds like you’re just used to dirty.”
  • Orion blitzes: “a kick off that left wind fogging up the edges of the dome.” “the center of the ring exploded in a colorful red.”
  • “There was a cherry gumball in the fist I was cracking you idiot! I preloaded myself cuz I knew you’d get antsy.” The knuckle-crack was a trap.
  • “You’re looking at future pro hero: Sugar Bomb!” Rebento is Portuguese for “I burst.” Azucre is Galician for “sugar.” His name has been “Sugar Bomb” all along.

What it’s doing. It turns “we’re not friends” cruel, adds the wager, sets the regeneration limit, and starts Orion vs. Rebento.

What works. The whistle return is a great beat. Ailani decoding “not friends” is sharp. The tooth rule is important and well-delivered. The cherry gumball is a clever trap.

What I’d push on. The “did you kiss” joke has now been made several times (Rebento’s “new boyfriend,” Rhatella’s shipping, this). Kage’s anger at it is a nice turn, but it would be even better if the book did something with that anger. Is he angry because it’s annoying, or because Lance matters to him and he’s afraid of that?

Score 7.5/10

4.5 Running Off Those Calories

1,105 words. Arena: Orion vs. Rebento.

What happens. Rebento floods the arena with pre-charged gumballs. Orion throws his gold loc rings to detonate a chain reaction, uses the rainbow smoke as cover, lands on Rebento, and drags his face along the ground while running circles, precisely, just enough to scrape. Thrown out, Rebento blasts himself back in bounds with a jawbreaker against the dome wall. He sprays explosive sprinkles, plants hard candies around the ring, and his eyes turn to rainbow swirls: his telekinesis tell. Kage yells from the window: “GET LOW!” Orion slides under the converging blast, appears behind Rebento, and finishes it. “He improvised a plan for me to execute.”

Details I caught.

  • “half of the arena floor in front of Rebento was covered with loads of rainbow polka-dots.”
  • “What good is running as a power if your feet can’t touch the ground?” A good taunt.
  • “A couple of loc rings hit the gumballs closest to Rebento.” Orion’s gold jewelry, described in his very first scene (1.1), becomes a weapon in his most recent one.
  • “He took a step, as if preparing to run, and then leapt.” A feint, from a boy who was feinted in 1.15.
  • “He used the rainbow smoke from the explosions as cover.”
  • “Orion ran circles across the arena while dragging Rebento’s head into the ground.” It’s brutal. Cheyenne: “That move could deform Rebento!”
  • Ailani: “That speed is like a light jog for Orion. If he wanted to tear Rebento’s face off, he wouldn’t be moving that slow. He’s staying that slow so he can be precise about the amount of pressure he puts on his face. It looks to me like he just wants to scrape him up a little.” Controlled cruelty. Orion has been sliding toward Kage’s darkness all section.
  • Rebento throws a charged jawbreaker at the dome: “The white blast swallowed him, hurt like hell, and hurled him back over the line.” He hurts himself to stay in. That’s a Kage move.
  • Orion: “You wasted too much energy charging those earlier gumballs.… I’m trying to win squeaky clean for you.” After the low blow, he wants to be clean.
  • “The smell of burning caramel filled the arena.” A great sensory detail.
  • Sprinkles: “The sprinkles spread wide, like a shotgun blast.” “A bunch of tiny explosions.”
  • “his eyes turned from normal into swirls of rainbow.” Lance: “When Rebento’s eyes go all colorful like that, that’s his tell for when he’s about to use telekinesis!” Kage’s tell is his hair. Ailani’s is her rotating sigil. Rebento’s is his eyes.
  • Kage: “ORION! HE’S SURROUNDED YOU WITH BOMBS! YOU GOTTA GET LOW!”
  • “I told you, speedster! One way or another, I was gonna make you go KABLAM!” The candies converge at chest height. Orion’s “baseball slide.” (Baseballs again: Elias’s baseball, Kage’s claymore swing, Lance’s batted crystals in 4.7.)
  • Rebento: “He’s done there!” with “a pyromaniac’s sparkle in his eyes.”
  • “Can you at least not do the face again? I got blood dripping down my forehead.” “You got it.” A punch to the gut instead. Orion grants the request.
  • Kage: “I TOLD YOU REBENTO HAD NO CHANCE! Me, Ailani, and Orion are the big three around here!”
  • “Oh, Orion knows why.” The converging glue orbs from 1.15: Kage beat Orion with this exact move. Orion knows how to dodge it because he didn’t.
  • Orion: “He improvised a plan for me to execute. Not bad.” “You improvise, I execute” (1.6, 2.6), one more time.
  • “For someone who really doesn’t like Kage, you sure do fight a lot like him. I’m shocked you guys aren’t friends.” Rebento joins Inazuma as another Kage mirror: loud, inventive, self-damaging, explosive. And Orion uses “not friends” as a jab.

What it’s doing. It’s a clean action chapter that puts Orion in the half final and shows the improvise/execute combo working from across a pane of glass.

What works. The loc rings are a great payoff. Ailani reading Orion’s pressure is a sharp detail. The candies and sprinkles make Rebento’s power fun. And the final line ties the whole Orion–Kage thread together.

What I’d push on. The face-dragging is the most violent thing Orion has done, and he does it to a classmate who was rude to him, not a villain. Ailani explains it away. But Orion should notice it himself. He’s been getting harder since Inazuma. A beat of him looking at Rebento’s bloody face would make his slide visible to him, not just to us.

Score 7.5/10

4.6 Curtain Call

1,591 words. The student lobby.

What happens. Ailani heads out early for her match against Lance. Both of them demand to know whom Kage wants to face in the grand final; he points at Orion, because the ban means this might be his last chance to fight him. Kage theorizes that Anubis co-signed the ban and might let a crowd-pleasing rematch happen. Rebento returns, angry that Kage coached Orion, and accuses him again. Kage says he knew the move because he invented it himself, then mocks Rebento: “You’re getting lapped as is.” Rebento leaves to sit with Lucifer and Elias. Lance tells Kage to earn Rebento’s trust; Kage says he doesn’t want to, because Anubis’s love “is conditional” and he can’t afford to lose. Then he lets slip that Anubis wants him as his successor. The whole group surrounds him. Ailani, heading to the arena, orders everyone not to ask him anything until she gets back: “This is my time, and I’ve been dying to perform.”

Details I caught.

  • Ailani wants “to be the first one out there for my match.” Kage wanted to be first through the stadium doors (2.15). Now she does too.
  • Lance and Ailani’s stare-down. Orion “awkwardly got past” them. “I ain’t got a clue. Sizing each other up, I guess?”
  • “Who do you want to win, Kage?” in unison.
  • Ailani: “You’re on the thinnest of thin ice, and you’re gonna risk cheering on my opponent?”
  • Lance: “Not to be rude, but I didn’t see you defending Kage when Rebento was going crazy.” That’s unfair: Kage stopped her. “Excuse me?!”
  • Kage “nervously fidgeted with his collar, and his hand moved up to his whistle.” He has it back.
  • He jabs his thumb at Orion. “YOU’RE HOPELESS!”
  • “I want to fight Orion because this might be the last time I ever get to fight him!” The ban.
  • “I know it’s only been a little over a week since we last fought.” Thursday to the second Sunday: ten days. The calendar checks.
  • “you just want to do something more when you know you’re not allowed to do it, right?”
  • Kage’s theory: “the ban was agreed upon between him and Anubis. At least, that was me and Orion’s theory at the time. That would mean Anubis has final say on it too.… if that crowd is calling for me and Orion to go at it, who are we to deprive them?” Kage is now thinking like Anubis: what the crowd wants.
  • Lance: “Thespian at heart, and a tactical brain. You are one weird guy, Prince.” Ailani: “Do not tell me that the whole ‘prince thing’ is catching on.” “He’s like a walking contradiction.” Orion: “I’ll be the first to agree with that. Kage makes no sense.”
  • Rebento: “your fucking lying friend told me you coached him.” Orion: “We’re not friends,” flatly. Orion’s turn to say it bitterly.
  • Lance: “Kage actually seemed to know more about combat than I did during that fight, but I did tell him your telekinesis tell. Sorry.”
  • Rebento: “And how did you know he had to get low?! You seen me do that move before?! You been watching when no one was looking?!”
  • Kage: “Nah, I haven’t been watching you. I knew how to dodge it because I did that move myself.”
  • Rebento: “That took me a year to get down, and you’re telling me you just did it after a bit of enhancement practice? I don’t buy it.”
  • “Kage was getting tired of hitting walls.” Ha.
  • Kage: “You’re the only one not buying, remember? At the end of the day, I’m in the grand finals, and you’re…well, you’re a choice for the Elite Program, I guess. It’s always good to have extra options.” Then: “I was just thinking about what would’ve happened if I did train last year. I mean, if I’m this far ahead of you with way less time, imagine if I was training the whole time. You wouldn’t even be a footnote right now. You’re getting lapped as is.” Kage is cruel here. Rebento was right about him, and Kage punishes him for it. That’s the biomass, or Kage, or both.
  • Lance: “You’re the one being disrespectful first. He’s matching your energy, but Kage, you should tone it down a little.”
  • Rebento: “You’ve known me this whole time, and you just met Kage yesterday!… I never thought I’d be agreeing with Lucifer, but here we are!” He goes to sit with Lucifer and Elias.
  • Lance: “You have to try to earn his trust like you earned mine, Kage.” Kage: “I don’t want to! We don’t get along, and that’s fine! I’m not trying to befriend everybody.”
  • “if I lose, that could be it between me and Anubis.”
  • “Yeah, he seems to. For now, but it’s pretty clear to me that his love is conditional. If I don’t perform at the levels he sets…” He thinks about what Lucifer said. “I don’t deserve that love.” The foster kid who stopped unpacking his bag. He believes love is earned and can be revoked, because every adult in his life has proven it.
  • Ailani: “What do you mean you wouldn’t deserve his love if you don’t win? You’ve done great, and you’re in the last match! That’s the main event! You don’t think that’s enough?”
  • “Should a number one hero ever be number two?” “He was willing to gamble me away before. Why would he stop now? He thinks I’m unstoppable. I have to make his thoughts a reality if I want to keep any shot at being his successor.” He covers his mouth.
  • “Every person around him made him feel smaller.”
  • Rebento: “Oh, your ego is that humongous?! You really think that shit?” “Lucifer and Elias both shot dirty looks at Kage as well, but neither of them was as surprised as everyone else.” Lucifer knew from the deal.
  • Lance: “He wouldn’t say that unless he knew. Kage, you’re really pegged to be Anubis’s full-blown replacement? Last night you said you wanted to be better than him.” Lance remembers.
  • Ailani “tied her hair up” (a dancer before a performance): “Don’t you move a muscle. After I win, you’ve got a lot of answering to do. But, I swear to god, you all better wait till I’m back before asking him a single question. This is my time, and I’ve been dying to perform.” She finally refuses to be reactionary. She takes the stage.

What it’s doing. It exposes Kage’s successor secret, shows his worst side, and hands the section to Ailani.

What works. Kage’s “conditional love” speech is the most honest thing he says in Section 4, and it ties his ambition back to his orphanhood. The successor slip is a great cliffhanger. Ailani’s exit line is perfect.

What I’d push on. Kage’s cruelty to Rebento is strong and true, but it slides past quickly. Since Rebento was right about him, Kage’s mocking is a guilty boy lashing out. A flicker of that guilt (“he was lying to a room of people who just agreed he was trustworthy” is in 4.3, but not here) would connect the two scenes.

Score 8/10

4.7 Saw Right Through You (new in Draft 3)

1,743 words. Arena: Ailani vs. Lance. The current final chapter.

What happens. Before the bell, Lance fidgets with his hilt, “counting my blessings.” Ailani asks if Kage acted strange last night; Lance says he’s no stranger than any of them. She marvels that Kage made a friend overnight, “one he actually calls a friend,” and jokes that they should show off so Kage picks one of them over Orion. Lance apologizes for his Rebento jab; she tells him to make it up to her by losing. Gene skips the countdown: “Screw the count! Just go!” What follows is the book’s first fight with no talking at all. Lance’s blades saw through Ailani’s crystals, but he has to hold the blade against each one, like a cast saw, so she overwhelms him with swarms of small crystals: “Two counts to her one, and Ailani had been counting beats her whole life.” He switches weapons constantly (cruciform sword, dagger, gladius, claymore, wakizashi, cutlass), feints with a thrown blade, and bats crystals back like baseballs. Her fingertips begin to crystallize. She uses her own half-crystal hand to block his blade and punches him with it. His eyes glow yellow. He throws his hilt, uses it as a baton, loses it to her pocket, and draws a spare. Then his eyes go fully yellow: his limit, which he calls “King Arthur,” a berserker state that blinds him with bloodlust. He reveals he can form blades without holding the hilt, blasting the pocketed hilt into the air with a giant odachi, and slams her toward the edge. She catches herself on a crystal platform and floats it back. With one construct left and her hands nearly locked, she makes a massive war hammer and smashes the floor in front of him. His foot slips into the crater, touching under the arena floor. Out of bounds, the Geneki rule. Lance snaps back to himself, laughing and apologizing. Ailani rewatched her friend’s loss until she knew it by heart. The second-half final will be Orion vs. Ailani.

Details I caught.

  • “Lance kept turning his hilt in his hand.” Ailani: “You’re fidgeting with that a lot. You nervous?” Lance: “Nah, nah. Just glad I made it this far. I’m counting my blessings.” He’s nervous about what happens if the fight goes long. We find out why.
  • “Can you tell me how yours and Kage’s talk went last night? Did he act strange in any way?” Ailani is still investigating, even now.
  • Lance: “He is a strange person, but I wouldn’t say any stranger than most of us.” Given what’s about to happen to Lance, that’s very true.
  • Ailani: “Sorry, I just can’t believe he was able to make a friend overnight. One he actually calls a friend! I don’t know if you realize how high of an honor that is.” In Kage’s language, “friend” is rarer than “not friend.”
  • Lance: “Oh, well, I feel super special,” he joked. “You seem like his closest friend. I bet that’s why Kage couldn’t pick between us.”
  • Ailani: “Duh! We’re his best friends. And yet, he still chose Orion. His ‘not friend.’ We should show off so he picks one of us instead.” She’s joking. She also just named the real bond in the book.
  • Lance: “sorry about the Rebento thing earlier. That was a low blow, and you know I’m against those.” He means his comment that she didn’t defend Kage. He uses the tournament’s “low blow” vocabulary on himself. That’s Lance: he holds himself to his own rules.
  • Ailani: “It was. You can make it up to me by losing.” A great line. She’s funny, confident, and setting terms.
  • Gene: “Screw the count! Just go!” After a whole book of “Three…Two…One,” Gene skips it. It’s a joke about pacing, and it launches the fight instantly.
  • The first exchange: a crystal, a cruciform sword that “slammed against the crystal before sawing through it.”
  • “She put a crystal wall up. Lance jumped, planted his blade on the top of it, and sawed down as he dropped, splitting the wall in half.” A cinematic image.
  • He switches to a dagger in a reverse grip “to strike faster than Ailani anticipated.” “For every dodge Ailani managed, she took two hits.”
  • “Her sigil glowed brighter as she formed a large crystal in front of Lance and launched him back.”
  • “Ailani and Lance didn’t bask. There weren’t even any words exchanged.” Every other fight in the book is full of banter. This one is silent. It’s a dance. And it’s the first fight in the book that belongs to a dancer.
  • “Once neutral was reestablished, they just both prepared to win the next exchange.” “Neutral” is a fighting-game term (the state where neither player has an advantage). It’s a subtle sign of a writer who knows fighting games.
  • A gladius now. “She tried to overwhelm Lance with a swarm of smaller crystals and noticed something. Lance couldn’t slice through them. His swings only hit, and he had to hold the blade against a crystal to saw through it.”
  • “That meant he couldn’t instantly cut her crystals, since his blade was more like a cast saw.” This is a brilliant refinement. A medical cast saw doesn’t spin; it vibrates. It cuts rigid plaster but won’t cut skin, because skin moves with the blade. That’s exactly the rule on Lance’s card (“flesh doesn’t get cut! It moves with the blade”). Whether you knew that about cast saws or not, the analogy is perfect, and it explains why his blades have to dwell on rigid things. It also matches 3.10, where his sword “slowly sawed through the arena floor.” (Lance’s card did say his blade cuts rigid things “like it isn’t even there,” which slightly overstates it. This chapter is the more precise version.)
  • “So, she kept up the numbers. Hit, then saw. Two counts to her one, and Ailani had been counting beats her whole life.” Her dancing (4.1, 3.3, 2.3) becomes her combat edge. That’s the best fusion of character and power in the section.
  • Lance lets crystals slip past; “he was forced to turn his gladius into a large claymore for more reach.” A claymore: the sword on Lucifer’s wall.
  • “He didn’t bother trying to cut through the incoming crystals. Instead, he set his stance and batted them right back at Ailani like baseballs.” Baseballs again.
  • “She blocked them with a wall again, but that made it so she couldn’t see Lance’s approach.” Her defense blinds her. Later, his offense blinds him.
  • “When Ailani dropped the wall, she saw a wakizashi in front of her. She blocked it, and realized there was no Lance holding it.” He threw his sword as a feint and kicked her from behind. That’s a trick his villain father might have taught: misdirection.
  • He picks up the hilt; it becomes a cutlass.
  • “Lance’s eyes glowed yellow as he struck again and again. Ailani noticed. Was that a tell, like the one Lance had pointed out in Rebento’s eyes?” Yellow, glowing eyes: Abby’s eyes glowed “a deep yellow” before Divine Judgement. In this book, glowing yellow eyes mean someone is about to stop being themselves.
  • “She noticed her fingertips were beginning to crystallize.” Her card’s cost, arriving on schedule.
  • “His blade needed time to saw through anything, so she put her half-crystallized hand in front of it.” She uses her own cost as armor, betting he can’t saw through before she moves. It’s brave and risky. Lance’s blade spares flesh but cuts rigid things, and her hand is now half rigid. She’s betting her hand.
  • “she kicked Lance away and got a few punches on his face with her hardened hands.” Crystal knuckles.
  • “Lance’s eyes glowed more, and they looked more battle-hungry than before. To Ailani’s confusion, Lance’s hilt had no sword formed out of it, even though his hands looked fine.” That’s the “limit” from his card: “a point where he decides that he needs to stop even when his hilt and hands are fine.” He’s trying to stop himself.
  • “Words remained a luxury neither of them wanted to spend.”
  • “Lance threw his hilt and hit Ailani in the forehead. He started to use the hilt as more of a blunt weapon in itself, picking it up and hitting Ailani with it like it was a police baton.” A police baton, in the hands of a villain’s son.
  • “Ailani caught the hilt during one of Lance’s advances before launching a crystal straight into his stomach. Lance felt like he’d coughed up a lung.”
  • “Ailani slid the hilt upside down into her pocket to force Lance to fight without it.” Smart.
  • “Lance pulled a spare hilt out of his pocket, one Rhatella had made him.” Faisceau starts with F. Rhatella’s alphabet holds. It forms a cinquedea, an Italian Renaissance short sword. “This one was smaller… but it still had the same sawing power.”
  • “The crystallization was crawling to her palms now.”
  • King Arthur: “Lance’s eyes were now completely yellow. The limit he’d been avoiding was something he called King Arthur. It was what he’d meant when he told everyone that sometimes, when fighting, you end up as someone else.” That’s his line from 4.3, explained. “When it took hold, he became so fight-starved that he was literally blinded by his desire for combat. He lost his sight, but his other senses sharpened. The problem was, his way of fighting got significantly less refined. He grew more anxious to strike first instead of planning.”
    • Why “King Arthur”? Lance is a Lancelot figure (his power is “Avalon’s Legacy”; he knights Volkan). Lancelot’s tragedy was that his greatest loyalty (to Arthur) and his greatest desire (for Guinevere) destroyed each other. Naming his berserker state after the king suggests the thing Lance fears most is the part of himself that wants to rule the fight. Cheyenne joked that if Lance can knight people, that makes him a king. Turns out he has one inside him.
    • It also makes three boys with a “someone else”: Lucifer and Abaddon, Kage and the biomass, Lance and King Arthur. Lucifer’s other self is a demon. Kage’s is a villain’s body. Lance’s is a legendary king. And all three are named for a kind of ruler: an angel of the abyss, a would-be “King Kage,” and King Arthur.
    • The blindness matters too. Lance, who sees good in everyone and holds them to honor, goes literally blind when the bloodlust takes over. He can’t see whom he’s hurting.
    • And it reframes his whole ethic. Lance “tr[ies] to hold people to the standard [he] didn’t hold to [him]self.” He polices honor because he knows how fast he can lose it.
  • The hidden trick: “Everyone figured his blades could only form in his hand, but he didn’t need to be holding the hilts to form them at all.” The pocketed hilt erupts into “an oversized ceremonial odachi” (a Japanese great sword), “and the sheer size of it sent the hilt skyrocketing out of her pocket.” “just as her fingers closed in, the blade disappeared at Lance’s command.” He throws the small hilt at her arm, catches the big one, and slams her with a falchion (a one-edged medieval sword).
  • Count the blades: cruciform sword, dagger, gladius, claymore, wakizashi, cutlass, cinquedea, odachi, falchion, and finally a basket-hilted broadsword. Ten blades in one fight, from five or six sword traditions. It’s a fun detail for weapon nerds, and it makes his power visual in a way no other fight does.
  • “She was flung towards the out of bounds, and only caught herself by forming a crystal floor just above the grass and hitting it hard. Her sigil turned ninety degrees as she floated the floor back to the arena.” The rotating sigil from 2.3, 3,000 lines later. It’s her telekinesis tell, and it’s used exactly as established.
  • “She could only make one more construct before her enhancement became useless.”
  • “She was perceptive, though. When she looked at Lance’s face, she could see that he wanted to end the fight now. He’d fully fallen into King Arthur’s grasp.” She reads him. He can’t see her.
  • “Lance made a basket-hilted broadsword as he ran at her to launch her out.”
  • The war hammer: “Ailani spent her last construct on a massive crystal hammer and brought it down hard.” Her card promised weapons “as massive as a war hammer.” “Lance heard it coming and stepped back, sure he’d dodged it.” He’s blind, so he listens. “Ailani wasn’t aiming for Lance, though. She smashed the arena floor right in front of him instead, and it broke apart all the way to his feet. Lance’s foot slipped down into the crater.”
  • “IT’S OVER!” “YOUR WINNER IS AILANI SHOMA!” “The crowd cheered tentatively, unsure how exactly she won.”
  • “Woah, that’s crazy smart!” Reina is the first to understand. Reina, who everyone treats as the cute, simple one, catches the rule before anyone else. That’s a nice beat for a character who just found out she’s been kept out of the loop.
  • Gene: “As established by my wonderfully splendid daughter Geneki’s failure, if you’re touching under the arena floor, that’s out of bounds!” Geneki’s loss in 3.2/3.3 becomes the winning move.
  • “When the match was deemed over, it was like Lance snapped back to reality. His eyes went back to normal.” “Oh, man! I lost! That was awesome, Ailani! Sorry if I got a little carried away there at the end.” “Carried away” is a gentle phrase for going blind with bloodlust. Lance laughs it off, the way Kage says he “kinda blacked out.”
  • Ailani, “her crystal hands still locked stiff at her sides.” She’s going into her next match with her hands locked. That’s a problem.
  • “When Geneki went out that way, I clocked it as a path to win. Gen’s my friend, after all, so I watched that replay way more times than she’d like. I’m just surprised no one else was paying attention to that.” Ailani’s friendship with the girl everyone fears is what wins her the fight. Kindness became strategy.
  • “Kage cheered as it all sank in, but his joy slowed when he turned to Orion, who seemed unhappy with the result.” “It’s easier to fight people you don’t know that well, but Orion didn’t have a choice in the matter.” Kage thinks Orion is upset about fighting a friend. He doesn’t know Orion likes her.
  • The last line: “The second-half final was going to be Orion Vance versus Ailani Shoma.”
  • The title has three meanings at least. Saw, the blade (Lance’s blades saw). Saw, the past tense of see (Ailani sees through Lance’s tells and his plan; Lance goes blind). Through, as in sawing through crystal and seeing through someone. And underneath, “saw right through you” is what Elias did to Kage in 3.12, and what Ailani has been trying to do all section.

What it’s doing. It gives Ailani the fight her section promised, reveals Lance’s limit, and sets up the love-triangle collision on the biggest stage in the book.

What works.

  • It’s the best-written fight in Section 4, and maybe the most elegant fight in the book. The decision to make it wordless is exactly right for a dancer, and it’s a relief after so many talky fights.
  • Character becomes tactics, twice. Ailani’s rhythm beats Lance’s saw. Ailani’s friendship with Geneki wins the match. Neither is a power-up. Both are who she is.
  • Every rule is used and nothing is cheated. The 25-meter range isn’t tested, but the 10–15-second construct limit is implied, the creeping crystal arrives on schedule, the war hammer is delivered as promised, the rotating sigil floats the platform, and the Geneki rule ends it. Lance’s blades cut rigid things only (the cast saw), his hilts are Rhatella-made (F before O), and his limit is the one his card mentioned.
  • King Arthur is a great reveal. It deepens Lance, rhymes with Kage and Lucifer, and gives the book’s most principled character a monster.
  • The aftermath is perfectly brief. Lance’s “carried away,” Ailani’s locked hands, Reina’s “crazy smart,” Orion’s face. And it ends on a single, loaded sentence.
  • It’s clean. No dialogue-tag punctuation errors, no tense slips. It’s the most polished chapter in the manuscript.

What I’d push on.

  • Ailani’s interiority. This is her section, and this is her first fight, but we’re almost entirely outside her head. One or two lines of what she feels (fear when the crystal reaches her palms, exhilaration at “counting beats,” what performing means to her) would make it her chapter in the way 2.6 was Abby’s. Even one sentence about her family, the people she wants to be “dependable” for, would raise the stakes.
  • The “half-crystal hand as a shield” moment is clever but risky in a way the text doesn’t acknowledge. A crystal hand is rigid, and Lance’s blade cuts rigid things. She’s gambling that she can kick him away before the blade saws into her hand. One clause (“before it could bite through her fingers”) would make that bravery explicit.
  • King Arthur’s blindness is a big new rule, and it raises questions. Can he turn it off? Is it his power or his psychology? Is it why his blades stop forming when he tries to hold back? A line or two in the aftermath, Lance admitting it to Cheyenne, would ground it.
  • Orion’s reaction is told through Kage’s misreading. That’s a smart choice, but readers might want one sentence from inside Orion before the section moves on, since we haven’t been in his head since the Rebento fight.

Score 9/10

Section 4 so far

Section 4 is named for Ailani, and it’s about someone who knew Kage before he changed, trying to see whether he’s still himself. It opens by showing us what the whole world was watching (Chapter NULL), then who Kage and Ailani were before any of this (Honey Toast). Then it puts Kage on trial in front of his class, exposes his successor secret, and finally gives Ailani a fight of her own. The new Chapter 7 is the section’s best chapter, and it moves Ailani from reacting to acting. The next chapter will put her against Orion, the boy who likes her and is hiding the worst choice of his life, for the right to face Kage, the boy she loved and is hiding a villain’s body.

Section score so far: 8.2/10.

5. Character Dossiers

Each dossier covers: what the book tells us, the power and its rules, the arc so far, the key lines, the relationships, what works, what I’d watch, and where I think the character is headed.

The Four Leads

Kage Ozul (“Sticky Situation,” “King,” “Prince,” “Speed-Limiter,” “Ozul,” “red one,” “ant”)

At a glance. A second-year from Japan. Orphaned young, raised in foster homes, came to the American program for “a bed that didn’t move every month.” Arctic blue eyes, scarlet hair (lazy and face-covering until 2.10, then a self-cut, floating, “cartoonish” style with eyeliner). Lean, “not bulky.” Seven inches shorter than Orion. Missing a tooth since 3.15. Worked at a maid café in first year (cat ears, maid outfit, “different-colored costumes,” good tips). Knows Inazuma from middle school. Eats cheese for fuel.

Power: Adhesive Manipulation. He creates and controls “any and all forms of adhesives,” excreted through his fingernails and toenails, fueled by lipids. No food, no power. Hardening requires cold drawn from his own body, so overuse causes fever (102°, 105°, then 110°F). Techniques we’ve seen: double-sided tape strips (the “flat peace sign”), glue sprays, toenail jets for propulsion and flight, hardened anchors, cement (from 1.6), concrete gauntlets, bouncy rubberized glue, key casts, glue “mines” compressed until they explode, confetti tape, a heat-resistant “fire-stop caulk” designed for Lucifer, and denser lipid-heavy tape. With certainty, he has telekinesis over his own adhesives (his hair stands on end). After 2.7, he regenerates through Thanatos’s biomass, which costs energy and can’t replace lost parts.

The arc so far, in six stages.

  1. The ghost (Prologue). Lazy on purpose. “Staying small and safe.” “Didn’t unpack.”
  2. The penitent (1.1–1.11). After the store, he trains until his hands bleed, chasing a word (“brilliant”) and a list of people to beat.
  3. The recruit (1.12–1.15). Seduced by Thanatos because a villain told him his chaos was a gift. Spies on his idol. Wins his first fight against Orion with a feint built on real tears.
